# Meraki progress

- Polished the Windows launcher UI while preserving package discovery, Inspect, Check imports, WARP launch, opt-in diagnostic frames, live logs and graceful Stop. The package card now shows the full executable path, package folder, manifest state and archive badges. The status band tracks the active action, session clock and latest runtime frame/FPS line. The diagnostics column shows the runtime build, session log and capture folder with open buttons, and live output highlights errors, warnings and progress. Added 11 launcher unit tests and 6 withdrawn-root Tk UI tests; all 113 tests pass. No live game run was performed.
- Evaluated native C++ Win32 against Tk for the launcher. Meraki retains Tk: no MSVC toolchain is present in the workspace, and a native rewrite would duplicate the tested process, log and Stop paths without an equivalent UI verification harness. Tk keeps feature parity and remains covered by the Python regression suite plus real widget construction tests under .local.

- Completed the [upstream comparison](docs/upstream-comparison.md) for XCL `e50836b`, VIctory `d2a4bae`, and the local read-only Leonida checkout `aa2988c`. XCL built in debug and listed 745 test entries. XCL game execution and runtime integration remain unverified. The first controlled experiment compares cold and warm shader and pipeline caches on the same package and scene.

### Live gameplay acceptance

- The isolated Rust capture candidate copies timed readback data into owned memory and encodes it on a bounded worker queue. The diagnostics build and 67 GPU tests passed, with one ignored. A short RX 7800 XT startup run saved five timed frames with a 2.533-second median and 4.300-second maximum gap and exited with code 0. A matched test is still needed to quantify render-thread overhead. Queued frames may be lost at process exit.
- The RenderDoc and `rdc-cli` synthetic Direct3D 12 evaluation is complete. A 65-check descriptor-retirement probe resolved four per-draw SRVs and matched render-target exports to snapshots. See [RenderDoc descriptor test](docs/renderdoc-descriptor-test.md). Timed runtime capture remains the live screenshot path.

- The 60 FPS study found 111 of 111 title interval requests at 33.333 ms across 23 logs. VIctory passes the requested interval through. In sampled hardware mission-transition frames, title-memory copies and copy-back dominated frame time. The synthetic timing model does not establish how the title's simulation would respond to a faster clock. Keep native pacing while validating the first mission at 15â€“30 FPS.

- A direct hardware capture smoke test saved five startup images at a 2.506-second median gap. The final image showed the account-picker prompts over a dark menu background. The run ended with exit code 0 after a Windows system-close request. This short run did not advance into the mission. Local evidence: `.local/sol-capture-20261124-112128`.

- Hardware is now the default in the launcher and live-test briefs. WARP remains an explicit diagnostic option. The launcher suite passed all 114 tests. The live tester will use the title's native pacing and prioritize working first-mission graphics and controls.

- The isolated timed-capture candidate built in diagnostics and release. GPU diagnostics tests passed (59 passed, one ignored). With `VICTORY_CAPTURE_TIMED=1`, it captures the first eligible rendered frame at or after each 2.5-second interval, records the actual gap, and caps saved frames. The live tester has the binary. A live WARP run saved 120 timed images with a 2.57-second median and 3.27-second maximum gap. Capture overhead remains unverified.

- Launcher capture now records per-frame host Present events alongside screenshots for the frame-pacing tool. The full 113-test suite passed after the change. Following the unrelated PC restart, no game process or completed new hardware result was found.

- Keep one visible game instance and one desktop test owner. Prepare the next candidate while the current run is active. Start the next comparison after verifying that the prior process exited. Let loading and shader compilation continue while logs or frames show progress.
- Capture the mission card, first rendered world frame or sustained black screen, and any failure. Keep timing logs running between captures. For each retry, record the build, cache state, scene, and single change under test. Confirm camera and movement before using a run to assess the 30 FPS target.

- The Navigation Experience input audit found that the prior 80 ms Continue presses were shorter than the roughly 100 ms setup frames, with foreground focus unverified for those presses. Later runs advanced the same screen to UI Text Size. Live tests now verify focus, hold Continue for 400â€“500 ms, and confirm the expected screen after release.

- The visible DeepSeek WARP test advanced from the account picker to Navigation Experience after focused Right and Enter input. Two focused Continue attempts left that screen unchanged. The account picker averaged 14.86 FPS and setup averaged 10.28 FPS. The worker requested Windows system close and verified that the process exited. This second run took 7 minutes 12 seconds, exceeding the five-minute test cap. Player-controlled gameplay and the 60 FPS hardware target remain unverified. Local report: `.local/live-gameplay/REPORT-20261123.md`.
- Across seven local logs, all 28 frame-interval requests from the title were 33.333 ms during startup and onboarding. VIctory honors the requested interval and calls Direct3D Present with sync interval zero. The measured menu cadence is therefore separate from host vsync.
- In those WARP sessions, GPU wait accounted for 72â€“87% of median frame time at 4K. This identifies a menu bottleneck. First-mission GPU cost remains unverified.
- A device-only D3D12 probe confirmed that the RX 7800 XT and WARP pass VIctory's shader model 6.6 and mesh shader checks. Both reported shader model 6.8 and mesh shader Tier 1. The pinned runtime sizes presentation from the title texture and has no independent resolution override. See the [hardware test plan](docs/hardware-gameplay-test.md).
- The frame-pacing tool counts logged host present calls, including repeated title tokens. It does not infer presentations from token gaps. All 96 tests passed. Live gameplay measurement is pending.
- A controlled 60 Hz versus 120 Hz advertised-mode comparison can test whether display modes influence the menu's requested interval. An unchanged request would leave other title settings and gameplay modes untested.

- Run interactive gameplay tests with the game window visible on the Windows desktop. Announce each live run before launch, share milestones during the run, and close the game afterward. Use the launcher to keep the live log available.
- The first performance target is 15â€“30 FPS on hardware during player-controlled gameplay. Verify camera and movement in a visibly rendered scene before measuring. Record the renderer, resolution, duration, and frame pacing. Keep the title's native frame clock.
- During the first visible mission, record an idle baseline, rotate the camera in both directions, then move forward and backward against a fixed landmark. Capture the matching frames and use the black-frame probe history to distinguish a fade from sustained dark output. Confirm keyboard focus before sending input.
- Launcher Stop now requests VIctory's Windows system-close path. A visible WARP smoke test presented frames and reached the runtime suspend handler. VIctory hit its one-second suspension deadline and exited with code 0. Pipeline cache persistence remains unverified.

DeepSeek implemented 11 launcher regression tests covering runtime routing, archive headers, and opt-in captures. All passed with `python -B -m unittest discover -s tests -v`.

The rendering-log audit reproduced misleading totals from truncated, missing, and repeated frame records. The parser now reports interval coverage and distinguishes draw counter differences from dropped-pipeline diagnostics. All 48 tests passed. The local boot log still reports complete coverage through frame 1024 with 16,430 draws and 957 dispatches executed. A separate worker is preparing the first-mission input and capture test.

## November 23, 2026

- Integrated VIctory at d2a4bae1f49533ef5cb512fd1b1ba1513f2e1989 as a submodule. Build.ps1 builds from the Meraki root.
- Implemented a Tk launcher with package discovery, executable inspection, WARP execution, process control and local session logs.
- Verified the Inspect action using package 1.0.2.1 on D:\. The three principal archives have the RPF9 on-disk signature documented by Leonida. This header check covers format identification only.
- Tested preparation of game_scarlett_master.exe on Windows 11 25H2 (build 26200). VIctory resolved 88 emulated platform imports, 427 host imports and 72 imports from the title's DLLs. All four title DLLs also loaded with zero missing imports.
- The WARP diagnostic run opened the account picker, accepted Enter and rendered the animated opening sequence. Local screenshots and runtime traces are under .local/smoke.
- Frame 900 showed the legal notices screen. The loader had already opened levels.rpf during startup, so opening that archive is insufficient evidence that the world loaded.
- Verified the launcher's Check imports action end to end against the local package. It completed with exit code 0 and reported the bindings for each module.
- The first-run flow reached Rockstar Games Legal Terms & Policies. Terms of Service was unchecked and Confirm was disabled. Testing stopped there for the user to review the terms. Locally verified milestones currently end at onboarding.

### World rendering investigation

- A RenderDoc descriptor-retirement synthetic probe passed on the default D3D12 adapter. One captured 192x144 frame contained four draws from a two-slot shader-visible SRV heap: red/green were drawn, the descriptors were retired after a fence wait, and blue/yellow were drawn from the remade slots. `rdc descriptors` and an independent RenderDoc API read agreed on four distinct SRVs (286-289); all four exported targets matched their exact expected pixels; and all four snapshots matched the RT exports byte-for-byte (65 checks). Evidence: `.local/renderdoc-descriptors/REPORT.md`; repo summary: [docs/renderdoc-descriptor-test.md](docs/renderdoc-descriptor-test.md).

- The descriptor retirement follow-up reproduced a premature release when a completed command list is replayed without reset. The revised patch keeps lists armed until reset or drop, and skips retirement after a failed fence wait. Game-free diagnostics tests passed (62 passed, one ignored). A full diagnostics executable has been built; its live RX 7800 XT mission test remains pending.

- The first descriptor bounds candidate built and passed 468 workspace tests with seven ignored. Review found a live-test blocker: its minimum clamp can send an invalid index near the limit to the wrong typed-null slot. A follow-up is implementing exact boundary selection and checking the generated DXIL before a hardware run.

- The retirement review reproduced unbounded retention from an idle command list and found that failed fence waits must block reclamation. Its revised model passed 56 GPU tests with one ignored. A separate build is checking replayed command lists before a live run, since a completed submission can still leave a list eligible for replay.

- A game-free D3D12 regression confirmed that replacing a descriptor slot in `ffe310e` can drop its previous resource reference while a list is live. A retirement patch passed the regression and 53 GPU tests, with one ignored. The patch retains the old allocation but leaves the slot pointing to the new resource. Review is underway for retention growth with long-lived lists before a live hardware run. Neither hardware crash has been tied to this exact code path.

- The fxceless source audit found that emitted view handles can omit a bounds check when the full heap is available, and sampler bounds can exceed the actual heap. The DRED address is consistent with an out-of-range descriptor index, although the triggering instruction is not identified. A defensive candidate is assigned to use live heap limits and preserve valid accesses, with CPU and shader IR tests.

- The fxceless `578d459` hardware run rendered detailed intro cards and reached mission entry with audio. Credits overlapped and froze, then the mission went black. Menus and credits had a 33.3 ms median presentation interval; mission entry measured 0.95 presents per second. The device was removed after an eight-list submission. DRED reported a fault address far beyond the view and sampler heap ranges. That calculation does not prove which descriptor caused the fault. The process exited, and no retry was made. Evidence: `.local/fxceless-live/session-20261124-113816/REPORT-20261124-live.md`.

- The compute lowerer investigation isolated all 253 previously emitted programs lost at `ffe310e` to `3dd467c`, which keeps values live through successive control-flow joins. The old output substituted zero where the title needed a descriptor word. Keep the liveness fix. Correct emission requires support for descriptor contents used as data. The synthetic probe recovered the counts by reverting liveness only as an experiment.

- The fxceless fork at `578d459` built in diagnostics and passed 116 GPU tests and 184 shader tests, with three ignored. A visible RX 7800 XT run is assigned with native pacing and default runtime flags.
- DRED matched both hardware fault addresses to recently freed textures, but the report does not expose their allocation base, sizes, or access type. The strongest lead is a descriptor slot being remade while an open submission still references its previous texture. The proposed regression records a table use, remakes the slot, and verifies the previous binding remains valid until the list retires.

- A user-supplied screenshot from the [VIctory livestream](https://rumble.com/v7iuddk-gta-6-time.html) shows a lit dialogue scene with visible character and environment geometry beyond Meraki's black-frame checkpoint. Stream playback could not be verified here. The build, settings, and timestamp are unknown. Public VIctory main matched Meraki's `ffe310e` pin when checked. Use the image as a visual checkpoint for the fork comparison.

- Added [fxceless/victory-gta6](https://github.com/fxceless/victory-gta6) as an isolated comparison candidate. The fork reports prologue geometry, HUD, and pad input at 2â€“3.5 FPS on an RTX 4070, with missing lighting in shadow. Its meshlet buffer views, descriptor handling, and texture synchronization changes are relevant to the local black scene. DeepSeek is building and reviewing the exact revision before a local hardware test.

- The updated VIctory `ffe310e` diagnostic build reached the opening credits on the RX 7800 XT. The first 60 seconds averaged 28.94 presents per second. Credits averaged 0.45, and the run ended after token 3540 with `0x887A0006` and a DRED page fault. No mission geometry or player control was verified. This was a cold-cache run with a changed driver and concurrent offline translation. Evidence: `.local/upstream-hardware/REPORT.md`.

- On the same shader archive, offline DXIL emissions fell from 22,156 at `d2a4bae` to 21,886 at `ffe310e`. Compute fell from 1,693 to 1,441. Resource tracking reached 234 more programs, while more programs then failed emission. The new test executable matches the freshly built diagnostics binary by SHA-256. This census omitted DXIL validation and pipeline creation. DeepSeek is investigating which previously emitted shaders were lost.

- The older PR #32 hardware probe lost the RX 7800 XT with `0x887A0006` during a staging-texture upload after token 1708. DRED recorded a page fault and breadcrumbs stopped at command 14 of 1,319. No Windows display-reset event or dump was found. Menus and intro averaged 26.55 presents per second in the first minute, with 33.84 ms p95. No playable world appeared. The driver changed after the reboot, and a bounded run on `ffe310e` is assigned. This comparison changes both runtime and driver.

- Sol's source review identified four changes to watch in the next mission trace: `3c81420` drops work when fresh descriptor tables cannot be allocated, `e8d3b64` increases the dynamic descriptor range where supported, `a4e7f1d` initializes pending descriptors before publishing the heap, and `cbb50b8` corrects the dimension of null UAVs. Check whether descriptor exhaustion still precedes device removal and capture the first in-world frame.

- Updated the VIctory pin from `d2a4bae` to `ffe310e`. Both runtime profiles built and all 113 Meraki tests passed. The fetched README reports 30 FPS in menus and a dark scene after the first-mission title card. The update includes descriptor heap replay, CPU-written texture upload fixes, and per-lane descriptor shader lowering. Offline shader coverage comparison is underway. Updated mission visuals and frame rate still need local verification.

- Live WARP results: the prewarmed PR #254 run reported 26 hits and two misses and still lost the device at mission entry. The PR #32 timing probe attributed about 96% of loading frame time to GPU wait. The removal probe first detected `0x887A0005` before submission at frame 1593, after the prior Present. This locates detection, while the triggering operation remains unknown. All test processes exited.
- With monitors off, desktop captures were stale, but VIctory captured frames and accepted focused input. The presentation resolution fell from 4K to 1080p, so those timing samples require a separate comparison. A bounded RX 7800 XT test is assigned to check mission entry on an adapter that supports the fixed compute wave sizes.

- Headless tests isolated the pre-removal WARP compute pipeline refusals to fixed wave-size requirements. WARP reports 4 lanes and rejects shaders requiring 32 or 64. The RX 7800 XT reports 32â€“64 lanes and accepted all 299 tested compute pipelines, including 118 with fixed wave sizes. WARP remains useful for diagnostics but cannot verify full mission rendering on this host. Device removal is a separate unresolved failure.

- The isolated PR #32 removal probe built and passed both focused tests. It records the first failed check before a submission or around Present, including the numeric HRESULT, frame, and submission. It is queued for a separate WARP run if device removal persists after the prewarmed-cache comparison.

- A headless C++ WARP harness passed four aligned texture-copy and readback cases in three consecutive runs, including a correctly sized mip-1 copy. Fences completed and all active bytes matched. The test covers the basic D3D12 transfer path. The mission failure still needs a trace that identifies the first device-removal result around Present and submission.

- Across six runs, the shader audit found 491 translation failures in 3,911 attempts, including 302 compute failures. A separate 352 pre-removal compute pipeline refusals returned `E_INVALIDARG` after translation had succeeded. The synthetic translator fixture passed four cases and does not reproduce host pipeline failures or device removal. A focused host pipeline investigation is assigned.

- VIctory PR #254 `0e6b2b4` built in diagnostics and passed 12 focused cache tests. Offline translation wrote 22,156 nonempty cache entries in about 287 seconds. The prewarmed candidate is queued for a visible WARP comparison after the frame-time probe exits.

- The opt-in PR #32 frame-time probe built and passed 55 focused GPU tests with one ignored. It separates preparation, GPU wait, copy-back, and residual time. A visible WARP run was assigned to validate the probe at mission entry. Existing mission-loading samples measured 1.29â€“3.06 presents per second and still ended in device removal. These samples do not measure gameplay.

- Four DeepSeek workstreams are assigned: diagnose the last texture-copy submission, build the shader-cache candidate, rank mission shader failures, and prepare performance instrumentation or a focused optimization. Compare one runtime change at a time. After the mission renders, test player control and fix visible rendering defects before measuring sustained 30 FPS.

- The warm-cache WARP run reached Lucia's mission loading card and then went black. It logged descriptor exhaustion (104 slots requested), a 1,242 ms submission with 32 lists, and GPU device removal. The mission-loading window measured 3.25 observed presents per second with a 932.7 ms p95 interval. Player control was never visible. The next run compares the isolated queue-ordering candidate against this failure sequence. Local evidence: `.local/live-gameplay/session-20261124-0030-warm`.

- Public VIctory main matched Meraki's pin when checked on November 24. Open [VIctory PR #32](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/pull/32) implements cross-queue waits. Its exact head `8fd82c9` built in diagnostics with zero warnings. Focused GPU tests passed with 55 tests in the default configuration and 57 with diagnostics, with one ignored in each. The binary is available for a separate WARP test under `.local/victory-pr32/target/diag`. Open PRs #254 and #275 address shader-cache and pipeline compilation stalls and can be evaluated separately.
- A local diagnostic log recorded a 672 ms host submission followed by GPU device removal, along with shader translation and pipeline failures. The trace lacks queue-wait telemetry, so it cannot establish whether the ordering patch addresses that failure. Verify the scene and repeat the test with matched settings and separate cache roots.

- VIctory developers reported two separate failure modes at first-mission entry. Cold shader compilation can stall rendering long enough for the title to close. Once shaders are cached, a GPU ordering problem can still leave rendering passes reading data before it was written, producing a black screen or crash. Upstream reported a fix in progress. Both findings still need local reproduction against Meraki's pinned revision.
- Preserve the shader cache between matched runs. Compare cold and warm runs using separate workspace cache directories, and correlate compile activity with presentation gaps and exit timing. If the warm run remains dark, capture the affected frames and examine GPU submission ordering and missing writes alongside pipeline counters. Compare any upstream fix against the current pin on the same build and scene.

A community report describes intro audio continuing over a black screen. VIctory's pinned README reports rendering the first mission's title card followed by a dark scene and identifies dropped draws from untranslated shaders. Compare a captured frame after mission entry with the same interval's draw, dispatch and missing-pipeline counters. In the local intro interval ending at frame 512, all 1,536 draws and 256 dispatches executed. This gives a working comparison point before world rendering. [Community report](https://www.reddit.com/r/FitGirlRepack/comments/1y3x934/rip_leonida/) and [VIctory runtime status](https://github.com/Marvisak/VIctory/blob/d2a4bae1f49533ef5cb512fd1b1ba1513f2e1989/README.md)

### Diagnostic capture and outstanding tasks

- Added opt-in capture to the launcher. A live WARP launch against the local title produced a PNG for frame 1 and a matching session log under .local/logs. Capture defaults to off.
- Host performance messages round missing-pipeline counts per frame and report only selected intervals. The separate queue counters recorded 13,194 issued and 13,194 executed draws from frames 513 through 1,024, along with 512 issued and executed dispatches.
- The offline shader pass on the local archive emitted DXIL for 12,272 of 14,001 vertex/pixel programs, 1,693 of 2,581 compute programs and 8,191 of 8,506 mesh programs that reached emission. The two most frequent reported blockers were 64-bit image atomics (653) and scalar memory reads as data (573). This pass measured emission only. A mission trace is needed to identify which failures affect the scene.
- Outstanding gameplay test: after the user completes the first-run terms screen, capture the transition into the first mission and verify visible world geometry and player-controlled movement. Compare the logged rendering counters at that transition with the working intro.
- Started a fresh WARP test with the launcher's persistent data folder and captured the first 128 presented frames. Session evidence is in `.local/gameplay-20261123-212748`. After onboarding, test camera input from a stationary position, then forward movement and release. Check the character's position against a fixed landmark before and after input so cutscene motion cannot be mistaken for player control.
- Tested menu input from Navigation Experience through UI Text Size, Finish Setup, copyright and photosensitivity notices. Reached the privacy and terms checkboxes with Confirm disabled. Closed the game and verified process exit. Both builds and the software-adapter probe passed independent validation.
- Integrated the delegated runtime log summary and verified identical frame interval totals on UTF-8 and UTF-16 copies of the local boot log. The report distinguishes rounded host samples from frame interval counters.

### Live WARP mission attempts (2026-11-24, test sub-agent)

- Baseline b99f876 warm-cache retry and PR32 17655DC5 both reached the mission-entry black scene and lost the device (0x887A0004/5) within seconds. Removals: baseline 07:36:48, PR32 07:46:26. PR32 cut pipeline refusals 107->21 and removed the n=104 descriptor exhaustion, but did not fix the removal.
- DRED on PR #32 listed buffer-to-texture copies in the last attempted submission. Earlier resource creation calls already returned device removed, following first-chance exceptions in WARP. This places the observed failure before the listed submission. The initiating operation remains unidentified. A headless WARP copy and readback test is assigned to validate the basic transfer path independently.
- No terms screen appeared and no terms were accepted. Player controls remain unverified because no visible playable frame was rendered. Report: .local/live-gameplay/REPORT-20261124.md

- Test sub-agent hardware arm (2026-11-24): single visible RX 7800 XT run on timed-capture ffe310e diag ran menus at a 33.3 ms median and reached the Vice City, Two Years Ago title card, then went black from frame 5780 with one flat-navy artifact frame and growing 3.5-5.4 s present gaps. ### fxceless refresh `4e17478` (2026-11-24, implementation sub-agent)

- Fetched current `fxceless/victory-gta6` main into `.local/fxceless-refresh`. Final head `4e17478b9c27bb530643e5b0e16a767cf4419e08` is 9 commits after the tested `578d459` (73 files, +5295/-262). The remote advanced once during the task from `90228a7` to `4e17478`; the extra commit changes comments and descriptions only. It built in diagnostics from `C:\Meraki` via `--manifest-path` with local Rust (1.98.1): `C:\Meraki\.local\fxceless-refresh-target\diag\victory.exe`, 8,710,656 bytes, SHA-256 `305C6F6428D112CAC2A3C6472C1D7CEBA0BDE9906BBFD33BCB0A71309AA599B9`. No game/desktop launch, baseline edit, or commit.
- Six focused shader tests passed: relative-VGPR access, both 16-bit wave-sum tree checks, lane-mask-as-number, SCC mask skip, and clean-up switch parsing. Three tests (16-bit tree, lane-mask-as-number, SCC skip) also ran headless compute dispatches on the host hardware device; the tree check returned `0x5914`, without a game window. The new head adds the parked-mask fix; its commit says this lets `CS_IrradianceCache_Shade`/`ShadeSecondary` translate. It also adds shadow/trace coverage, shader clean-up passes, configurable pipeline waits, and the wave-wide stability disablement.
- No semantic descriptor-safety or texture-lifetime change since `578d459`: `descriptor.rs`, `host.rs`, and `resource.rs` have comment-only changes; `descriptor/host.rs` and `capture.rs` add diagnostics only, while `texture.rs` has one integer-idiom edit. View clamping remains conditional on the capped-heap probe, and samplers still clamp to 2047 rather than the bound heap's count. The prior AMD DRED class (view and sampler addresses past heap end after an 8-list mission submission) is therefore not directly fixed.
- Hardware recommendation: one visible, bounded RX 7800 XT run on the new binary with fresh cache/data, DRED, capture, default switches, and no WARP. Use `W/A/S/D` to move selection, `Enter` to activate, verify game focus before each send, and stop after the first removal. The aim is to test the improved shade/trace coverage against the prior black mission and to see whether the descriptor/lifetime fault persists. Report: `.local/fxceless-refresh-REPORT.md`.

### fxceless-refresh `4e17478` warm-cache RX 7800 XT arm (2026-11-24, test sub-agent)

- One visible hardware run used the exact `4e17478` diag binary, SHA-256 `305C6F6428D112CAC2A3C6472C1D7CEBA0BDE9906BBFD33BCB0A71309AA599B9`, with the prior isolated fork data/cache. Native pacing, DRED and default flags; no WARP or experimental switches. Session: `.local/fxceless-refresh-hardware/session-20261124-144046-hw`. PID 19596 ran 14:40:46-15:07:23 local.
- After a focused `Enter` on the account picker, the intro rendered lit world geometry: Lucia, the sunset prison yard, structures, windows, NPCs, sunlight and reflections. It then reached third-person over-the-shoulder walking. The previous black-frame failure did not recur. World-view cadence was a median 1.8 fps / 556.1 ms/frame (150 samples; 0.6-2.8 fps, median 5,840 draws/frame). No HUD appeared and no player control was confirmed.
- A 2 s focused `W` press was delivered with all focus samples true, but cinematic camera motion could not be separated from player movement. The user separately observed the pause menu at 20 fps / 49.9 ms / 92 draws and supplied the screenshot saved under the session evidence.
- No device removal, root-signature refusal, host-pipeline refusal, replay warning, panic, out-of-memory marker, or WARP use occurred. Twelve shader translation warnings remain (16-bit wave sum, `v_permlanex16_b32`, descriptor-as-data, `v_cvt_f32_f64`, half interpolation).
- The passive 30 s PID sample reported zero/suspect per-PID GPU counters but a 42% in-process card clock and host-bound timing. Memory sampler peak was 24,623 MiB private / 17,270 MiB working set, with minimum 338 MiB free commit and 1,931 MiB free physical; the process stayed alive but headroom was tight. `WM_CLOSE` led to the runtime ending the title after its suspend timeout; no game process remains.
- Verdict: lit intro world rendering is a major advance, but this is not yet playable. Evidence: `.local/fxceless-refresh-hardware/session-20261124-144046-hw/evidence/REPORT.md` (contains summary, captures, telemetry, screenshots and the passive sampler).
- Host-side performance work is underway on the measured `gpu_idle_host_bound` path (42% median in-process card clock; 295 ms median idle behind the submitting thread). The next live test must confirm a HUD and an unambiguous input handoff before its cadence is treated as player-controlled gameplay FPS.

### fxceless-host-perf `4e17478` VICTORY_WIDE_REFRESH A/B (2026-11-24, test sub-agent)

- One matched visible RX 7800 XT pair on the unpatched diag binary `F664F166...48B3E25` (8,710,656 B), hardware D3D12, DRED, native pacing, no WARP, no other `VICTORY_*` switch, `VICTORY_WINDOW_KB` unset in both (grid 4,096 KiB), per-arm byte-identical warmed cache/data. Arm A unset; Arm B `VICTORY_WIDE_REFRESH=1`. Focused `Enter` on the account picker (5/5 focus samples) reached the same lit Lucia prison-window intro in both arms; no HUD and no player control. Post-Enter to the wide-shot landmark was 64.2 s (A) vs 66.9 s (B), so loading matched.
- Matched windows (settled 400-700 ms, >=4,000 draws; draw band pins the same visible geometry): landmark +60..200 s, A 475.80 ms / p95 538.24 vs B 461.40 / 518.72 (n=25/25); band 4,900-5,050 draws, A 476.00 / 615.40 vs B 460.80 / 574.87 (n=26/40); but landmark +200..330 s, A 465.95 / 491.64 vs B 472.90 / 563.98 (n=24/23) and band 4,750-4,900, A 463.25 / 507.87 vs B 472.90 / 568.70 (n=78/31). Copies MB unchanged (+0.6..0.9%); reported copies ms flat-to-higher; card busy +1-2 points on B; draws within 0.3%; dropped draws median 353 vs 360.
- Memory/commit matched: private median 22.58 vs 22.70 GB, peak 22.97 vs 23.29 GB; lowest free commit 597 MB (A) vs 400 MB (B, transient in load), both recovered, no OOM. Two gates stand: B needs `VICTORY_CARD_BUFFERS=0` or the hold is refused as `in the card's memory`, and B needs `VICTORY_BVH_RETAIN_DIAG=1` or a zero-read result is unattributable.
- Built `.local/bvh-reader-validation`: a parser for the exact `shadow.rs`/`host.rs`/`pipeline.rs` formats, 12 verdicts, and CP0-CP5 checkpoint records imported from the existing gameplay visual gate. `python dry_run.py` exits 0 with 26/26 verdict cases, the NUL-hash case, and the checkpoint-state check. It separates `CONFIRMED_RT_READ`, `READ_WITHOUT_RT`, `VALID_NEGATIVE`, `HOLD_RELEASED_BEFORE_READ`, `REFUSED_RETENTION`, `MISSING_EVIDENCE`, `BUILD_GATE_FAILED`, `QUARANTINE_OFF` and the arm-A cases, and treats a read logged up to 64 lines before its hold (`held.push` then `say_held`) as ambiguous, never a confirmation or a negative.

All 343 `.local` logs were scanned; none contains a real hold or reader line, so the grammar is source-derived and the first live B arm is the writer's first true-format test. `compare` records CP4 as `matched_dimensions` with framing/means pending, CP3 `unverified`, and CP2/CP5 one-arm-only; reference dims and SHA-256 for all six CPs are pinned in `checkpoints.manifest.json`.

Verify focus before every send; Enter activates, W/A/S/D move selection; hardware rendering by default. Read the parser verdict before any pixel or fps claim; REFUSED or MISSING is not a negative. ### marked-page copy-back offline performance verification (2026-11-24, perf sub-agent)

- Offline only: no title launch, desktop input, WARP, or subagent; writes only in `C:\Meraki`, with `C:\Leonida` and `D:\` untouched. Isolated worktree `C:\Meraki\.local\marked-page-perf\src`, branch `perf/marked-page-copyback` at base `940d32f69`. Only test instrumentation was added (`BENCH_NO_UNARM`, `armed_pages()`/`saved_pages()`, `shadow_perf.rs`); production code is byte-identical to upstream.
- Parity: 7 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored on the marked-page suite, re-confirmed on disk after the report rewrite; full `victory-gpu` diagnostics 153 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored (146 upstream + 7 new; 2 pre-existing ignored + benchmark). Covers sparse/dense model parity, title-write races, unmarked/unwithheld pages, repeated rounds, partial-tail `0x22` vs `0xDD` A/B, and armed-set cleanup.
- Game-free benchmark on a real write-watched 174 MiB host pool (2,784 pages, 26 overlapping ranges, 7 measured rounds, median): snapshot+full-compare baseline vs marked copy-back saved 35.3 ms at 0.1% sparse, 39.5 ms at 1% (27 pages), 49.2 ms at the observed 252 pages, 96.2 ms at 50%, and 152.9 ms at 100% (2.5x-25.5x for the measured copy work). Arm costs 1.4-1.7 ms; marked write-back is ~34 us/page (8.5 ms at 252, 102.6 ms at 2,784).
- `940d32f69` isolation: dense 100% `copy_back_marks` with `unarm` 96.844 ms vs without 114.303 ms, i.e. +17.459 ms / +15.3% (6.27 us/page) without it, from per-page `saved` read/boxed allocation. So the line is both a correctness and a performance fix.
- Refresh lower bound (`Shadow::update`, one full 174 MiB range): 0.000 ms at 0% changed, 0.235 ms at 1% (1.7 MiB), 2.075 ms at 10% (17.4 MiB), 34.274 ms at 100%. Live behavior is far worse per byte because it issues 23,143 ranges per frame.
- Budget: 33.333 ms/frame. Newer active `4e17478` world median is 468.0 ms (14.0x over, 92.9% must go); deleting all 96.8 ms copy-back still leaves 371.2 ms (11.1x), and a benchmark-shaped 49.2 ms saving leaves ~419 ms (12.6x). Older hardware-60/upstream logs are 55.4x/74.5x over, and even after the claimed 0.45-0.70 s snapshot saving remain 34.4x-41.9x / 53.5x-61.0x. The commit pair alone cannot reach 30 FPS.
- Next largest actionable bottleneck: the unchanged-page refresh/watch path of the 174 MiB heap (23,143 refresh ranges / 18.4 GB asked, 10.4 GB checked, 98.6% unchanged in the active log; 461.5 ms of watches over 12.5 GB, 97.5% unchanged in hardware-60). Then: O(1) title dirty bitmap instead of `BTreeSet` range scans, batching 12-13k tiny title copies/moves, and pooling `vec![0u8; n]` in `copy_back_marked`.
- Evidence: `C:\Meraki\.local\marked-page-perf\REPORT.md`, `bench-final.log`, `parity-final3.log`, `full-gpu-final.log`; tests at `C:\Meraki\.local\marked-page-perf\src\crates\victory-gpu\src\shadow_perf.rs`. No live claim or FPS claim was made.

### offline gameplay visual acceptance harness - built and demonstrated (2026-11-24, offline sub-agent)

- Scope: offline only. No game launch, no desktop input, no live test, no subagents. Writes stayed under `C:\Meraki`; `C:\Leonida` and `D:` were not touched. `C:\Meraki\.local\gameplay-visual-gate` is the harness; `python` was used explicitly.
- Built a dependency-free gate (stdlib + numpy) that reads the existing lit RX 7800 XT checkpoint captures and runtime logs. Commands: `python -B -m gvg report`, `session`, `check`, `classify`, `reference`, `controls`, `selftest`. `README.md` documents usage, thresholds, arm provenance and limits.
- Frame identity: classifier separates `world`, `world_dark`, `black`, `ui_on_black`, `menu`, `credits_card`, `credits_scroll` and `unknown`. Checkpoint checks cover matched-camera compare (area-averaged resample, so capture resolution does not dominate), geometry, materials, lighting and HUD. The CP2 HUD is pinned to the documented left-centre 27-34% band; a prompt moved out of that band is rejected.
- HUD stability was tightened after finding that `hud_watch` counters alone false-positive on intro art and on a white prompt fragment over a black frame. `session.hud_sequence()` now decodes and re-classifies every candidate; only real `world`/`world_dark` frames with the prompt box count. Result: the 19:05 baseline has 3 verified HUD frames (all world+HUD), while the paused a803c87 session has 0 (its three late hits are `ui_on_black`), matching the operator note.
- Shader-arm provenance: the log build id `b99f876...` is shared by every arm, so the gate resolves the arm from `meta.json` (`runtime`/`runtime_sha256`). `4e17478` is the pre-fix baseline (six fixed classes may reappear); `a803c87` is the candidate (they must stay removed). `--expect auto` is the default. A baseline session now reports `shader_quality PASS` for the baseline class set plus `candidate_shader WITHHELD`; it is not silently graded as a candidate pass or fail. An explicit override is labelled in the report.
- Evidence results (`evidence/report.json`/`report.md`): CP0 account picker, CP1 first-mission card, CP2 tutorial HUD, CP2 same camera without prompt, CP3 sunlit hair, CP4 doorway over-shoulder and CP5 night face all PASS. 24/24 threshold controls behave as expected (synthetic + derived image controls, and synthetic logs for healthy 60 FPS, missing RT pass, draw loss, shader reappearance, device removal, 60 FPS window and 30 FPS intervals).
- Reference session `tutorial-input-live/session-20261124-190543-hw` (baseline): 9/9 RT passes bound and 0 dropped, builder-off baseline, draws median 5,005.5 in band, lowest world sample 5,022, shader baseline class set matches, 3 verified HUD frames. Cadence FAIL (idle_before 3.06 s / 0 samples, move_forward 8.96 s / 2 samples, idle_after 1.04 s / 0 samples); idle scene static (ssim 0.986) but the move is a sub-pixel/camera-locked change (ssim 0.884, dx/dy 0,0), so the move result is WARN. Performance WITHHELD: only 33.333 ms intervals, observed 2.05 fps, p50 489 ms. Device BLOCKED: 4 removals, 2 page faults, 2 view-heap and 2 sampler-heap past-end faults, 1 host GPU failure.
- Reference refresh: `diff_reference` previously compared only classification kind and numeric stats, so a stale `CP2.hud_crop` HUD flag (`False` while the current detector says `True`) was reported as "reference unchanged". The diff now includes `hud_present`, HUD box/score/text-row fields and text-row counts. Refreshed `reference_stats.json`: CP2.hud_crop hud False -> True (score 0.674, box 0.03/0.15/0.325/0.375); text-row counts changed on CP2.hud/CP2.world/CP5; all eight cases still classify as expected and source hashes are unchanged.
- Tests: `tests/` has 40 unittest cases across PNG round-trip, classifier/HUD, reference integrity/hash/thumbnail/currentness, runtime.log passes/shader arms/device/performance, controls, cadence and arm provenance. `python -B -m gvg selftest`: 40/40 OK.
- Limitations: captures and the session log are from different runs, so a checkpoint image is not proof of that log; the 4e17478 baseline cannot exercise the candidate shader criterion (WITHHELD); no clean a803c87 candidate session exists; performance remains withheld until a same-scene idle -> W -> idle run has >=7.5 s and >=10 samples per window at 16.667 ms intervals.

### descriptor-data review - no 24-byte shader stride; 12-byte records verified (2026-11-24, source-only review)

- Scope: independent offline re-check of `.local/descriptor-data-prototype` against the reported "24-byte shader stride vs 12-byte host records" discrepancy. No game, live/visual test, desktop input, GPU presentation or subagent. `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` were not touched, and the prototype was not edited: `logs/tree-compare.txt` hashes all 169 files and finds only the two expected test additions (prototype `host.rs` `C5A491A6...722DBA2`, `lower.rs` `3EBD77EF...24C21D3`; `logs/prototype-manifest.sha256` pins them). Work is in `.local/descriptor-data-review/tree`; `review-tests.patch` (+213/-0, both hunks inside `mod tests`) applies to the prototype and reproduces the review tree byte-for-byte with `core.autocrlf=false`.
- Verdict: no 24-byte stride and no real bug. The numbers are different structures: 12 B is one metadata record (3 dwords); 25,165,836 B is the whole table `(2^21 + 1) * 12` = 24.0 MiB + 12 B sentinel; 32 B is the title's own view-heap slot (`heap_index = slot << 5`) that the raw size load still uses; 8 MiB is the pre-existing one-u32-per-slot stride table. The only 24-byte items are unrelated: the host's RTV/DSV descriptions (`descriptor.rs:243`), indirect argument strides (`indirect.rs:766,1058`) and the DXIL program header. No local report ever claimed a 24-byte metadata stride; the prototype/design say "24.0 MiB" and "12 bytes".
- GPU readback test (game-free, real hardware SM 6.6, DXIL validated 2,624 B): `emit::lower::tests::a_size_only_heap_load_reads_adjacent_records_and_the_zero_sentinel` builds a synthetic GFX10 compute shader with `s_lshl_b32 s8,s1,5` plus a size-only `s_load_dwordx8`, and stores the three metadata words to a raw UAV. Against a table with slot 0 buffer `0x1111`, slot 1 image (2D 1024x512/6/5), slot 2 buffer `0x3333`, slot `COUNT-1` buffer `0x4444` and a zero sentinel, readback is `[0,0x1111,0]`, `[1023<<30,0x007F_C0FF,0xD004_0FAC]`, `[0,0x3333,0]`, `[0,0x4444,0]`, and `[0;3]` for both `COUNT` and `COUNT+1`. Slot 1 is an image between buffers, so a wrong stride cannot accidentally agree.
- Negative control: changing only the shader constant to `WORDS * 8` (24 B) fails slot 1 with `left: [0, 13107, 0]` (0x3333 = slot 2's record); reverted. Log `logs/negative-24byte-stride.log`.
- Host test `descriptor::host::tests::descriptor_records_are_twelve_bytes_and_adjacent_slots_are_distinct`: asserts `WORDS*4 == 12`, `COUNT == 2_097_152`, offsets 25,165,824 / 25,165,836, table len, each of slots 0/1/2 reads its own record, sentinel zero and `COUNT+1` absent.
- Final offline suites (cwd `C:\Meraki`, `--offline`, review `CARGO_TARGET_DIR`): victory-shader lib 214 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored (`logs/shader-lib.log`), victory-gpu lib 118 / 0 / 1 (`logs/gpu-lib.log`), cache round-trip 1 passed (`logs/cache-identity.log`). The prototype's `.local/gpu-descriptor-data.log` failure is stale; the current tree passes, and the one-off `0xc0000005` harness crash did not recur.
- Cache identity: slot 252 rides the existing `root_srvs: Vec<u8>` written length-prefixed (`shader_cache.rs:231-234`, decoded at `:302`), so there is no new field and no schema bump, and entry length is unchanged. The build key (BLAKE3 over exe + loop limit + group sync + `VICTORY_WAVE_WIDE`/`DEEP_SKIPS`/`OWN_BITS`/`F16_NEAREST` + `shader_opt`) changes with the binary, so no old entry can be served; entries also differ by their `root_srvs` content. No cache change needed.
- Four puddle containers translate game-free and pass `dxil.dll`: `2fd01fca16993001` 68,659 B, `6b6db9f1a526daeb` 63,347 B, `7d0f14ce44746d7e` 69,481 B, `abc6a80d46edc294` 62,031 B, each `root_srvs=[252]` VALID (`logs/puddle-translate.log`, `logs/puddle-validate.log`). Sizes differ from the older twin-kind proof because the metadata path replaces it - expected.
- Correction: `.local/puddle-kind-proof/REPORT.md:51-55` mis-transcribes `8f0c850c` as `s_lshl_b32 s12, s12, 12`; it is shift **5** (dest `s12`, `ssrc1=0x85` = inline 5). Decoded from the fixtures directly: all four contain `8f0c850c`/`8f0d850d`/`8f0c850e` twice each and zero shift-12 variants (`ssrc1=0x8C`) - `logs/puddle-shift-decode.log`, `logs/puddle-shift-scan.log`. The design report already says 5; the older proof report's conclusion is unaffected.
- No change to the prototype or to `main`: the requested fix/regression was conditional on a real bug, and none exists. Runtime rendering of puddles remains an unclaimed live-test question. Evidence: `.local/descriptor-data-review/REPORT.md` plus its `logs/`, `tree/`, `probe/`.

### dirty-page refresh/watch batched asking - game-free benchmark and semantics (2026-11-24, offline perf worker)

- Scope: offline only. No title launch, live/visual test, desktop input, push or subagent. Writes only under `C:\Meraki\.local\dirty-refresh-perf`; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` were read-only and are not runtime dependencies. `python` used explicitly; the repo-local toolchain (`C:\.local\rustup`, `1.98.1`) at `--profile diag --features diagnostics`.
- Tree: a clean clone of `.local/fxceless-refresh` at `4e17478` in `.local/dirty-refresh-perf/src`; production call sites unchanged. Added: `crates/victory-gpu/src/dirty_refresh_perf.rs` (fixtures, probe, 8 semantics tests, benchmark, behind `#[cfg(all(windows, test))]`), `watch.rs` prototype (`ask_once_union`/`ask_union_run`/`region`, `open` returning its blocks, `guarded_written`, test-only `GUARD_JOURNAL` and `forget`), `copystat.rs` test-only `snapshot()`, one module line in `shadow.rs`.
- Semantics preserved - 8/8 tests: write-after-query is seen by the next asking (not the copy that follows the standing answer); a write-watch reset loses only the OS bits, so two copies both get a page from the journal; overlapping aliases produce **one** kernel asking and both copies update; `title_memory_remapped` makes the next asking take the block again; untracked memory keeps the whole-copy fallback; a partial tail page stays stale for its remainder; a guarded view write is in this submission's copy when journalled and is deferred exactly one submission (never lost) with `GUARD_JOURNAL` off; a 12-round sequence through `ask_once` and `ask_once_union` copies identical bytes and leaves identical title bytes.
- Fixture had to be corrected twice, both real kernel behaviours: `ReadProcessMemory` faults unfaulted 4 KiB granules and their zero-fill sets write-watch bits (so a fixture must pre-touch every granule, or everything reads dirty); and `VirtualQuery` reports a mapped view in 64 KiB pieces, so cutting a batched run at those boundaries splits every multi-page view - the prototype now tries the guards over the whole run first and only uses a region cut for unrecognized memory.
- Benchmark (real 174 MiB `MEM_WRITE_WATCH` pool + pagefile-section views, real `Shadow::update`, 58 copies of 4,700 KiB, 39 submissions, overlapping spans, 58 pages dirtied/submission, 2,262 askings and 10.89 GB checked per frame - the live log's 10.49-10.68 GB). Per frame, median of 7 rounds, watch path without memcpy: views-only 20.6 -> 5.8-7.0 ms (**2.95-3.57x**, 2,262 -> 0 askings, guard time 5.3 -> 0 ms); mixed 34 watched + 24 views 11.9-12.8 -> 6.4-7.9 ms (**1.62-1.84x**, 975 -> 39); watched-only neutral (6.3-7.9 vs 6.3-6.5 ms) because `ask_ahead` already merges one reservation per submission; mixed spans 4 KiB off page alignment 13.0-13.8 -> 7.3-7.4 ms (1.79-1.86x) with identical bytes. With copies (282 MiB/frame): views 42.2-43.8 -> 25.5-27.0 ms (1.62-1.66x), mixed 34.8-35.6 -> 27.6-29.1 ms (1.22-1.25x), watched ~parity.
- Residual budget: live is 505-590 ms/frame at 1.7-2.0 fps against 33.3 ms; watch path is 34.1-43.9 ms. A 46% (mixed) to 72% (views) cut projects to ~13-16 ms (mixed) to ~22-31 ms (views-heavy) saved, leaving a 474-577 ms frame - still 14-17x the budget, with preparing 190-244 ms, GPU 194 ms and copy-back 109-134 ms binding the frame. Safe but not close to 30 FPS.
- Not wired in: `host.rs` still calls `ask_once`; the union functions are `cfg_attr(not(test), allow(dead_code))`. Notes for integration: call `ask_once_union` at the existing submission asking point (`host.rs` ~3917-3942) with the same `ask_once_end`/`wrote` discipline; the journal's `written` map is global by page number and unpruned, so a freed-and-reused address inherits stamps (extra copies only; the harness added test-only `watch::forget`); a journalled view write is copied once more by the next asking's guard (existing behaviour).
- Verification: full `victory-gpu` lib suite **125 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** (2 pre-existing ignored + probe + benchmark, i.e. 125 includes the 8 new tests); `watch::` 7/7; non-test lib builds with no warnings. Evidence: `.local/dirty-refresh-perf/REPORT.md`, `probe.log`, `bench2.log`, `bench3.log`, `bench-final.log`, `suite.log`.

### dirty-refresh batched asking - integrated (opt-in) at the real host call site (2026-11-24, offline)

- Scope: offline only. No title launch, live/visual test, desktop input or subagents. Work under `C:\Meraki\.local\dirty-refresh-integration`; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only and not runtime dependencies. `python` used explicitly; repo-local toolchain 1.98.1 at `--profile diag --features diagnostics`, `--locked --offline`.
- Tree: clean clone of `.local/fxceless-refresh` at `4e17478` in `.local/dirty-refresh-integration/src`, plus the four prototype files. Batching is **opt-in** (`VICTORY_ASK_UNION=1` with `VICTORY_ASK_ONCE` not `"0"`); default (unset) is unchanged `ask_once` per span, so production parity holds without the flag.
- Call site: `host.rs` both submission askings (initial and the behind/stale re-ask) branch on `watch::union()`; the surrounding `ask_once_end`/`wrote` discipline is untouched.
- The prototype was not safe raw; three correctness fixes + one convergence fix were required: (1) merged runs larger than `AHEAD_MAX` are split, not truncated (the tail had been dropped); (2) a `title_memory_remapped` under a standing asking now stamps still-committed blocks via `guarded_written` (holes skipped) - otherwise the union's journal answers `writes` without re-guarding and would miss a remapped view block that per-span `ask_once` re-guards; (3) `watch::writing` and the fault handler journal their opened guarded blocks (else the title's own write after the batched guard is missed); (4) `copy_stale` takes `now.max(write)` so a remapped/own-written page is copied once, not every later refresh in the submission (identical where `write <= now`).
- Files: `watch.rs` (+`union`, `ask_once_union`, `ask_union_run`, `region`, `guarded_written`, `committed_until`; `open` returns its blocks; test-only `state`/`forget`/`GUARD_JOURNAL`), `host.rs` (union branch), `shadow.rs` (`mod perf`, `copy_stale` stamp), `copystat.rs` (test-only `snapshot`), new `dirty_refresh_perf.rs` (8 semantics + 7 integration tests, probe, bench, all `#[cfg(all(windows, test))]`).
- Verification: test build 0 warnings; `dirty_refresh` **15 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored**; full `victory-gpu` suite **132 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored** over 3 consecutive runs; non-test build 0 warnings. Aliases/write-order/remap proven by parity tests (remap parity, multi-flush with mid-submission `ask_once_end` + late write, 12-round union-vs-current sequence). Lifecycle bounded: 32 rounds show `kept`/`ahead` cleared at every `ask_once_end`, `written` no per-round growth, empty submission leaves nothing standing.
- Pre-existing flake `frame::tests::waits_end_on_ticks_and_count_frames` (timing assertion) was seen once on the untouched perf tree; passes 3/3 alone here and did not fail in 3 full runs. Not caused by this change.
- Not live-tested and not enabled by default. Expected from the perf prototype (same algorithm): view-heavy watch path 2.95-3.57x, mixed 1.62-1.86x, watched-only neutral; the frame is still 490-577 ms, so batching is a cost/latency reduction, not a 30 FPS fix. Enable with `VICTORY_ASK_UNION=1`. Evidence: `.local/dirty-refresh-integration/REPORT.md`, `build.log`, `build-nontest.log`, `dirty-refresh-tests.log`, `suite.log`, `suite-no-flake.log`.

### gameplay-gpu-budget - windowed indirect clamps made the default (2026-11-24, GPU-budget worker)

- Scope: offline only. No title/desktop launch, no live or visual test, no subagent. All writes under `C:\Meraki\.local\gameplay-gpu-budget` plus this `PROGRESS.md` section; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only and no runtime dependency on them. `python` used explicitly; repo-local toolchain 1.98.1, `--locked --offline`.
- Commits compared: `4e17478` (1-2 FPS lineage; binary `305C6F64...AA599B9`, 8,710,656 B), fork `a803c87` (5 commits: shader fixes + texture/VRAM work, `indirect.rs` untouched, binary `B9438D3C...CEA08`), upstream `940d32f` (separate lineage; source has no `VICTORY_INDIRECT_WINDOW`, `VICTORY_LATE_WAIT`, `VICTORY_COPY_BACK_RELAY`, `bound_ahead` or `VICTORY_INPLACE`, binary `BB2EC4C5...EB01BC`). No live gameplay log in the workspace is attributable to `940d32f`.
- Steady gameplay sample is only `tutorial-input-live\session-20261124-190543-hw` (`4e17478`): 480.8-510.9 ms/frame, 4,995-5,010 draws, 0 being made, 0 indirect; 194.4-197.2 ms "on the GPU", 172.7-206.0 ms preparing (copies 73.1-76.3 ms / 158.6-162.2 MB; textures 7.6-16.8 ms), 93.1-98.4 ms copy-back. The `a803c87` samples are not comparable: `195543` is compiler-bound (381-476 dropped, 1,033-1,035 being made), and `223158` is boot/loading.
- 0.7 FPS sample discriminated: `05:33:59`, 546 draws, 648 dropped (787 being made), 970 indirect, textures 324.8 ms / 136.2 MB, `heap buffers 1.0 passes 842.9 ms`, 1,455 uploaded / 21,851 waiting; next line 0.3 FPS / 3,794.7 ms. Pre-HUD compilation/upload, excluded from FPS claims.
- Hardware, not CPU software fallback: `runtime.log:52-54` reports `D3D12.X device version=0x20004`, host wave lanes 32-64 and `host D3D12 device ready`; no WARP/software adapter. The diagnostic `fallbacks` counter (`host.rs:5399`) is a retry after a failed bulk title read, 1-3/frame on the steady lines, not software rasterization. On the 2.0-2.1 FPS lines the card has `nothing of ours queued 282.7-315.0 ms` (~38-41% busy), 274.1-307.9 ms while the submitting thread worked, then a 196.8-200.0 ms fence wait. The card is starved by command preparation, transfers and synchronization.
- "On the GPU" is the submitting thread's fence wait, not card time: the card's own time by what ran is ~5-37 ms per pipeline group while the wait is ~194-197 ms. The GPU segment is serialization, and the card idles much of the wall clock.
- GPU-side bottleneck found and quoted from the log: `waits for every earlier shader write, per frame: 709-720 for the title's barrier groups ... 5,151-5,162 indirect commands bounded (a dispatch of ours between two such waits each)`. Each record is a compute dispatch between two global UAV barriers (`indirect::record_clamp_as`), ~5,000 a frame.
- Change (one variable): `draw::windows()` now defaults on, `VICTORY_INDIRECT_WINDOW=0` turns it off. A draw that continues the title's observed walk (32-byte steps; 16-byte steps only where the named stride allows) clamps 16 then 32 then 64 records in one pass, removing the per-record dispatch, its two UAV barriers and the draw-pipeline rebind. `next_window` was extracted unchanged into a pure function for testing. Quality-neutral by construction: identical clamped bytes per record; only budget timing differs, and a window record that asks for more than can run is left empty and charges nothing (run-93 guard).
- Validation on the local RX 7800 XT: focused `draw::`+`indirect::` **10 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored**; full `victory-gpu` lib **122 passed / 0 failed / 2 ignored**; `cargo check --offline --workspace` clean; clippy `-D warnings` clean; `diag` build 8,822,784 B, SHA-256 `5A4E26E1D5DDF69312358B1CF5856E77C5F9AC5F5EF87628320478BD710916C1`. Final ABBA hardware benchmark (5,000 one-record draws, step 32 B, record 12 B): single median 33.2639/28.7743/25.541 ms vs window median 3.674/2.7236/3.9682 ms, **9.05x/10.56x/6.44x** lower; CPU recording 10.3947/9.4087/10.6915 ms -> 389.6/396.1/346.6 us; 4,921 fewer clamp passes and ~9,842 fewer global UAV barriers per frame.
- Largest GPU-resident candidate identified, not enabled: title-memory in-place placement (`VICTORY_INPLACE`, off by default; `inplace.rs:66-90`, `121-169`, `192-213`; `shadow.rs:1878-1887`, `2351-2355`, `2786-2791`, `3008-3015`). It opens private committed read-write title pages as a D3D12 heap and places a buffer over them, so the GPU reads/writes the title's own bytes with no copy, refresh or copy-back. The measured host-copy inventory is 4,376 MB, including 3,392 MB in 822 windows outside buffers; if every steady copy + copy-back byte qualified, the optimistic upper bound is 166.2-174.7 ms/frame removed, leaving roughly 306-345 ms (~3.0-3.3 FPS). It excludes view/page-pool memory (reserved section refused) and big carded buffers, is capped at 4,096 MB, and stage 2 must not be combined with `VICTORY_LATE_WAIT`; no live in-place census exists.
- Expected but NOT live-verified: ~22-30 ms less GPU card time per lit frame from the windowed clamps, not 30 FPS. Perfect three-way overlap caps this frame at ~180 ms (~5.6 FPS); the recorded relay budget leaves ~310 ms even with every copy and copy-back removed. 30 FPS still needs overlap plus a ~5x cut in submitted work. `VICTORY_CARD_BUFFERS` is already on by default for big buffers; `VICTORY_CARD_REHASH_RUNS=1` (joins ~400-424 hash passes over ~1,089-1,189 pieces) is off and left for its own arm.
- Next live step (not performed): one hardware arm on the leftmost monitor `\\.\DISPLAY1` (work area -1920,0,1920x1032) with this binary and `VICTORY_INDIRECT_WINDOW` unset. Success signature in `runtime.log`: `indirect commands bounded` ~5,200 -> ~80 a frame, the matching drop in `waits for every earlier shader write`, and ~20-25 ms off the frame line's GPU wait with no visual change. Patch: `windowed-indirect-default.patch` (26,183 B, SHA-256 `C3470E15F43B6D35C31264601F087B32D1970D3C7808C0B71051B77BD1414744`, reverse-apply check passed). Binary `target\diag\victory.exe` not launched.

- Verdict: the `a803c87-223158` (session `20261124-223158-hw`) alternation is **real at present level, not a capture artifact**. `queue::blinks` records one brightest-channel level per present: frame 1 `0`; frame 1123 `44x11,0` (the same loading transition as every prior session); then 2703 `113..177,1`, 2705 `..177,1,46,1`, 2707/2709/2711 continuing `1,46,1,46,1`. One resource `0x1dffaf533e0`, `state=0x4`, `copied=true` throughout. Prior lit `190543` fades monotonically (`151,145,...,45,4`) then recovers; `195543` cuts to black at pause and stays; `192453`/`193244` have the same single `sudden_dark_after_lit_ramp` drop but climb back lit instead of alternating.
- Capture side separated: 109 saves / 78 unique at 1 s. Repeated byte-identical buffers are sampling artifacts (the desktop is slower than 1 fps here, presents 2-3 s apart) and skipped presents are sampling artifacts. The dim frame is not a scaled lit frame: `dim = 0.1235*bright + 21.65`, residual 8.6, i.e. a constant ~+22 lift over a scaled field - a different composite, not the title at 20% opacity. Near-black captures are 0.6 mean and the probe agrees, so `PrintWindow` is not dropping draws.
- Cause: under compile backlog the title presents its intro target before the composite is complete - one present carries background plus fade overlay (level 46), the next a cleared/unwritten target (level 1), repeating. The host unconditionally `CopyResource`s `plane.resources[0]` and `Present(0)` (`host.rs:5926-6035`, copy `:6000`, present `:6029`; `queue.rs:757-841`), so incomplete presents reach the screen. Contributing, not causal: pipeline `being made` 500-4,715/frame with the 500 ms wait at `pipeline/make.rs:97` gives the 8 s stall (30 fps -> 1-2 s presents, 274 MiB free commit).
- Fix, isolated worktree `.local/intro-flash/src` detached at `a803c87c48078e57b296e06e08146d7ceca44530`, 2 modified files. `queue.rs`: diagnostics-gated `should_hold_dark_present(levels, same_resource, held_last)` plus `PresentHold::observe/reset`; holds one present only when the probe says black (<8), the same resource as the previous present, a lit level (>=64) within the last 12 probes, and the tail is not a monotone fade (wobble 2 over 3 steps); `present` now passes the title handle. `host.rs`: `Presenter.hold`, `Host::present(..., resource)`, probe/dark decided before presenting, held presents skip `Present(0)` and the meter and log `held a dark present after lit content`, hold reset on swap-chain resize, captures (`read_now`) never held so evidence is unchanged. Non-diagnostics builds are unchanged.
- Offline regression: `cargo test --locked --offline --manifest-path C:\Meraki\.local\intro-flash\src\Cargo.toml -p victory-gpu --profile diag --features diagnostics` -> **126 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored** (pre-existing benchmark), doc-tests 0; all 7 new `queue::tests::intro_guard` cases pass (hold once per black present, release consecutive blacks, monotone fade shown, stable-bright cut shown, initial black + 44->0 loading shown, new resource/lit shown, bright frame past the 12-probe window not held). `rustfmt --check` drift is pre-existing (~1,800 lines); added blocks follow local style. Log `.local/intro-flash/tests.log`.
- Not live-verified and diagnostics-only: expected effect is that `1,46,1,46` no longer reaches the screen at the loading-to-title transition, with `held a dark present after lit content` in `runtime.log`; the pipeline wait and compile stall are untouched. ### in-game texture quality - a803c87 recheck audited, 64 KiB whole-hash correction (2026-11-24, offline)

- Scope: in-game texture quality only (textures picked by index, their recheck/refresh and its coverage). Offline only: no title launch, no desktop/device input, no subagent. All writes under `C:\Meraki\.local\gameplay-textures`; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only and not runtime dependencies. `python` used explicitly; repo-local toolchain 1.98.1, `--profile diag --features diagnostics`, `--locked --offline`, cwd `C:\Meraki`. Build inputs are a byte copy of `.local\fork-a803c87-build` (`a803c87c48078e57b296e06e08146d7ceca44530`) in `.local\gameplay-textures\tree`, so the tracked checkout is untouched.
- What `a803c87` does (verified in source): a whole upload stores `(at, len, print, looks, uploaded)` and is looked at again on frames `RECHECK_FRAMES = [4,12,40,120,400]` (+4, +16, +56, +176, +576 after upload, 5 looks). A changed fingerprint re-uploads and warns (first 200 and every 1000th); an unchanged one reschedules; the fifth look is terminal. A partly streamed upload (`again.is_some()`) gets no recheck - the `mapped`-bytes escalation covers it. A remap/write clears `checked`; a `seen` write preserves it. Fingerprint is a kernel read (unmapped pages count as zero) of `blake3(..)[..8] | 1`: all of it up to 48 KiB, else 16 KiB at start, `(len/2)&!0xfff`, end.
- Log evidence, 3 `a803c87` hardware sessions: `195543` hit the 200-warn cap (107 slots, 108 addresses, 16 slots repeated, one slot 24 times) between 03:01:02.74 and 03:04:20.94 UTC; sizes min 256 / median 55,552 / max 2,818,048 B, buckets <=4K:62, 4-48K:19, 48-256K:76, 256K-1M:23, 1-4M:20. **144 of 200 (72 %) are 55,552 B.** `223158`: 9 warns (8 x 55,552 B, 1 x 176,128 B), all at look 0. `220816`: 0 warns. 200 is the logging cap, not the total; `last_look_changes = 0` is silence because unchanged looks are never logged.
- **Correction to the 195543 record (including this file's gpu-budget entry "195543 is in world"): it is not a lit 3D world run.** Decoding all window-capture PNGs: 285 rich frames (mean > 10, `cap-0011`..`cap-0295`, 02:56:00-03:00:45 UTC, mean 36-144.5) are the picker and the CP1 intro art cards; from `cap-0296` (03:00:46) on, 364 frames have none mean > 10, 224 are pure black (mean 0, max 0), the rest are HUD text on black (mean <= 3.03). HUD onset 03:05:41 is 99.779 % black. The log agrees: 7-9 draws/frame, 381-476 dropped, 1.4-2.1 fps, 25,166 uploads / 7,472 MB by 03:07:59. All 200 logged changes fell after the last lit frame, during the load. So these captures neither confirm nor refute a surviving stale texture in 3D, and the earlier "median sampled fraction 0.8848" was an overcount (summed overlapping windows).
- Coverage defect, exact: for 55,552 B (13.5 pages) the three 16 KiB windows cover `[0,16384)` and `[24576,55552)` = 47,360 B (0.8525); the hole is exactly two pages `[16384,24576)` = 8,192 B, missed at every look. 176,128 B leaves a 69,632-B gap (0.279 sampled at 49,152); 2,818,048 B leaves 1,392,640 B (0.0174). `analyze_recheck.py` now merges ranges and reports the largest gap.
- Correction (one behaviour change, `tree\crates\victory-gpu\src\texture.rs`): `WHOLE = 4 * WINDOW` (64 KiB) instead of `3 * WINDOW`; each look hashes all of a size <= 64 KiB. Same API, same schedule, same five looks. Cost on 55,552 B is +6,400 B per look (+13 %); larger textures unchanged. Worst case if all 25,166 uploads were that size and took five looks: 0.8 GB over the session, 0.25-0.5 s at 0.3-0.6 ms/MB, under 0.5 % of wall time. It adds no look, no frequency and no steady-state per-frame work, so it cannot trade textures for FPS. Diagnostics-only: the 5 s frame line now prints `{picked_unchanged} recheck looks found them unchanged and {picked_changed} found them changed` from the already-collected `HEAP_TEXTURES_RECHECKED` counters, so a future run can count past the 200-warn cap.
- Regression: 5 new `texture::fingerprint_tests` (55,552 B hashed whole with changes at 16,384/20,480/24,575 and five other offsets seen; 65,536 whole; 65,537 keeps the pinned `[16 KiB,32 KiB)` hole; stable/never zero/distinct; absent pages read as zeros) and 1 new `descriptor::host::tests::a_recheck_is_kept_until_the_last_look_and_a_remap_forgets_it`. Negative control (tests added, 64 KiB change reverted) fails exactly `a change at 16384 is seen` and `64 KiB is hashed whole`, other 4 pass. With the change: targeted 6/6 pass; full `victory-gpu` lib **125 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored** (pre-existing `host::batch::texture_checks_per_submission` benchmark). Python `tests/test_evidence.py` **12 tests, exit 0** (`Ran 12 tests ... OK`), pinning the 200/1..200 sequence, 107 slots/108 addresses/24 rewrites, looks-to-frames, the 47,360/8,192/69,632/1,392,640 arithmetic, 144 covered sizes, the 03:01:02-03:04:20 window and the tree constants.
- Lighting separated, not a texture bug: `.local\mission-lighting\REPORT.md` (4e17478, same GPU) measures lit hair 108.2, hair rim 154.1, sky 132.7, lit wall 151.1, shaded torso 12.6 (p50 10.0) and 0 % pure-black pixels; the RT structure builder is force-gated off so the title's indirect/bounce light never runs. Lit 4e17478 checkpoints show correct skin/hair/cloth detail (ponytail strands, jacket weave, foliage), so dark geometry is missing lighting, not stale textures. Those frames are not a803c87, so the audit still cannot attribute a 3D stale-texture defect to the recheck either way.
- Residual, unfixed and stated: holes above 64 KiB (a 65,537-B texture keeps a 16 KiB hole; the 176,128-B and 2.8-MB gaps remain), the fifth look is final (~5.6 min at 1.7 fps), partly-streamed uploads get no recheck, a refused re-upload still logs "uploaded again", and per-change logging still caps at 200.
- Go/no-go: **GO for the isolated tree only; not applied to the tracked checkout, not built into the a803c87 binary, not live-tested.** Next live step (not performed): one a803c87-plus-correction arm on the leftmost monitor `\\.\DISPLAY1` (work area -1920,0,1920x1032), hardware rendering, chaos-free CP3 (sunlit hair) and CP5 (night face) captures on the same save; menu controls Enter = focused button, W/A/S/D = Tab-like selection, keyboard focus proven before each send; compare the new unchanged/changed counters; same-save negative control `VICTORY_HEAP_RECHECK=0`. A texture regression would be flat colour/garbage in a **lit** region, not a black shaded side. Evidence: `C:\Meraki\.local\gameplay-textures\REPORT.md`, `correction.patch` (15,344 B, LF; hunks reconstruct `tree\` exactly), `patch-check.log`, `out\ANALYSIS.md`, `out\recheck.json`, `tests-evidence.log`, `tests-negative-fingerprint.log`, `tests-negative-queue.log`, `tests-correction.log`, `tests-full.log`, `build-baseline.log`, `tree\`.

### intro glitch root-cause review (2026-11-24, offline)

- Scope: offline review of `a803c87-live/session-20261124-223158-hw`; no title launch, no desktop input, no subagents. All new evidence is under `C:\Meraki\.local\intro-root-cause-review`; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` stayed read-only and are not runtime dependencies. `python` was invoked explicitly for analysis.
- Verdict: the intro presents really are incomplete composites, but the mechanism is a **pipeline-creation storm plus drop-on-timeout**, not a cleared target or token inversion. Frame 2703 first-binds **1,384 unique pipelines** (1,880 first-bind lines; 1,595 unique for the whole run), while only **114** host-pipeline creation lines (113 distinct shader sets) appear during its ~8 s frame. `pipeline/make.rs:10-15/101/120` allows 500 ms/frame, then drops the draw; the present therefore copies only the passes whose PSOs happened to be ready (flat level-46 gradient or level-1 near-black).
- Tokens/ordering/RT: 2,712 presents, tokens 1..2712 with zero gaps and max step 1; `late()` is off, submissions are synchronous (`host.rs:3659`), and there are zero fence-wait lines in the flashing window. The same target `0x1dffaf533e0` is used with `state=0x4`, `copied=true`; no swap/resize/format errors. One held-submission force at frame 1936 (`05:33:16.248`) is 37 s before the flash.
- Drops: nearest summaries are 2703 = 834 (747.9 RT-builder-off + 500.2 being-made), 2707 = 648, 2709 = 2,624, 2711 = 717. Exact `blinks` draws since the previous present are 1,051 / 1,109 / 546 / 2,789 / 2,309. The host **does** filter draws (though not which present it shows); the earlier report's "host does not filter" is backwards as a draw-admission claim.
- Frame suppression rejected: `should_hold_dark_present` only holds level `<8` (`queue.rs:765/798` in the uncommitted diagnostics worktree; not in `a803c87`) and cannot hold level 46; the observed `172,177,1,46,1,46...` would settle on a dim 46 image, not repair the intro. Latent defect worth fixing: `Plane.token` and `execute_x`'s token are ignored (`queue.rs:590/597/757` pristine); host copies then `Present(0,...)` (`host.rs:5926/6000/6029` pristine).
- Correction: required intro-composite work must never be dropped (stall/defer the frame instead); make PresentX token-aware and force/settle work tagged `<= N` that writes the present target; present only a complete frame (hold/repeat the previous complete frame, never partial content); prewarm the ~1,384-pipeline intro set; keep the RT-builder-off gate separate. Regression A: token 10 red then token 11 blue to the same target; `Present(10)` must read red. B: a blocked required PSO must block/defer with `dropped_required == 0`; current drop-after-500-ms behavior must fail the negative control. C: real monotone fades and deliberate level-46 frames must still be shown. D: live integration only, leftmost `\\.\DISPLAY1`, hardware rendering, keyboard focus proved, Enter activates and W/A/S/D move selection.
- Credits/other glitches classified separately (fxceless `578d459`): clipped glyphs (`Writt  By`, `Dir ctor of D  lop    t`) are font atlas/coverage; overlapping/stale rows are text clear/scroll/layout; frames 3153 vs 3161 are pixel-identical, a genuine crawl freeze amid 0.8 fps / 1,330.7 ms/frame / 4,933 draws / 3,237 dropped; mission-entry black plus the `0x887A0006` device removal are separate and higher severity. None is an intro-present fix, and none should use frame suppression.

(68733304832, 115200)` and multiple 2560x1440 inputs/UAVs. `runtime.log` in `fxceless-refresh-hardware\session-20261124-144046-hw` shows it translating (line 669, `user_data=2`), a host pipeline (line 671) and first bind at frame 19,558 (line 71,235): translation refusal and pipeline refusal are both ruled out.

Current clamp runs at most 65,535 per dimension (65,535/8 = 8,191.875 tiles of 14,400), so the tail of the tiles â€” the bottom band of the screen â€” never gathers and keeps its previous contents. `115200` in the ops file is a buffer length, not a recorded group count; the count is inferred from the tile arithmetic. Class: bad inputs / host limit, not a refusal and not missing RT bounce.
- Patch: `.local\shader-lighting-triage\finalgather-dispatch-split-a803c87.patch`, 21,593 B, 464 lines, SHA-256 `165A76ABA6C5EB1825884638CFF57B6BCC2F764C3787BE2228F079173D81FB1C`; 6 files, +237 / -25 (`draw.rs`, `indirect.rs`, `emit/lower.rs`, `emit/mod.rs`, `emit/shader.rs`, `emit/valu_tests.rs`). `USER_GROUP_BASE = 0x104` added for one-dimensional compute programs (compute stage, reads `GroupId.x`, not y/z, <= 20 user-data words, not spilled); direct dispatch loops `SetComputeRoot32BitConstant` + pieces <= 65,535; indirect path clamps one record into two 12-byte records (`has_count` bit 2) and runs two `ExecuteIndirect(1)` with base 0 then 65,535. Meraki's `windows()`/`next_window` path untouched.

New translation test `a_group_number_in_x_counts_from_the_host_s_first_group` **1 passed** â€” x-only reads get base 65,535; reading y yields no base and 0. Negative control (same shader, no split bit) `warp_count_buffer_ignores_garbage_tail_and_preserves_source` still clamps `100_000 -> 65,535` and leaves source/count buffers untouched. Regression `warp_window_leaves_unfilled_slots_empty_and_uncharged` passes after `record_clamp_as` changed from `ahead: bool` to `how: u32`.
