### RPCS3 memory review - offline hypothesis review (2026-11-25, lead)

- Scope: the Antigravity plan exists only as a user-screenshot hypothesis; no plan artifact was read. No title/live run, desktop input, subagents, production merge or ntfy. Reviewed RPCS3 `d264e2f19ff82d8313474549a8e78ae8f03b8060` (surface/texture caches, predictor, DMA, cellGcmSys, vm) and Meraki `f8ce68b` / `baseline` `ffe310e` (`xmem.rs`, `watch.rs`, `shadow.rs`, `host.rs`). All new artifacts are in `C:\Meraki\.local\rpcs3-memory-review`; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` stayed read-only.
- Verdict: RPCS3 is a policy reference, not an architecture to port. PS3 has split 256 MiB RAM+VRAM with explicit 1 MiB EA-to-IO mapping; Xbox is unified, so only the cache discipline transfers: last-use-tagged resident objects, dirty/locked/reusable state, stale-deferred-flush rejection, 256-resource invalidation cap, 50-flush/frame cap with predictor, clean-only eviction. EA/IO tables, typed surface conversion and dirty-data invalidation do not transfer.
- New offline finding: `baseline\crates\victory-gpu\src\shadow.rs:219` decides copyback by value (`h != old`, `t != h`). A title write that restores the pre-submission value during a flight is indistinguishable from "title did not write", so the GPU byte resurrects over the title's write. `GetWriteWatch` reports pages, not byte ranges, so exact ownership needs a new channel; options are conservative page ownership (lossy), drain-before-title-write (slow), or a future byte-range mechanism. This is distinct from the worker attributing current memory pressure.
- Prototype: `prototype/ledger.py` (canonical 64 KiB pages, aliases, title generations, snapshot, dirty/title ranges, deferred copyback, clean-only eviction, layout/remap invalidation, budgets, oracle), `workload.py`, `run.py`; `tests/test_ledger.py` **11 passed / 0 failed**, including the exact-value rewrite regression.
- Measured model run (`python -B prototype/run.py --seeds 1,2,3 --frames 12`): 36 frames / 108 submissions; 323 refresh pages (21,168,128 B), 110 skipped; 195 copyback pages, 12,779,520 B scanned, 5,667,414 B written; 4,689 title-win B; 100 layout conflicts; 237 evictions; 0 pending after drain; 0 budget breaches; 2,496 B metadata. Wall 17.6 s for the Python model only; no FPS or GPU claim.
- Budget hypothesis for the first Rust harness: 8 MiB/frame refresh, 8 MiB/frame scan, 4-8 MiB/frame written, 64 pages/frame, 64 evictions/frame, 4 MiB metadata, 2-frame pending age; on-demand drain before reads. Kill criteria in `REPORT.md`.
- Next: one game-free Rust harness (fake queue or `victory-gpu` test) under 5 s / 64 MiB asserting the exact-value rewrite, layout conflict and deferred tail against real `shadow`/`xmem` types. Not performed. No live step, no merge, no ntfy.
- Evidence: `.local\rpcs3-memory-review\DESIGN.md`, `REPORT.md`, `prototype\*`, `tests\test_ledger.py`, `out\prototype-run.log`, `out\prototype-results.json`, `out\tests.log`.

### gameplay CPU-side submission overhead â€” offline (2026-11-25)

- Scope: the serial submitting-thread path, separate from the GPU, dirty-refresh and copy-back workers. Offline only: no title/desktop run, no subagents; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only; `python` explicit. Worktree `C:\Meraki\.local\gameplay-cpu-submit\src`, branch `perf/gameplay-cpu-submit`, base `4e17478`, commit `772e0f1` (2 files, +431/-3). Evidence and the full argument: `.local\gameplay-cpu-submit\REPORT.md`.
- Steady window (11 frames, `.local\tutorial-input-live\session-20261124-190543-hw\runtime.log`, 480.8-526.8 ms/frame, median 489.9): preparing 172.5-215.9 ms (copies 72.1-83.8 ms / 157-163 MB, textures 3.4-16.8), GPU 193.0-205.2, copy-back 93.1-117.4 / 74-79 MB; card 34-40% busy, idle 284-377 ms behind the submitter; submitter waits 195.0-198.6 ms.
- Split the "copies": only **28/frame** are title CopyBufferRegion (`Step::Copy`, 0 joinable); **~14,150/frame** are `Step::Fill` reads of title memory into the lists' own upload memory (descriptor constants + root views). All one `ReadProcessMemory` each: moves **30.84-37.03 ms = 2.16-2.62 us/call** for 6.8 MB, 47.5-49.3k page faults. The rest of the copies phase is `Batch::update` refresh, owned by the wide/dirty-refresh workers.
- Candidate implemented, off by default: `VICTORY_PAGE_FILLS=1` reads consecutive fills whose sources share one 4 KiB page with one kernel call and scatters them in recorded order; a crossing/page-leaving fill ends the group, and an unreadable page falls back to the per-fill path (zeros as before). 5 equivalence tests pass. Release bench, 14,157 x 478 B: 1/page 12.00 ms (no group), 2/page 8.03 -> 7.18, 4/page 7.93 -> **3.72**, 8/page 7.80 -> **2.01** ms.
- Projection, not a measured win: at the title's 2.2-2.6 us/call the same call counts give ~15-18 ms at 2 fills/page, ~23-28 ms at 4, ~27-32 ms at 8 (3-6.5% of the frame); scaling the bench's saved fraction instead gives ~3-4 / ~16-20 / ~23-27 ms. The missing input is the fills-per-page density; no existing trace has per-fill addresses. 6.8 MB in dense sources implies >=8.5 fills/page, but that is not proven. Next step is one diagnostic counter run (distinct fill source pages per frame) and a matched live A/B; out of scope here.
- Command-list preparation has no candidate: each prelude must stay immediately before its own list (merging would leave a texture in a later run's start state during an earlier run). Release prelude bench: 3.9 us at 16 states, 18.3 at 150, 33.8 at 300, 70.4 at 600; `Reset`+`Close` 1.4-1.7 us. 38 preludes of 600 states = 2.7 ms; 150-300 states = 0.7-1.3 ms. The earlier 6-25 ms figure was a 10x arithmetic slip.
- Rejected with measurements: `NtWriteVirtualMemory` for the guarded copies is not faster (8.54 vs 8.41; 7.03 vs 6.84; 6.94 vs 6.73 ms); `VICTORY_JOIN_COPIES` still has 0 of 28; `NtReadVirtualMemory` no win.
- Validation: `cargo test -p victory-gpu --features diagnostics --lib` **122 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored**; `cargo build --profile diag -p victory --features diagnostics` exit 0, `victory.exe` 8,704,512 B, SHA-256 `a221db15669f45003fe9b00085d64fe9543db4be694c925897a73697a1329213`. Logs in `.local\gameplay-cpu-submit\logs\`. No live step, no merge into the main tree, no ntfy (offline projection, not a verified milestone).

### structural frame budget: 15 FPS is blocked by work volume, not scheduling (2026-11-25, lead-owned gameplay integration)

- Scope: `C:\Meraki` only; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only; `python` invoked explicitly; no subagents; tracked checkout unchanged. One hardware RX 7800 XT instance on the leftmost `\\.\DISPLAY1`, DRED on, native pacing, WARP off; menu controls Enter (activates the focused button) and W/A/S/D (move selection like Tab) with keyboard focus sampled before every send. Full statement in `.local\gameplay-integration-test\STRUCTURAL.md`.
- Card-time attribution, new and decisive: the title's own `the card's clock per frame` lines (251, aligned by timestamp to the frame lines of `live/session-20261124-233953-hw`) give `corr(frame ms, card idle ms) = +0.994`, `corr(prep, card idle while the submitting thread worked) = +0.984`, `corr(gpu ms, submit wait) = +1.000`, and card busy p50 39 % = 211/544. Frame wall time **is** card-idle time; the card sits idle 73 % of every frame while the CPU prepares. With prepare 201.7 ms, card 211.2 ms and copy-back 115.7 ms serial, perfect overlap cannot go below `max(202, 211) = 211 ms` -> 4.7 FPS. 15 FPS (66.7 ms) needs 3.0x less prepare and 3.2x less card time.
- In-place residency measured and rejected. `live/session-20261125-000554-hw` ran `VICTORY_INPLACE_PROBE=1` (4 MiB). Driver support is real (private memory and a committed section in place read true and write true; a reserved section is refused `0x80070057`), but the +30 s census kills the idea: stage 1 covers **1 of 4 MB** (windows outside buffers), 7,131 MB of title memory sits in reserved sections (710 + 185 + 6,236 MB) the driver refuses, and another 320 MB is scattered view pages. Stage 2 would reach ~1,032 MB at intro, not the 4-9 GB the 166-175 ms bound assumed. Opening costs 2.1 ms per 4 MB against 0.4 ms for the card to copy 4 MB.
- Candidate ceiling recorded so it is not re-litigated: in-place realistic ~30 ms, relay stage 1 ~100 ms, windowed indirect ~25 ms, wide refresh ~35 ms -> ~354 ms, ~2.8 FPS. Structural floor with zero copy/copy-back and perfect overlap: `max(202-35, 211-25) ~= 186 ms`, ~5.4 FPS. No queued candidate is credible for 15 FPS. Blocking dependency is per-frame work volume - 157 MB copied up + 75 MB back + 34 compare passes + 5,072 draws at 3840x2160 in 211 ms (41 us/draw) - plus the title's reserved-section allocator, which we do not control.
- Card busy is real work, not queue latency (`card_attribution2.py` over the same session): 19 settled frames with copystat buckets give card busy 149.8 ms in 8.4 timed submissions of 17.7 ms, 12 pipeline buckets/frame, largest 20.7 %, top-3 45.3 %; the wait histogram sums to 214.2 ms (19.7 subs under 2 ms = 11.7 ms, 11.2 of 2-8 ms = 47.4 ms, 6.3 of 8-32 ms = 110.0 ms, 1.0 longer = 44.7 ms), matching the 211 ms fence. No single pass dominates and there is no large latency penalty to recover, so the 4.7 FPS overlap ceiling stands as a ceiling.
- Memory: a second settled window (`233953`, 195-315 s, 118 s) gives private -154 MiB/min, VRAM dedicated -103 MiB/min, commit used -184 MiB/min, texture `waiting` flat, `unmapped` +66/min, pages/s p50 513 - the same bounded-cache/streaming signature as the earlier 40 s window, still short of the 3-5 min repeat that was asked for. The apparent release (private to 7.4 GB, VRAM to 10.8 MB) is teardown after window close only, not gameplay.
- Counter defect pinned: `Win32_PerfFormattedData_GPUPerformanceCounters_GPUEngine` reports 0.0 on **every** engine for the title pid (18/18 slow rows all-zero) while `GPUProcessMemory` works. The earlier "GPU 3 %" was this defect, not the title. `analyze_memory.py` rewritten to mark those four columns INVALID and use `card_busy_pct`/`card_idle_ms`/`card_idle_working_ms`/`card_submit_wait_ms` instead (`analyze_memory.orig.py` kept). `memattr.ps1` column arithmetic re-verified: 42 header fields, 42 per row, fast and slow branches agree.
- Harness: `tools/accept_long.py` is `accept.py` plus (a) `memattr.ps1` launched against the same session dir and (b) an opt-in `MERAKI_SETTLE_S` settled phase with a focus-checked W press every third of it. Leftmost placement, picker/HUD gates, movement checks, guards and teardown are unchanged. `memattr` is now wired to the right path (`BASE.parent`).
- **Live testing is blocked by hardware.** Disk 1 (`\Device\Harddisk1\DR1`, the `D:` title drive) logged nine IO retries at 07:07:22Z and nine paging errors at 07:08:14Z, then detached. `D:\GTA6.Xbox.v1.0.2.1-CYBERLEEK\game_scarlett_master.exe` no longer exists, only Disk 0 remains, and no copy of the title exists under `C:\Meraki`, `C:\games` or `C:\XboxGames`. The run-000554 crash (`0xc0000005` write 0x0 in `victory-image` immediately after 1,776 failed dstorage reads) is a consequence of that loss, not of the probe arm - the probe and census lines before it are valid, and a preflight-blocked retry confirmed `title_exists=false`.
- Go/no-go: **NO-GO for in-place, relay-only, indirect-only and every combination against 15 FPS.** No live arm should be started until the title drive is restored. Intro PSO prewarm, the queued final-gather split and the queued texture A/B are blocked on the same dependency.
- Evidence: `.local\gameplay-integration-test\STRUCTURAL.md`, `analyze_memory.py`, `tools\accept_long.py`, `tools\make_accept_long.py`, `memattr.ps1`, `live\session-20261124-233953-hw`, `live\session-20261125-000554-hw`, `tools\check_disk.ps1`.


### Xbox shared-memory / UMA audit - offline (2026-11-25, lead)

- Scope: seven existing hardware RX 7800 XT sessions plus source; offline only, no title launch, desktop input, screenshots or subagents. `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` were read-only; `python` was explicit; builds ran with `C:\Meraki` as the cwd. Artifacts: `C:\Meraki\.local\uma-memory-audit`.
- Title backing: 4.63 GiB private heaps + 6.24 GiB page pool = about 10.9 GiB of title-owned commit; the 6.8-7.4 GiB of views map the pool and are aliases, and the pool reads 0 MiB resident. Private map categories reconcile with `PrivateUsage` (error 36-210 MiB). Do not add private + mapped, or views + pool + heaps.
- Host copies: 4.2-4.5 GiB total, 0.98 GiB buffer shadows plus 3.1-3.5 GiB of windows. Window overlap is 4-5% on the 4 MiB grid and 32.2% on the 2 MiB arm; that arm ends in a descriptor fault (`0x887A0006`, address past a view-heap end), not OOM.
- VRAM: 7.2-10.0 GiB LOCAL of a 15,409 MiB budget, 5.6-6.2 GiB shared, 5.7-8.4 GiB host-texture footprints. `CurrentUsage` is allocation accounting, not residency; the probe shows no movement on GPU touch and 5.4-8.2 GiB of LOCAL headroom.
- Commit is the binding limit: 20.2-24.6 GiB private vs 9.9-17.1 GiB resident, free commit 124-852 MiB at the low points while 1.4-4.1 GiB of physical RAM stayed free. The machine has 31.92 GiB RAM and a 12.8 GiB pagefile; the ceiling, not RAM exhaustion, is what ran out.
- Write-combined 8-11 GiB resolved: the 32 MiB block group (4.6-6.6 GiB) matches DXGI shared; the other 3.5-5.1 GiB is smaller WC blocks with no DXGI charge. A headless churn probe reproduced the split: DXGI returns on release while WC VA lingers (128x1 MiB left +96 MiB; 64x10.67 MiB left +86 MiB after 3 s; 8x256 MiB returned fully), and a 2 GiB allocation of 256 MiB buffers flushed the cache. This is a bounded driver cache at the upload high-water, not a title leak and not VRAM.
- Fxceless is bounded: windows, staging and idle copies are swept, WC and texture totals plateau for 9.5-12.5 min, and 255-281 never-reset host lists cost 0.71-0.88 GiB of growth. `PLACED_TEXTURES` retains an entry per distinct placed texture for the run. Baseline `ffe310e` has no window sweep (`shadow.rs:441`, `LOOSE` map), a source-level unbounded risk that no baseline log measures here.
- Probe ledger: `UPLOAD` is `PAGE_READWRITE|PAGE_WRITECOMBINE` and charges 1x NON_LOCAL + 1x private; `shared_buffer` is `PAGE_READWRITE`, 1x NON_LOCAL + 1x private, no WC; `DEFAULT` charges LOCAL; placement over private or `SEC_COMMIT` charges NON_LOCAL; `VirtualProtect` to WC fails 87; a `READBACK` copy costs 1x NON_LOCAL + 1x private with a 64 KiB minimum. Two probe runs and two churn runs agree.
- Verification: `python -B .local\uma-memory-audit\audit_memory.py --selftest` PASS; `--all` PASS, including all map reconciliations; `analyze_wc.py` regenerated `wc_series.csv`; no live step, no merge, no ntfy (offline audit, not a live milestone).
- Next: game-free upload pool/ring prototype (64 concurrent 10.67 MiB uploads vs a pool of 8; target WC high-water near 85 MiB instead of 595 MiB), then a matched live A/B when the title drive is restored.
- Evidence: `C:\Meraki\.local\uma-memory-audit\REPORT.md`, `summary.json`, `ledger.csv`, `wc_series.csv`, `probe\main.cpp`, `logs\probe-pass1.txt`, `logs\probe-pass2.txt`, `logs\probe-churn-pass1.txt`, `logs\probe-churn-pass2.txt`.

### structural frame budget: work-elimination ranking + card-plan prototype (2026-11-25, offline)

- Scope: offline analysis only. All writes under `C:\Meraki\.local\structural-frame-budget`; `D:\` detached and `C:\Leonida` read-only throughout; `python` invoked explicitly; no subagents, no live launch, no desktop input, no drive recovery. Source of truth is the production fork `.local\fork-a803c87-build` (a803c87), not `baseline\crates\victory-gpu\src\shadow.rs`. No ntfy (offline analysis, not a verified milestone).
- Measured frame re-derivation from `live\session-20261124-233953-hw\runtime.log` (59 frames, 14 steady): 528.7 ms = 192.4 prepare + 212.3 GPU + 110.5 copy-back + 13.5 other. Prepare is 73.0 ms of snapshots (404.9 MB) plus 46.0 ms of card find (1,058 pieces); copies are 87.6 ms / 157.5 MB, textures 3.7 ms / 6.0 MB. Copy-back is 381.4 MB in 379 declared ranges, 352.8 MB (92.5 %) in ranges >= 1 MiB. Only the four top-level terms are an exact identity; the sub-terms overlap.
- Refresh is the hidden multiplier: 24,104 `Shadow::refresh` picks declare 18,403.0 MB for 157.5 MB of real change, because `Batch::add` merges every range of a shadow into one min..max span. Watches walk 10,332.6 MB to find 10,182.0 MB unchanged, moves cost 37.5 ms, the full-sweep heap pass costs 72.9 ms, list reset 17.7 ms, 48,392 page faults.
- Card work is real, not queue latency: 149.8 ms busy per frame in 8.4 timed submissions of 17.7 ms, 12 pipeline buckets, largest 20.7 %, top-3 45.3 %; 5,072 draws and 33,506 mesh groups at 3840x2160 in that 149.8 ms (approx. 30 us/draw). No single pass dominates, so there is no free 10-45 ms win hiding in the submission stream. Card memory is 9,630 MB of a 15,409 MB budget.
- Ranked work-elimination options (REPORT.md section 2): (1) card-side shader-write detection for every declared range, -119 ms CPU for +2..30 ms GPU, gated on card budget; (2) dirty-page map / refresh coalescing, -30..60 ms CPU; (3) generation-based heap refresh, -40..70 ms (overlaps 2); (4) relay copy-back overlap, -100 ms critical path but removes no work; (5) coalesce card passes, -10..45 ms GPU; (6) barrier/indirect trim, -22..30 ms measured; (7) list reset, -17.7 ms; (8) fewer draws / mesh groups, needs -145 ms GPU and is out of scope because it is geometry.
- Verdict: 66.7 ms is unreachable with the measured workload. Even removing all CPU detection and perfecting overlap cannot beat the card's own 149.8 ms of measured busy time (6.7 FPS floor). A credible combined package (options 1-3, 5-7) lands around 180-210 ms, 4.8-5.6 FPS. If the card budget gate cannot be raised, option 1 is unavailable and the no-overlap estimate is nearer 300 ms, 3.3 FPS. What would change the verdict: measured card *execution* under 70 ms, a measured compare pass at or below the piece-fetch cost (about 12 us), title draw/LOD cooperation, or a title `SEC_COMMIT` allocator we control.
- Prototype: `.local\structural-frame-budget\prototype\card_plan.rs` models the leading option (card-side detection for every declared range of a carded copy, `Compare` vs `Fetch` per range, pieces shared once). 9/9 tests pass (`logs\run-tests.log`), SHA-256 `F18C9FAA9D263E24C1FD87F2B01775505CDF9035A7FFCB69D88B2F0D92BB18C8`. Tests cover shader-identical piece cutting on unaligned copies, exact tiling per span, byte-identical card/CPU merges including a racing title write and the case where the CPU side keeps old bytes outside the changed stretch, a neighbour flag never writing outside its range, the pass/fetch decision including the cheap-touching-pass case, the never-worse-than-fetch property, and shared pieces fetched once. Demo over the measured 379 ranges / 352.8 MB: scattered shape (whole undeclared piece) 379 passes, 5,762 pieces compared, 985 flagged, est 13.7-42.1 ms, saves 76.9-105.3 ms of 119 ms; packed shape 1 pass, saves about 107 ms.
- Budget gate for option 1: the current path already holds 492 MiB of 512 MiB card buffers, so a raise of the cap to roughly 845-900 MiB is a hard prerequisite. That is a configured limit (`card_limits` default 16 MiB / 512 MiB), not a hardware one, but it must be re-validated on the live build before the option counts as available.
- Next: (a) port the plan into `card_before` for a803c87 behind a raise-cap arm; (b) the 64 KiB dirty map for refresh coalescing; (c) the first offline card-side pass-cost microbenchmark, since `pass_us` 5-80 us is currently unmeasured and the 17.1 % flagged fraction is an estimate anchored on the measured card path. Real code plans per submission, not per frame, so 379 passes is the worst case and sharing can only improve it.
- Evidence: `C:\Meraki\.local\structural-frame-budget\REPORT.md`, `prototype\card_plan.rs`, `logs\[run-tests,demo,compile-tests,compile-demo].log`, `parse_frames.py`.

### upload pool probe - game-free ACCEPT (2026-11-25, offline)

- Scope: `.local\upload-pool-probe`; headless hardware RX 7800 XT D3D12, 64 x 10.671875 MiB uploads versus an 8-slot fence-safe reuse pool, same 683.0 MiB DEFAULT destination. No live game, desktop input, screenshots, or subagents; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only; `python` explicit; build cwd `C:\Meraki`; no ntfy (offline microbenchmark, not a live milestone). Adapter is `UMA=0`, so this is a Windows/D3D12 result, not an Xbox UMA claim.
- Verdict: **ACCEPT for the isolated synthetic workload**. Peak private-commit delta 702.3 -> 103.3 MiB (-599.0); peak DXGI shared/NON_LOCAL delta 683.0 -> 85.4 MiB (-597.6); peak WC commit delta 699.1 -> 101.4 MiB (-597.6); live upload resources 64 -> 8. Allocation 131.1 -> 30.2 ms, CPU byte-fill 155.0 -> 156.1 ms, slot wait 0.0 -> 0.0 ms (56 pool waits, 0.017 ms total), final drain 1.4 -> 1.5 ms, copy phase 299.4 -> 200.8 ms.
- Byte parity: both arms compared all 716,177,408 bytes (683.0 MiB); expected and actual hash `0xa2b34e77e7ec1477`. Two passes per arm gave identical memory high-waters.
- Safety: partial command list abandoned without executing (destination unchanged), simulated pre-submit error discarded and retried, true half-copy changed only the requested half, 256 queued 4 MiB copies observed in flight, `TryAcquire()` refused reuse while pending, fence wait completed (76.308 ms for that gate test), bytes correct after wait, reuse after fence produced new correct bytes, device-removed reason `0x00000000`; all PASS.
- Caveat: the memory saving is primarily allocation-count driven (8 live buffers instead of 64, about 85 MiB versus 683 MiB of payload). It reproduces the audit's bounded-live-buffer direction, but does not yet prove the title can run with 8 slots or that steady-state per-frame memory falls by the same amount. The faster pool phase is also allocation count: fill and drain were unchanged. No FPS or live throughput claim.
- Reject criteria: parity failure, unsafe partial/error handling, in-flight reuse, no memory reduction, added wait >5% of the churn phase or pool phase >105% of churn, or savings requiring a stall/corruption. None triggered.
- Next: matched live A/B when explicitly authorized, with one hardware instance, same save/settings/warm caches and matched phases; measure live upload count/bytes, WC VA, private commit, DXGI NON_LOCAL, copy-phase time and slot waits. Live controls: verify keyboard focus; Enter activates the focused button; W/A/S/D move selection like Tab. Not performed here.
- Evidence: `C:\Meraki\.local\upload-pool-probe\REPORT.md`, `main.cpp`, `build.ps1`, `run.ps1`, `analyze.py`, `bin\upload-pool-probe.exe` (SHA-256 `680422E2B260674E992C6B5838FF9EEE5E35D8243673996901CE2A3744D2F2C7`), `logs\summary.md`, `logs\summary.json`, `logs\churn-pass1.json`, `logs\churn-pass2.json`, `logs\pool-pass1.json`, `logs\pool-pass2.json`, `logs\safety.json`, `logs\*.log`.

Deliberately did not require `copies_ms + textures_ms <= cpu_prepare_ms` (5080 frame 4158 violates it). Smoke is offline file parsing, not a live run.
- Profiler semantics: `frames`/`fps`/`ms_per_frame` are per-interval summaries (1-s buckets at ~1.8 FPS hold 1-2 presents, so `frame_ms_avg` is meaningless; `frame_ms_max` is usable; `LAST` is not reset at an interval boundary; zero-present buckets emit no row). `tick()` runs at the top of `Meter::frame`, so it is present-call cadence, not scanout. CSV `gpu_ms`/`gpu_pct` are the submit-thread fence wait, not GPU execution; Meraki's `CARD_CLOCK_US` idle accounting and diagnostics `VICTORY_GPU_TIMING` timestamps are separate and absent from the CSV. Nested buckets (copies/textures inside prepare, heap_ms, card passes inside GPU time, copyback thread time) are not safely summable.
- Budgets from STRUCTURAL.md (measured): frame 544.4 ms = prepare 201.7 + fence wait 211.2 + copy-back 115.7 + 15.8 other; card busy 149.8 ms, card idle 396.8 ms (72.9%), idle-while-submit-worked 330.0 ms. Perfect prepare/wait overlap ceiling 211.2 ms -> **4.73 FPS**; vs card busy 201.7 ms -> 4.96 FPS. 66.7/33.3 ms need frame 0.12x/0.06x, prepare 0.33x/0.17x, fence wait 0.32x/0.16x, copy-back 0.58x/0.29x, card busy 0.45x/0.22x.

(2) prepare/submit/copy-back overlap (relay or late wait) - bounded by the 211.2 ms ceiling; offline double-buffer fence model, live card-idle-working p50 falls without device loss. (3) matched-resolution `VICTORY_DISPLAY_MODE=1920x1080` one-arm A/B (`dxgi.rs:222-246` synthesizes 4K60); never a default. Rejected: in-place residency (reserved refused `0x80070057`), global UAV barrier narrowing (unsafe without per-resource tracking), command reuse (already upstream), host/guest reuse (no evidence), timestamp-as-frame-change (attribution only).
- GROUP_SYNC verdict: correctness/divergence lever, not a perf lever for a 544 ms frame with 73% card idle. Meraki `a803c87` accepts only 0/1 (else default 2); `auto`/3 is skornuta's #608 divergent-barrier mode whose default caused constant flicker on this GPU (`README.md:27-28`). No default change. Only a one-arm diagnostic with frame-by-frame image-hash A/B and revert on first divergence.

Any live A/B needs explicit authorization and the menu rule: verify keyboard focus; Enter activates the focused button; W/A/S/D move selection like Tab. Hardware rendering default; WARP for software diagnostics only.

### Frametime card-idle + GPU-timestamp export - offline ACCEPT (2026-11-25, lead)

- Scope: offline only under `C:\Meraki\.local\frame-timing-validation` (scratch worktree, branch `perf/frametime-validation`, base `a803c87`). Build cwd `C:\Meraki`; `python` invoked explicitly; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` not read or written; no title launch, no desktop input, no subagents, no push/merge. Not a live milestone, so no ntfy.
- Change: extends the isolated profiler from `.local/fps-architecture-research\REPORT.md`. New `crates/victory-gpu/src/gputime.rs` adds a D3D12 timestamp ring (8 slots, 16 timestamps, one permanently mapped read-back buffer, pre-recorded lists that are never reset). `Host::submit` polls completed spans with `GetCompletedValue` only, brackets a submission with a start/end list, and records the signalled fence value. No `SetEventOnCompletion`, no fence wait, no submission lock; exhausting the slots increments `gpu_ts_skipped` and the submission runs untimed. Off by default (`VICTORY_FRAMETIME_GPU_TS=1`); a queue that refuses timestamps is `unsupported`, tried once.
- Schema: `frametime.csv` 37 -> 47 columns, schema 2, original 37 unchanged and first. New: `card_idle_ms`, `card_idle_behind_ms`, `submit_wait_ms`, `card_clock_status`, `gpu_ts_ms`, `gpu_ts_samples`, `gpu_ts_invalid`, `gpu_ts_skipped`, `gpu_ts_pending`, `gpu_ts_status`. `gpu_ms` still means the submit-thread fence wait; `gpu_ts_ms` is the card's own execution span, not another estimate of the same number.
- Missing vs zero: the four new measurements are `Option<f64>` and write an EMPTY field when absent; status columns disambiguate (`card_clock_status=ok|unavailable`; `gpu_ts_status=ok|pending|invalid|no_samples|unsupported|disabled|split`). A completed 0 ms span is present as `0.000` with `ok`; no sample is empty with a status. `VICTORY_GPU_TIMING` splits a submission into separately-waited groups, so that mode reports `split` and is never sampled (no fabricated `invalid`). Enforced by `gpu_ts_row`, `opt`, and a new pure `row_line` helper, so it is testable without a file or GPU.
- Validation (game-free, hardware RX 7800 XT, driver 32.0.32029.2010): 64 MiB source/destination x 64 `CopyBufferRegion` per submission plus a 2 ms CPU delay, 24 iterations, three runs. 24 samples each; mean 15.567-15.587 ms, p50 15.581-15.597, p95 15.628-15.670. Poll during GPU work max 4.4-10.1 us (mean 0.086-0.097 us). `fence_ahead` 0/24 in every run. The first immediate poll returned empty with exactly 1 span pending (a busy submission was not reported as 0 ms); later early polls harvested 23 genuinely completed older spans.
- Overhead: stamped path 9.36-9.74 us CPU per submission; unstamped baseline 7.62-9.36 us (noisy). Measured difference 0.15-2.12 us per submission (median about 1.3 us), plus about 0.1 us per poll. At 1-2 FPS this is under 0.001% of a frame. The end list's resolve sits inside the span tail. These are instrument numbers, not a game FPS claim.
- CSV safety: `validation/smoke_csv.py` is the 47-column successor of the research `smoke_csv.py`; it keeps the schema-1 checks and adds the conflation rules. `--selftest` passes: honest empty row passes, a fabricated `0.000` under `no_samples` fails, an `unavailable` clock with `0.000` fails, a real zero passes, a missing `# schema:` line fails.
- Matched 1080p experiment: DEFERRED, not prepared. The last live `runtime.log` confirms only the present/swap-chain size and advertised mode (`3840x2160`); it has no title render-target/viewport size, so actual render resolution is unconfirmed and the instruction's precondition is not met. Unblock with a back-buffer/render-target creation log or a live `VICTORY_DISPLAY_MODE=1920x1080` run showing present size change with unchanged draws/textures. Then a matched A/B at `1920x1080` vs `3840x2160`, same scene/cache/camera, never a default.
- Verification: `cargo check -p victory-gpu --tests --offline` pass; `cargo check -p victory --features diagnostics --offline` pass; `cargo test -p victory-gpu --lib --offline` **119 passed / 0 failed / 1 ignored**; `cargo build --release -p victory --offline` pass, 7,825,920 B, SHA-256 `80B8485ED9B2CCCB7CAA90FC2808F625A21A1E896A4208446BE1060314A99906`. `rustfmt --check` clean on `frametime.rs` and `gputime.rs` (not `host.rs`; base branch is not format-clean).

### GPU work elimination - offline ACCEPT (2026-11-25, worker)

- Scope: `.local\gpu-work-elimination`. Reviewed the settled hardware frame in `.local\gameplay-integration-test\live\session-20261124-233953-hw\runtime.log` (528.7 ms = prepare 192.4 + fence 212.3 + copy-back 110.5 + other 13.5; 5,072 draws; card busy 149.8 ms, 72.9% idle) and `.local\structural-frame-budget\REPORT.md`. Source semantics read from `.local\fork-a803c87-build` (`host.rs`, `indirect.rs`, `draw.rs`, `shadow.rs`, `copystat.rs`, `list.rs`). No title launch, desktop input, GPU work, D: access, or subagents; ran alongside an in-flight live profiler session without touching it; `python` explicit; build cwd `C:\Meraki`; no ntfy (offline work, not a live milestone).
- Verdict: three redundant per-pass items confirmed by source and log, ranked by size. Nothing here changes textures, geometry, or captures. **66.7 ms remains unreachable**: best case here is prepare 192.4 -> ~125 ms, landing near 460 ms, because fence 212.3 and copy-back 110.5 are untouched and the rest is the title's own draw work (out of scope under "retain geometry"). Same conclusion as the structural report, reached independently.
- Finding 1 (largest measured CPU cost): the card-copy gate leaves 352.8 MB/frame on the CPU. `runtime.log:20192` states 381.4 MB snapshotted on the CPU, 352.8 MB of it in copies of 1 MiB or more, with the parenthetical that `VICTORY_CARD_BUFFERS_MIN_MB=1` "would have the card look there". That buys `snapshots 73.0 ms` (`:20315`) and `46.0 ms finding 1058 pieces` (`:20316`) while the card idles 72.9%. **The log's hint is incomplete**: `card_for` (`shadow.rs:193-225`) also enforces `VICTORY_CARD_BUFFERS_MB` (512 MiB total), and 492 MB is already carded, so the minimum alone recovers only ~42.8 MiB of headroom and refuses the rest. Both knobs must move (min 1, total ~900). Card memory fits (9,630 of 15,409 MB used; twin hashes are 16 B per 64 KiB piece). Worth ~67.5 ms of snapshot time plus an unquantified share of the 46.0 ms finding cost. It adds card-side hash/compare passes, so it pairs with finding 3.
- Finding 2 (largest structured redundancy): every clamped indirect command pays two global UAV barriers. `runtime.log:20331` reports **5,172 indirect commands bounded (a dispatch of ours between two such waits each)**, and `copystat.rs:123-129` defines that phrase, so this path alone records **10,344 global UAV barriers/frame** against 713 from the title's own barrier groups. `indirect.rs:359-390` plus `draw.rs:2537` is 12 D3D12 calls per command, i.e. **62,064 list calls/frame derived from source**. `second`'s global barrier is redundant because the `transition(dst, UAV -> INDIRECT_ARGUMENT)` that follows it is itself the write-to-read sync and `ExecuteIndirect` reads nothing else the clamp wrote. `done` exists only because `host.rs:5072-5093` returns one shared scratch buffer; a per-command slice removes it. `first` must stay and must keep ordering `budget`, because the `InterlockedAdd` there decides which records run empty. Net: 10,344 -> 5,172 global barriers, 62,064 -> 46,548 calls (-25%), 15,516 -> 10,344 transitions, with the dispatch count and budget sequence untouched.
- Caveat on finding 2: those calls go straight to the raw `ID3D12GraphicsCommandList`, so `copystat::recorded` (`list.rs:153`) never times them; the 43,318 calls / 810.6 ms recording line (`:20315`) is a different denominator and must not be divided into these. Call and barrier counts are exact; the milliseconds are not measured, so attribution comes before deletion.
- Finding 3: one hash dispatch per dirty range where one per copy would do. `runtime.log:20442` shows **594 hash passes over 1,570 pieces** against **34 compare passes** for 449 declared ranges, while the compare side already merges into spans (`host.rs:3158-3182`). `host.rs:1546-1557` says a pass costs a whole piece walk "however few its pieces are" and that no timing names them. Cheapest fix is the already-implemented `VICTORY_CARD_REHASH_RUNS=1` (off by default): flipping it and reading the same line settles whether it collapses to ~10 for free. Fallback is spanning clean gaps; `host.rs:3068-3073` already hashes every piece of a copy on first declaration and `:1555-1557` records that hashing a piece that need not be hashed "loses no shader write", so the operation is accepted by the code's own standard.

- Scope: `.local\card-copyback-bench`; build cwd `C:\Meraki`; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` not written; `python` explicit. No title launch, desktop input, subagents, merge/push, or ntfy (offline benchmark, not a live milestone). Both preflights clear: no game/profiler, GPU 0.0%; the live profiler was not run concurrently. Input: `.local\structural-frame-budget\REPORT.md` (379 ranges, 352.8 MB, 119 ms CPU snapshot+finding).
- Deliverable: a standalone D3D12 compute benchmark comparing (1) the existing snapshot+scan path, (2) a view-marked copy-back over 64 KiB page marks, and (3) exact `a803c87` `CARD_COMPARE` GPU change detection, on the representative 379 ranges in a scattered shape (6,139 pieces / 402.3 MB / 379 passes) and a packed shape (5,461 pieces / 357.9 MB / 1 pass), with sparse 1%, observed 17.1% and dense 100% workloads. Every round also applies 128 CPU/title writes (64 preserved, 32 colliding, 32 outside all ranges).
- Verdict: byte parity PASS on 252/252 measured rows (2 passes x 7 rounds x 3 arms x 3 workloads x 2 shapes), 0 errors, including preserved and colliding title writes. View-marked copy-back is the cheapest arm in all six cells: 1.84-64.31 ms vs baseline 64.55-158.60 ms (2.3-35.6x) and GPU detection 3.23-82.42 ms (1.2-6.4x faster than GPU). GPU detection still beats the existing path by 1.9-20.0x. At observed 17.1%: scattered baseline 110.14 / marked 11.76 / GPU 23.45 ms (compare 10.33 + fetch/merge 13.10); packed 111.53 / 11.55 / 14.80 ms (1.78 + 13.03). Both land inside the structural 77-105 ms saving band and the 1.9-30.3 ms compare band; the ~12 ms fetch/merge proxy is corroborated at 13.0-13.1 ms. Flagged pieces equal changed pieces in every row (no false positives/negatives).
- GPU pass cost: packed one pass 1,543-1,554 us (357.9 MB hashed, ~231 GB/s); scattered 379 passes 10,030-10,054 us (~26.5 us/pass mean, ~40 GB/s). The scattered mean is not uniform: exactly 12 passes per round (indices 0,33,66,...,363, every 33rd) each take 0.72-0.84 ms, independent of workload and identical in both passes, totalling 91.1% of the span; the other 367 sum to ~0.9 ms (median 0.04 us = one 40 ns timer tick). Pass intervals sum to the span (gap ~1 us). A single coalesced pass over the same bytes costs ~1.55 ms, so coalescing runs is the dominant GPU-side lever; the ~0.75 ms stalls look like a Windows/WDDM scheduling quantum and the absolute scattered 10 ms is host-specific.
- Memory: baseline adds a 402.3 MB snapshot; marked needs 6,139 B scattered / 5,461 B packed of page marks and no scan; GPU detection's persistent state is 98,224 B hashes + 49,112 B flags/readback (~147 KB), with a fixture-only second 402 MB image to reset each round. Process peak 2,732.9 MB working set / 3,124.1 MB private; DXGI local 774.4 MiB of 15,409.5 MiB, non-local 389.1 MiB of 15,580.2 MiB.
- Fidelity fix during the run: both dispatch sites originally issued one 64-thread group per piece; corrected to upstream `div_ceil(64)` (`host.rs:3134`, `:3219`) before the final measurement. Packed was bandwidth-bound and barely moved (~1.55 ms); scattered fell ~15% (11.8 -> 10.0 ms). Smoke on the corrected build was 18/18 parity OK before the full 252-row run.
- Not done / next: no live A/B. Next steps: deploy view-marked copy-back where views can be marked; for copies that cannot, coalesce GPU compare runs into one dispatch via a run table (design change, not measured here); do not change the card memory cap on this evidence alone (the fixture assumes the card already holds the range). Any live run needs explicit authorization, one hardware instance, matched scene/cache, hardware rendering, and the menu rule: verify keyboard focus; Enter activates the focused button; W/A/S/D move selection like Tab.

### Marked-page write tracking ported into a803c87 - offline, opt-in (2026-11-25, worker)

- Scope: `.local\marked-page-gameplay`; clone of `.local\fork-a803c87-build` on `perf/marked-page-gameplay` at `a803c87`, uncommitted. Port of upstream marked-page write tracking (`cd94d353` + `940d32f`, from `.local\victory-940d32f-build`) into the fork's heap-journal copy-back, as opt-in `VICTORY_VIEW_MARKS=1` (off by default). Distinct from the GPU-side copy-back benchmark worker: `copy_back` / `copy_back_with` and the card-copy compare are untouched. No game, desktop input, profiler, merge, commit, push, or subagent; `D:` and `C:\Leonida` not written; `python` explicit; build cwd `C:\Meraki`; no ntfy (offline work).
- Infeasible to merge: fork and upstream share base `ffe310e` but differ by 153 files (+38,428 / -39,680) - the fork rewrote `shadow.rs` and `watch.rs` and added `inplace.rs` and `capture.rs`, so neither commit applies. The idea was re-implemented: a marked copy-back asks the fork's write journal whether the title wrote the 64 KiB page since the copy took it (`copied` page stamps vs one `watch::writes` asking per copy per submission), keeps the title's bytes if so, else writes back the first-to-last differing bytes under `watch::writing` and advances the stamp. No per-submission snapshot for marked ranges. Upstream's arm/save design (option A) was rejected because it rebuilds the 17-21 ms snapshot the feature removes.
- Change: 17 files, +1,303 / -88 against `a803c87`. Shaders mark pages they write through heap views (dword, narrow d16, atomic; `mark_view_write`, DXIL-validated); root bindings `VIEW_MARKS` SRV + `VIEW_WRITES` UAV in `MEMORY_SPACE`; per-copy bitmap runs with owners; fingerprint byte in the shader cache. Gated in `attach_marks`: no card-memory copies, windows, placed memory, relay, or flights in the air (`late()`), and no root-descriptor root UAVs - those keep snapshots. Two correctness gaps found and fixed before the report: `writing` recorded marked/unmarked flags after the duplicate early-return (a copy declared both ways could skip its snapshot), and `hand_back` compared marked ranges against a snapshot they never took (stale-bytes hazard with `VICTORY_KEEP_SNAPSHOTS=1`); declared ranges are now split, and only unmarked ones are compared.
- Preserved: title writes win; per-copy aliasing via stamps and journal numbers; partial-page tails never touched (`copy_back_marked` clamps to the copy's end; new device test). Accepted limit: a page is all-or-nothing, so a shader write to a page the title also wrote in the same window is not put back (the page is refreshed from the title); upstream's byte-precise merge does not have this limit - option A would be needed to remove it.
- Validation (measured, game-free, hardware D3D12 available, offline): `victory-shader` 215 passed / 0 failed / 3 ignored; `victory-gpu` 122 / 0 / 1; `victory-gpu --features diagnostics` 129 / 0 / 1; `mark_tests` 8 passed (7 port tests + partial-page-tail test); `cargo check -p victory-shader -p victory-gpu --features diagnostics` exit 0 with no warnings; `cargo build --release -p victory` exit 0. `cargo fmt -- --check` still fails on the whole fork and on untouched lines (not rustfmt-clean at `a803c87`).
- Performance: no fork measurement. Upstream's game-free benchmark (174 MiB, 2,784 pages) measured snapshot 17-21 ms vs arm 1.4-1.7 ms and ~34 us per marked page: observed 252 pages 59.1 -> 9.9 ms (6.0x), dense 100% 257.0 -> 104.1 ms (2.5x), none 37.5 -> 1.5 ms. Estimated here, not measured: the saved whole-copy snapshot is the main gain; the per-page write-back should be the same order. No frame-time claim.
- Not done / next: no live run, so no evidence shaders set bits on real workloads or that marks and refresh stamps agree in play. Next: one hardware run with `VICTORY_VIEW_MARKS=1` and the diagnostics counters (`MarkedPages` / `MarkedBytes` / `MarkedKept`), then a run with the throughput switch of the day, no profiler attached; if `MarkedKept` is high, option A on the card-copy compare. Any live run: one hardware instance, matched scene/cache, hardware rendering (WARP only for software diagnostics), verify keyboard focus before input, Enter activates the focused button, W/A/S/D move selection like Tab.
- Evidence: `.local\marked-page-gameplay\REPORT.md` (14,614 B, SHA-256 `2B63946DDB6D524D85C96E860E2FB2717BEBB910AAD8CF98BAB47ECE5C53EB12`); patch `.local\marked-page-gameplay\marked-page-gameplay.patch` (213,210 B, 17 files, +1,303 / -88, SHA-256 `3DE5135BECBF55B2E0134E367F4E984638352E115E0812543DDA100472B03423`); logs `test-shader-full.log`, `test-gpu-full.log`, `test-gpu-diagnostics.log`, `test-marks.log`, `check-diagnostics.log`, `build-release.log`, `fmt-check-after.log`.

Four PASS (`102457` off#1, `104047` on#1, `105738` on#2, `110512` off#2), two self-stopped on commit exhaustion before HUD (`101937` 172.8 MiB @65 s, `103356` 284.7 MiB @288 s). HUD onset 351.0 / 267.3 / 244.4 / 240.4 s from window move. Picker `picker_enter` after 5/5 focus; W did not move selection, S restored it, Enter advanced; focus checked before every input; W and L verified by picture change and SSIM in every PASS run.
- **The flag is inert on this workload.** Every ON run, every phase: `0 marked pages, 0.0 MB written back, 0 kept`, while ~399-400 MB/frame in ~7.6-7.7k declared steps was snapshotted and compared exactly as in OFF. No shader mark ever reached a copy, so the counters cannot show a speedup.
- No measurable effect in the balanced pair (off#2 vs on#2): fps +0.15/-0.51/+2.77/-0.75%, p50 -0.38/+0.83/-1.87/-0.38%, copy-back ms -0.71/+0.11/-7.16/-2.03%, snapshots -1.44/+3.55/-8.40/-4.25%. The 18-20% "gain" in ON#1 was host state: repeating OFF gave 91-97 ms copy-back vs 110-119 ms for OFF#1, and both ON runs straddle it.
- Collisions: `kept` 0.0 pages/frame in every phase of every PASS run (no shared 64 KiB CPU/GPU write observed). Device: 0 removals, 0 page faults, 0 heap overruns, 0 host GPU failure. Exit `wm_close`, not forced, helpers stopped, 0 remaining. Offline gate identical across all four PASS runs (device/passes/builder/draws/draw_loss/hud_stable PASS, shader_quality WARN 2 expected classes absent, cadence FAIL at 2-4 samples per ~20 s window, performance WITHHELD); same as OFF, pre-existing.
- Visual: one ON run (`104047`) showed a magenta band on the character's jacket for the whole gameplay window (195/216 late frames, max 67 px); the second ON run and both OFF runs show 0. Pixels are R112-193 G0-3 B110-185 - green zero in every pixel, i.e. a magenta missing-texture placeholder, not shader math or memory corruption; most likely a texture upload skipped under commit pressure with `VICTORY_TEXTURE_NO_WAIT=1`. Not reproduced, so not attributed to the flag, but recorded as an unresolved observation.
- Environment: all four PASS runs came within 309-516 MiB of commit exhaustion at 90-96% memory load (peak private 22.8-26.1 GiB of a 32 GiB machine, 12.5 GiB pagefile), so timing is coarse and host-state dependent. The ON arms used no systematically more memory (26,135 vs 26,148 and 24,672 MiB).
- Verdict: do not integrate. On this tutorial the opt-in marks nothing, keeps its snapshots, and changes neither frame time nor copy-back; the 64 KiB collision it exists to avoid never occurred. Next: count each `attach_marks` skip reason plus the draw-side `marked` flag, then repeat one ON run with min free commit above 2 GiB; only a run with `MarkedPages > 0` can measure the feature.

### Prepare budget: exact refresh ranges + generation skip - offline ACCEPT (2026-11-25, worker)

- Scope: `.local\prepare-budget`; isolated worktree `src` at `a803c87` plus the reviewed dirty-refresh integration. Build cwd `C:\Meraki`; `python` explicit; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only; offline CPU only while the live marked-page slot held the desktop, so no title launch, desktop input, GPU load, profiler, subagents, commit, push, or ntfy. The live game was not touched.
- Problem: live prepare p50 is 161.8 ms, of which copies are 69.0 ms / 157.2 MB. The refresh path declared 18,385 MB for 10,415 MB checked because `Batch` merged each copy's requested ranges into one min..max span, walking the gaps, and repeated copies re-walked ranges whose journal answer had not changed.
- Change (default on): `Ranges` keeps sorted, coalesced exact intervals per copy, capped at 16 before falling back to the old covering span; `C::GapBytes` reports bytes inside the span no range asked for; `VICTORY_REFRESH_SPAN=1` restores the old form. `watch::standing()` exposes an asking whose journal is current for a range; `Shadow.walked` records the ranges walked under that asking (bounded at 64) and `update_free` skips a second walk while it stands, counting `C::Skipped`/`SkippedBytes`; `VICTORY_SKIP_WALKED=0` restores the walk. Any guarded write, title remap, or our own write bumps the asking and forces the walk. The batched `union` asking is now default (`VICTORY_ASK_UNION=0` restores per-span asking).
- Semantics proven: exact ranges produce the same copied bytes per round as the span form while walking 4.56x fewer page stamps; a write or remap between walks is not skipped; overlapping aliases both receive a written page; plain pools and pagefile views with no standing asking still walk whole (`VICTORY_UNWATCHED_FRAMES` unchanged); empty/oversized range lists fall back to the covering span. `cargo test -p victory-gpu --lib --profile diag --features diagnostics --offline -- --test-threads=1`: **141 passed, 0 failed, 4 ignored**. `cargo build --profile diag -p victory --features diagnostics` built (`target\diag\victory.exe`, 8,841,728 B, SHA-256 `EE59E972DE8A809A8227EBBE680AA5F8E46A9BF6DFFC1819877F75919F6C9A64`), not launched.
- Measured offline: ranges bench on 58 copies / 174 MB heap / 39 submissions: one span **0.07 ms, 277 MB checked**; exact ranges **0.05 ms, 61 MB checked (4.56x)**; 232/232 repeated walks skipped. Union bench on the live 10.4 GB shape: mixed 34 watched + 24 views `full` **28.96 -> 22.97 ms (-5.99, 1.26x)**, all-views **34.13 -> 21.95 ms (-12.18, 1.56x)**, askings 975 -> 39 and 2262 -> 0; watch-only views 3.14x. Every arm copied the same bytes.
- Why not -30..-60 ms: `copy_stale` touches one sequential stamp per page at about 0.4 ns/page, so the 10.4 GB checked is under 1 ms; `writes` answers from the journal in about 0.5 us. The 69.0 ms copies split into 28.77 ms memcpy, 31.73 ms kernel asking, and about 8.5 ms bookkeeping. Only the kernel asking is materially reducible, and the union does that.
- Residual (modelled, not live): prepare **161.8 -> ~152-156 ms**, frame **447.2 -> ~439-443 ms**; about 94-96% of the baseline prepare remains. The 66.7 ms target is not reachable from CPU prep: GPU fence alone is 188.4 ms (2.8x the mark), and reaching ~120-130 ms would require the card-side detection plus ~900 MB more card memory.
- Decisive live A/B remaining (not run): one frame line at the same scene/cache with `VICTORY_ASK_UNION=0` versus `=1`; expect copies time to fall, identical copied bytes, `0 skipped` to become non-zero in the copies line, and the new `(.. MB of gaps)` field populated. Use one hardware instance, hardware D3D12, verify keyboard focus before every input; Enter activates the focused button and W/A/S/D move selection like Tab.
- Evidence: `.local\prepare-budget\REPORT.md` (13,697 B, SHA-256 `532408DE7E7D791B9E0DBD3E6678AC0A99D713D1896E9E1531DDFAF9C1D3D247`); patch `.local\prepare-budget\prepare-ranges.patch` (88,170 B, 5 files, +1,878/-29, SHA-256 `E641741BE99159364C3399F8D02C4F5F79BDA4193630D2B5B561BBC002DEB221`); worktree `.local\prepare-budget\src`; raw bench `.local\prepare-budget\bench-union.txt` (5,592 B, SHA-256 `BE2A39B2FDA04FBBC012D4907A5F46C1DE81F2F08DCBD08867D22B9ED9B1F1C4`); tests/bench `crates\victory-gpu\src\dirty_refresh_perf.rs`.

Write-watched pages are never freed (the watch journal keys by page number).

### Table/address marking feasibility - NO-GO as-is, conservative fallback; offline only (2026-11-25, worker)

- Instruction: stop DeepSeek visual/live testing; Claude owns desktop/visual work. This thread did no launch, input, GPU-slot use, or visual test. Offline analysis only in `C:\Meraki`; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` read-only; `python` explicit; no subagents.
- All-clear: no owned game/helper process and no live-slot lock (`.local\frame-timing-live\LIVE-SLOT.lock.json` absent). An unowned `victory.exe` PID 33328 from `.local\upstream-rorodev6\repo\target\diag\victory.exe` remains; it was not started or touched by this thread and must not be killed here.
- Decision: **do not enable table-backed or address-based marking as-is.** Address declarations have no upstream marking semantic: upstream layout is `gpu_writes(..., false)`, shader `memory_flat` never calls `mark_view_write`, and local numbered address copies use `raw_uav_at`, which clears the mark entry. Table declarations have an upstream precedent, but upstream marking requires `make_marked`/thread-local capture plus `set_mark(index, mark)`; local `descriptor/host.rs::make_at` unconditionally calls `set_mark(index, None)`, so changing `readable(..., true)` alone would still mark nothing.
- Newer-title handling is not lossless locally: `copy_back_marked(page, title_wrote)` keeps the whole page when the title wrote it, losing GPU bytes there; `copy_back_marks()` has no `withheld` argument and `watch.rs` lacks upstream `saved`/`arm`/`disarm`. Upstream's unarmed shortcut has the same all-or-nothing loss; only the armed saved-page merge preserves both.
- Quantification from existing logs (steady-play tail, last 30 non-zero per-frame lines): ON #1 **381.4 MB/frame** (381.4-382.8) in **378** ranges, 352.8 MB/frame in copies >=1 MiB, **0 marked pages / 0.0 MB written back / 0 kept**; ON #2 **380.7 MB/frame** (380.7-381.7) in **376** ranges, 352.8 MB/frame >=1 MiB, **0/0.0/0**. Undeclared remainder is 741.9 MB/frame in 17 steps. Actual eligible marked volume today is **0 B/frame**. The table-vs-address split is unavailable because the logs predate `shadow::marks_line`.
- Shader context: ON #1 293 marked entries (cs 273, ps 13, vs 7), ON #2 291 (cs 272, ps 12, vs 7); 289/293 and 287/291 marked shaders declare zero root UAVs; OFF #2 has 0. Consistent with table/address declarations and no marked copy in use.
- Offline prototype `merge_model.py` (4/4 tests pass) demonstrates current all-or-nothing loss, the unarmed-upstream loss, and the armed saved-page merge that preserves both bytes; it also shows a true table flag with no slot entry still yields no mark. This is supporting evidence, not a substitute for the Rust regressions.
- Conservative fallback retained: table/address declarations stay unmarked and keep snapshots/comparison. A future table path needs the upstream `make_marked`/`set_mark` capture, shader-table `marked=true`, `Tracked::saved`/`arm`/`disarm`, saved-page merge, `copy_back_marks(withheld)`, and withheld collection before any lossless claim. Address marking needs a separate design.
- Artifacts: `.local\table-mark-feasibility\FINDINGS.md`, `QUANTIFICATION.md`, `quantification.json`, `quantify_marks.py`, `merge_model.py`, `merge_model-test.log`; source reference `.local\marked-page-gameplay\src` (dirty worktree, no commit). No source files changed, no commit.


### GPU timestamp dispatch and texture tags for Claude (2026-11-25, offline)

Reports include counters for omissions and show the 24 heaviest retained variants. The patch adds no queue work, timestamp queries or fence waits.

### Series S black screen FIXED; real intro + 3D cutscene render (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- Root cause: in Series S mode the title presents **two display planes** (plane 0 = game picture, plane 1 = UI overlay; `PlaneParameters` stride 72 bytes). The host showed only plane 0, so every UI/menu/intro frame was black (the UI target had the correct picker pixels at 3840x2160; confirmed with `VICTORY_CAPTURE` + `VICTORY_DUMP_TARGETS`).
- Fix (upstream tree `.local\upstream-rorodev6\repo`; patch `.local\upstream-rorodev6\series-s-overlay-fix.patch`): `queue.rs` passes plane 1 to `Host::present`; `host.rs` gains an alpha-blended overlay pass (`OVERLAY` shader, blend SRC_ALPHA/INV_SRC_ALPHA, `record_pass(..., overlay)`) drawn over the swap-chain buffer after the plane-0 copy. Also `lower.rs`: an 8-dword table image descriptor read only as data now falls back to a 2D texture (two UI pixel shaders `7d0f14ce`, `6b6db9f1` translate and DXIL-validate; they were dropped before).
- Live (hardware, Series S, Xbox exe from `GTA_VI_FOR_PC_LEGAL`, progressed save, focus verified): picker, intro art and the 3D cutscene all render, no black screen.
- **Frame rate (measured, 3D prologue cutscene, 4,570 draws/frame): 2.4-3.4 FPS, ~300-410 ms/frame; card ~120 ms "on the GPU", prepare 70-84 ms, copy-back 66-78 ms.** Plain Series X mode showed 1.0 FPS in an early cutscene frame. Upstream's 7-9 FPS claim is not reproduced on this title copy/GPU. 15 FPS is not reached.
- Not verified: other titles' multi-plane HUD blending in gameplay (overlay alpha assumed straight); Series S shader warm-up was run without the console flag.


### Series S + upstream performance preset: first real speed-up, 7-8 FPS (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- Run: upstream-based build with the overlay/translator fixes, `--console series-s`, preset `VICTORY_RELAY=2 INDIRECT_WINDOW=1 LATE_WAIT=3 WIDE_BACK=1 CARD_BUFFERS_MIN_MB=1 CARD_BUFFERS_MB=1024 JOIN_COPIES=1 CARD_REHASH_RUNS=1 SHADER_LOOP_LIMIT=256`, native display mode, progressed save, Xbox exe from `GTA_VI_FOR_PC_LEGAL`, hardware D3D12 (RX 7800 XT), focus verified. Launcher: `tools\run_series_s.ps1`. Gameplay-load context (prologue cutscene), measured.
- **7.2-8.2 FPS, 121-138 ms/frame at ~4,600 draws/frame** (same scene without the preset: 2.4-3.4 FPS). Breakdown: preparing ~97 ms (copies 56 ms / 96 MB, textures 7 ms), GPU ~22 ms, copy-back ~74 ms; they partly overlap. Matches the upstream README's 7-9 FPS. 15 FPS (66.7 ms) not reached.
- `VICTORY_INPLACE=1` + `VICTORY_POOL_COMMIT=1`: no change (copies still 45-57 ms / ~97 MB per frame; 5.4-7.7 FPS). Not a lever here.
- Next: the copies cost ~0.57 ms/MB, far above memcpy speed, so detection/walk overhead rather than bandwidth dominates; target the dirty detection and the 74 ms copy-back (single submitting thread is the limit).

### DexNom/VIctory source review vs a803c87 (2026-11-25, offline)

- Scope: source-only comparison of https://github.com/DexNom/VIctory against Meraki gameplay lineage `a803c87`, cloned to `.local\dexnom-review\repo` (clean). No build, no test run, no live/desktop session, no GPU load, no visual check, no subagents, no merge, no push. `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` untouched. Pins in `.local\dexnom-review\PINS.md`, full report in `.local\dexnom-review\REPORT.md`.
- Pinned: `main` `2e01ab4` (test-only tail `4775e8a`+`2e01ab4`; functional head `b8ff22e`), backup `921d8d3`, `v0.1.0` `8cca4d2`, `bc3cd4b` (writeback ownership), `5f4c3df` (fill workers/preludes), `570a941` (170-file import), `ed0dbfd` (busy-range title writes), `cd94d35`/`940d32f` (view marks), common base `ffe310e`. `a803c87` is not an ancestor of `2e01ab4`; the lineages are separate, so every item is a port, not a cherry-pick.
- **No 15 FPS result is demonstrated by anyone.** DexNom's best claimed gameplay figure is 3.52 -> 4.05 FPS indoor City (one new-build run). Meraki's settled hardware gameplay session is 462.8 ms p50 = 2.16 FPS, with card busy 191.9 ms = 2.88x the whole 15 FPS budget. Nothing here closes that gap alone.
- Tree delta `a803c87..2e01ab4`: 93 files, +20,107/-1,907. Largest `descriptor/host.rs` +1,988, `draw.rs` +1,612, `host.rs` +1,422, new `material_demand.rs` +405.
- Defaults delta: card min **16 MiB -> 1 MiB**, max **512 -> 1024 MB**; `relay` opt-in -> **on by default** (stage 2); `wide_back` opt-in -> **on by default**; `wide_refresh` still off; `late_stage` stage 3 under relay. Prove relay before touching it: largest async/remap surface in the diff.
- **Two corrections to earlier assumptions.** (1) `joined_piece_runs()` and `rehash_runs()` **already exist in a803c87** and are off by default in both trees â€” this is a flag to enable and measure, not a port. (2) `scratch_pool()` is already in a803c87, so the DexNom per-list scratch pool is not an adoption candidate; Meraki's 8-slot fence-safe upload ring has no DexNom equivalent.
- Card path: the substantive change is **granularity**, not gate names. `2e01ab4` hashes 64 words per 64 KiB piece (4 lanes per 4 KiB page) and fetches only dirty pages; a803c87 hashes once per piece and invalidates whole pieces. Hash storage cost ~1.56% of carded bytes (~14 MB at 900 MB). Measured gate context: Meraki cards 10 copies / 492 MB of 512 with ~2.7 GB VRAM headroom, and 352.8 MB/frame sits in copies >=1 MiB with snapshots 73.0 ms + finding 46.0 ms. Gate fit is plausible; it cannot reach 66.7 ms alone and must ship with coalescing (~594 hash passes vs 34 compare passes; bench 1.55 ms packed vs 10.03 ms scattered, with 12 dispatches carrying ~9.1 of the 10.0 ms).
- **View marks are absent from `2e01ab4`.** `git log -S 'fn update_busy'` shows exactly `ed0dbfd` (adds) and `570a941` (removes by merge side). Marks exist only pre-import; Meraki's port is already measured inert (0 marked pages / 0 kept), so DexNom main is not evidence for marks. Table/address marking remains NO-GO as-is.
- **New correctness finding: the `ed0dbfd` defect is live in Meraki.** `Shadow::update` still skips title writes under ranges an earlier list declared as written, in *both* `a803c87` and `2e01ab4`. Upstream saw a dispatch read sizes as zeros and loop until device removal on an RTX 4050. The fix is only at `940d32f` and depends on `Tracked`, which a803c87 does not have, so it is a design-level port against Meraki's `pending`/snapshot structure using the upstream test as acceptance â€” not a cherry-pick.
- `bc3cd4b` writeback ownership (`writeback.rs`, 321 lines, unchanged in `2e01ab4`) is the strongest scene-agnostic correctness option: `Changes`/`Owners`/`needs_new_snapshot`/`publish()` withhold bytes whose backing was remapped after a flight started, with four upstream tests. Port the ownership guard subset, not the RT/page-table work.
- `5f4c3df` is a mixed 20+ file commit (texture streaming, sparse textures, shadow pipelines, fill batching, shader emitters) and is not separable. Fill batching targets the same 30.8-37.0 ms as Meraki's existing page-fill projection (15-32 ms), so implement one, not both. Parallel preludes are 3.9-70.4 us here and not worth adopting.
- License/deps: GPL-3.0-or-later both sides; `Cargo.toml` identical `a803c87` vs `2e01ab4` (only `Cargo.lock` +21 lines); no new crate needed for any option; toolchain Rust 1.98.1 / edition 2024 / Windows x64. `570a941` untracks `bcpack-tables.bin` and downloads it hash-verified (SHA-256 `1F1D4760...C36B`), documented as separately supplied non-GPL data. No proprietary assets, no runtime dependency on `C:\Leonida`/`D:\`.
- Ranked for FPS: (1) card gate 1 MiB/900-1024 MB **with** `VICTORY_CARD_REHASH_RUNS=1`; (2) 16-pages-per-piece compare + dirty-page fetch to cut copy-back/merge bytes; (3) page fills *or* fill batching; (4) wide back/refresh defaults only if snapshot CPU still dominates; (5) skip parallel preludes and relay defaults.
- Ranked for correctness: (1) `ed0dbfd` busy-range title writes (defect live here); (2) `bc3cd4b` ownership guard; (3) `5f4c3df` streaming/shadow pipelines only for a named visual defect; (4) RT material demand only if RT is in scope.
- Handoff is written for Claude: baseline, coalescing-only, gate-only, granularity, fill path, then the two correctness ports, each with its own A/B. No commit, no ntfy, no live run.


### CPU sampling of the 7-8 FPS Series S run: flat, wait-bound profile (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- Tool: `tools\cpu_sample.py` (out-of-process thread sampler, no admin, no rebuild; dbghelp symbols from `.local\upstream-rorodev6\repo\target\diag\victory.pdb`). Run on the live prologue cutscene (Series S + preset, ~6.5-7 FPS while sampled), 30 s at 100 Hz over ~105 threads. Raw: `.local\cpu-sample\run5\{samples.csv,top.txt}`.
- Result: ~96% of samples are idle waits (`ntdll`/`win32u`). The ~8 busiest threads are only 21-34% busy each. Non-idle time is flat: the title's own code (`victory-image-*.dll`), driver shader compile (`amdxc64`, still compiling pipelines), then many small runtime functions (`draw::State::bind`, `shadow::lookup`, `descriptor::view_at`, `HostHeap::mirror`, mutex `lock_contended`, blake3 card-copy hashing). No single function dominates.
- Reading: the 130 ms frame is a chain of waits across threads (prepare -> GPU -> copy-back), not one hot loop; shaving one function will not reach 66.7 ms. Gains need removing serialization (overlap prepare with copy-back, fewer submissions/fences), which earlier reports bound at ~5 FPS for the old build and which the preset has partly done.
- 15 FPS not reached.

### Upload pool: opt-in fence-safe bounded reuse, CPU-tested; live attribution gated (2026-11-25, offline worker)

- Scope: offline R&D only in `C:\Meraki`. No game launch, input, screenshots, GPU/desktop work, or subagents; `D:\` and `C:\Leonida` untouched; no ntfy. `python` explicit. The patch lives in a dedicated a803c87 worktree; `baseline/` was not touched.
- Reviewed the probe (`.local/upload-pool-probe/REPORT.md`: ACCEPT on 64 x 10.671875 MiB, WC 699.1 -> 101.4 MiB, NON_LOCAL 683.0 -> 85.4 MiB, parity over 716,177,408 B) and the production sites: scratch chunks, `HostList::upload` (called from `list.rs:969`), oversized scratch, texture staging, and the title-facing `allocate_upload`.
- Measured from session 20261125-021128-hw (2.0 FPS, 502.7 ms/frame): `MADE` 45,162 upload buffers / 19,551 MB by 09:17:47 (from 4 / 9 MB at 09:11:41); scratch 188 chunks / 137 MB alive with 92 spare / 82 MB and 40,337 spare takes; list uploads 3 alive / 0 MB at all three samples; staging 190 alive / 78 MB, 381 made, 191 let go / 117 MB.
- Inference, not a trace: removing staging leaves 44,781 buffers / 19,356 MB, which fits about 42,600 x 256 KiB plus 2,200 x 4 MiB. So the 256 KiB first-chunk class is about 95% of creations by count while bytes split about 55/45. The UMA audit's "sizes, not owners" gap is now instrumented.
- Implemented `upload_pool.rs`: a GPU-free bounded pool (`Fit::Exact` / `Fit::AtLeast`, per-size count cap, byte cap, statistics) behind two default-off switches. `VICTORY_UPLOAD_POOL=1` pools list uploads (8 per size, 64 MiB, best-fit). `VICTORY_CHUNK_POOL=1` keeps chunks in 32 x 256 KiB plus 8 x 4 MiB classes (40 MiB, below the replaced 128 MiB cap). Releases happen only in `Deferred::drop`, at reset after the submission wait or at the list drop, so no in-flight buffer is reused.
- Added `MADE_BY_SITE` and a diagnostics line `upload buffers made by site: scratch, lists, staging, other`, plus an `upload reuse` line when a switch is on. The default path is unchanged.
- Verified offline: 131 tests pass with diagnostics (12 new), 124 without; clippy exit 0; `cargo check --workspace --features victory-gpu/diagnostics` exit 0. The CPU test `the_probe_geometry_keeps_eight_slots` reproduces the probe's 85.375 MiB peak with 120 takes after 8 creations.
- Blocker: no live run, so there is no WC, private-commit, or frame-time claim. If the new site split shows `lists` is not the creator, the list-upload pool is inert; if `scratch` dominates, `VICTORY_CHUNK_POOL=1` is the first switch to evaluate. Both are off by default.
- Artifacts: `.local/upload-pool-integration/REPORT.md`, `HANDOFF.md`, `upload-pool.patch` (SHA-256 `946A1F8F93682D87BA0909EAB5D8CBA623ADE600FB7C682BEC76521CDAF1964B`), worktree `.local/upload-pool-integration/src`, and the test/clippy/check logs in that directory.

### Bounded indirect clamp: second global UAV wait removed (2026-11-25, offline R&D)

- Landed an isolated 3-file patch (`indirect.rs`, `host.rs`, `copystat.rs`) on branch `perf/clamp-sync` (commit `93b16f7`, base `a803c87`) in `.local\synchronization-rd`. Each bounded indirect command now records one global UAV barrier instead of two: the wait after the dispatch was redundant because the `UAV -> INDIRECT_ARGUMENT` transition already orders that write for `ExecuteIndirect`. Dispatch count, budget `InterlockedAdd` order, draws and the `done` return transition are unchanged.
- **Measured (log counts):** 66 per-frame lines in `frame-timing-live` session `20261125-021128-hw`, 52 nonzero, 29 in the 5,093-5,134 band, median 5,106.5 clamps/frame; the older gameplay session shows 5,167-5,246. So this path is exercised in the stable tutorial scene (no alternative branch needed), and the patch removes about 5,100 global UAV barriers a frame (10,186-10,268 to 5,093-5,134). **Estimated/unmeasured:** milliseconds - the clamp records straight to the command list, so no per-call timing exists. No timing claim is made.
- Verified offline only: `cargo check` default and `diagnostics` clean; `cargo test -p victory-gpu --lib` 118 passed / 1 ignored (base 112 / 1), with `diagnostics` 125 / 1. Six new device-free mock tests pin wait count and position, timing mode, transition targets, alias refusal, and the return to `COMMON`. `clamp-sync.patch` passes `git apply --check` on a clean `a803c87` and its tests pass in a throwaway worktree that had only the patch applied. One early parallel test run died with `0xC0000005` inside WARP tests; baseline and later parallel/serial runs passed, so it is unassigned, not attributed to the patch.
- **Blocked bigger win, documented:** narrowing the remaining wait to a resource-scoped UAV barrier needs `D3D12_RESOURCE_FLAG_ALLOW_UNORDERED_ACCESS` on `src`/`count`/`budget`; D3D12 fails the whole list at `Close` (`E_INVALIDARG`) otherwise, and the project itself refuses non-flagged title buffers as UAVs (`memory.rs:150-153`, `host.rs:1724-1727`). Unblock with one diagnostics run that logs `GetDesc().Flags` for those three buffers.
- Claude handoff with the live A/B plan (hardware rendering, one instance, focus before input, Enter activates / W/A/S/D move selection, draw-loss and budget-drop gates) is `.local\synchronization-rd\HANDOFF.md`; evidence in that folder's `REPORT.md` and `logs\`. No game process, desktop session, or capture was started; the only device work was the crate's own unit suite, which the regression run requires.


### Skipping refused write-watch calls on known view memory: no measurable effect, reverted (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- Change tried in `watch.rs::writes`: do not ask `GetWriteWatch` for a range whose first block the guards already hold (view memory, kernel always refuses). Built and run on the preset (Series S, prologue cutscene): 5.9-6.9 FPS, 144-169 ms/frame, within the run-to-run spread of the baseline (7.2-8.2 earlier; scene content varies 1,400-3,500 draws). The refused-call counter stayed at 3,312/frame (10 ms): the refusals mostly come from another call site, not this one, so the change did not touch them.
- Reverted; `series-s-overlay-fix.patch` regenerated (overlay + descriptor-fallback changes only).
- Reading: the submit-side cost is many 5-10 % items (asking ~26 ms, guards ~19 ms, stale copies 17-29 ms, refused asks ~8-10 ms, textures 7-13 ms) plus GPU ~25 ms; single-function fixes are below the measurement noise. A 2x gain needs a different architecture for view-memory tracking (replacing per-block guard scans) and fewer submissions, not tuning.
- Best verified: 7.2-8.2 FPS (preset). 15 FPS not reached.


### Upstream reality check: the base fork's own ceiling is 10-12 FPS (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- `shadowdog00/vi-pc-combo` (the Series S base this build uses; README saved at `.local\upstream-rorodev6\shadowdog-README.md`) reports **10-12 FPS peak, ~10 FPS average (~100 ms/frame)** in small-console mode on an RTX 5060 Ti, after weeks of work, and its own estimate is "30 fps ... months; nobody public is near it". Its list of dead ends matches mine: `VICTORY_INPLACE` 2-4x slower, `VICTORY_WIDE_REFRESH` no gain (Windows calls wait on each other in the kernel), a faster card gives the same frame time, more shader pre-compilation no gain.
- Its remaining levers are structural (merged indirect draws with real occlusion queries, lazy copy-back and cheap write tracking, translator work for per-draw descriptor tables, host DXR for ray tracing): "weeks" each.
- My 7-8 FPS on this PC/title copy is in the same regime. 15 FPS with good geometry/shaders is not reachable by configuration or small patches; it needs the structural items above.

### Textures/shaders pass 1: translator failures 106 -> 71; pipeline precompile for load stalls (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- User report (live play): moving into new areas freezes for a long time (0 fps) while new objects load. Cause: pipelines compile on first draw (`dropped N without a host pipeline (M being made)`, 500 ms/frame wait budget). Fix: `victory precompile` (jobs 6) with the game's switches builds the pipelines listed by earlier runs ahead of time; measured rate ~200 pipelines/s for drawn/compute, slower for mesh. Drawn set (2,055) was already in the library; full set 83,569 pipelines from 19,108 shaders is being built into `state\cache\pipelines\host-pipelines.bin` (13-38 not translatable). Re-measure stalls after it finishes.
- Shader translator (warm over all 25,758 shaders, `warm2.log`): refusals 106 -> **71** after the 8-dword descriptor fallback (`lower.rs`): all 35 pixel "descriptor read only as data" shaders translate. Remaining 71: 37 pixel + 3 mesh "descriptor chosen by control flow", 10 pixel "hardware SGPR input", 8 pixel "first half of an interpolation", 5+1 compute `v_cvt_f32_f64` / `v_permlanex16_b32` (no f64 support in the emitter), 5 compute + 1 "descriptor read only as data (non-image)", 1 "opaque lane mask".
- Next: debug the 40 "chosen by control flow" shaders (`VICTORY_PHI_DEBUG`), then SGPR input and interpolation, then f64.


### Textures/shaders pass 2: 53 more shaders translate; biggest remaining gap found - ~40% of draws are dropped (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- Translator fixes (`.local\upstream-rorodev6\series-s-overlay-fix.patch`, 4 files): lone first-half interpolations that something else reads are evaluated as P0 + i P10 (`io.rs`; 8 pixel shaders, DXIL-validated); pixel programs reading unset SGPRs s17-s105 read zero and `SRC_POPS_EXITING_WAVE_ID` (operand 239) reads zero (`lower.rs`; 10 pixel shaders emit); 8-dword descriptor fallback (35 pixel shaders, earlier). Warm refusals 106 -> ~53 expected (full recount pending). Regression pass over all 25,953 saved programs: 14,211/14,259 vertex+pixel, 2,722/2,734 compute, 8,762/8,765 mesh emit; 271 pixel shaders and ~105 mesh shaders still fail `dxil.dll` validation (packed-row interpolation mode mismatch; mesh output size > 32 KB) - the runtime does not validate, effect on the picture unknown.
- Pipelines: `victory precompile` built 82,530 of 83,569 listed pipelines (library 10 MB) to remove first-draw compile stalls; re-check live.
- **Largest geometry gap (measured, live, cutscene): 2,450-3,100 draws per frame are dropped "without a host pipeline (0 being made)" next to 1,100-4,100 drawn, i.e. roughly 40% of all draws.** Cause: the title creates ~9,000+ pipelines through two D3D12.X device methods the runtime has not written (`unknown_pipeline` in `device.rs`, slots 0x1c8: >=8,192 calls, and 0x218: >=1,024). The description is a root-signature object pointer followed by (pointer, size) pairs: three contiguous small blobs (0x184, 0xbc, 0xd0/0xb0 bytes) and two separate 0x200-byte blocks, i.e. native shader programs plus register-state blocks, not DXIL containers. Implementing them means decoding that console-specific format into D3D12 pipeline state: a reverse-engineering task of its own, and it is probably the main reason for missing walls/objects.
- Measured in the same run: 4.8-7.4 FPS, 135-208 ms/frame. 15 FPS not reached; fixing the dropped draws will add draw load before any speed-up.


### CPU-efficiency pass (goal: feed the GPU faster): measurements, no frame-rate gain yet (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- Observations (live, Series S + preset, prologue cutscene): Task Manager GPU 0 = 2% (45 C), total CPU 22-45%, RAM 88%; per-thread CPU over 10 s: 4.8 cores busy of 16, busiest thread 73%, the next eight 24-38%. The game process holds 12.8 GB dedicated + 6.9 GB shared video memory on the RX 7800 XT (adapter 0x10b23), so it is on the hardware GPU; the card is simply idle (CPU/latency-bound pipeline), not unused by design.
- Where a ~125 ms frame goes (runtime counters, steady state): submit thread prepares ~97 ms (copies 47-59 ms for 100-120 MB in 754 ranges, textures 8 ms), waits 21 ms for the card; the copy-back thread lands 74 ms but is mostly waiting (27 ms for the card, 36 ms for fetches of card-copy pieces, only ~11 ms of work). Write detection per frame: ~5,000 refused `GetWriteWatch` calls (9-12 ms) + guards 13-23 ms + 4,300 journal asks + stale-copy copying 17-31 ms. CPU samples across the title's recording threads show `shadow::lookup`, `State::bind`, `HostHeap::mirror`, B-tree searches and `Mutex::lock_contended`, i.e. shared-lock contention, each thread only ~30% busy.
- Tried, no measurable gain (all within the 7-8 FPS run-to-run spread): timer resolution (already `timeBeginPeriod(1)` + power-throttling opt-out in `runtime.rs`); `VICTORY_ASK_ONCE=1`; `VICTORY_WIDE_REFRESH/MERGE=1`; replacing the guard code's ordered clean-block set with a paged bitmap (unit-tested; guards stayed 13-23 ms because the cost is call volume, not the scan; re-guarding `VirtualProtect` is only ~3.7 ms/frame at 16 us x ~230 calls); skipping refused asks on known view memory. All reverted.
- Stability note: one run (`logs\run23`) ended with `DXGI_ERROR_DEVICE_HUNG` (0x887A0006, "invalid command") after the frame rate fell to ~3 FPS; two earlier runs of the same build ran 10+ minutes clean. Upstream lists `VICTORY_INDIRECT_WINDOW=1` as the first thing to remove if the GPU is lost. Not yet root-caused.
- Next concrete lever: cut the number of per-frame asks (5,000 refused + 4,300 journal) by memoising per-copy answers inside a submission, and remove the shared-lock contention in `shadow::lookup` (per-thread cache of recent lookups, invalidated by a generation counter bumped on register/unregister/place changes). Both need care for correctness; expected gain is tens of ms of the 97 ms prepare, not yet measured.


### Per-thread buffer-lookup cache: no gain, reverted (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- Change: `shadow::lookup` answers recent asks from a 16-entry per-thread cache of weak references, invalidated by a generation counter (bumped on register/unregister/in-place let-go/idle sweep), refreshing `used` on a hit; 137/137 `victory-gpu` unit tests passed. Live (Series S + preset, same cutscene): 7.2-8.0 FPS, prepare 86-118 ms, copies 48-64 ms - identical to the unpatched runs. Recording-thread lock contention is not what gates the frame. Reverted.
- Conclusion for this goal: the frame is gated by the submit thread's ~5,000 write-detection asks + ~100 MB of copying per frame and by the copy-back wait chain, not by lookups, guards, timers, priority, or recording-thread locks. All cheap, safe changes have been measured and exhausted; a real gain needs a different change-tracking design (e.g. marking written pages on the GPU/shaders so most asks disappear), which is a multi-day change.


### Skip refused write-watch asks on known view memory: kept (CPU work down ~9.5 ms/frame, FPS unchanged) (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- `watch.rs`: `known_view(first_page)` (the guards already hold the first block, read-only or absent: view memory the kernel never write-watches) makes `ask_kernel` and `writes` skip the `GetWriteWatch` call and take the guard path directly; `VICTORY_WATCH_ASK_VIEWS=1` restores the old behaviour. Both call sites covered (an earlier attempt covered only `writes`).
- Measured (live, Series S + preset, prologue cutscene, same counters line): refused `GetWriteWatch` calls **5,005-6,113 -> 354-403 per frame**, their time **9-12 ms -> 0.55 ms**; answered calls unchanged (254 per frame). Frame rate 7.3-8.3 FPS, 120-155 ms/frame, i.e. inside the unpatched 7-8 FPS spread: the saved time is summed over threads that mostly wait on each other, so it does not shorten the critical path by itself. `victory-gpu` unit tests: see run below; no hang or device loss in this run.
- Still unchanged and now the largest write-detection items: `guards` 19 ms/frame (call volume of `guard()` incl. `is_view` queries for ranges not yet known) and refresh "asking" 23.6 ms/frame.


### Per-range copy ranking: no dominant range (2026-11-25, Claude lead)

- From the runtime's own `copied from` dump (live, Series S + preset): the costliest ranges are the 174 MB heap `0x32910000+0xae00000` (12.6 refreshes/frame, 5.5 ms), `0x960710000+0x2d00000` (5.6x, 5.2 ms), `0xac6160000+0x3200000` (6.1x, 3.7 ms), `0xac9360000+0x1900000` (4.1x, 1.1 ms); everything else is <0.4 ms. The top four total ~15 ms of the 47-60 ms/frame "copies": the cost is spread over hundreds of small ranges and ~9,000 asks, so no single-range fix exists. Confirms that a gain needs a different change-tracking design, not a targeted fix.

### AvatarManz RX 7900 XTX fork review and local memory limit (2026-11-25)
