The author's README reports 13â€“20 FPS indoors, 19â€“22 FPS during the first boat mission, and 6â€“10 FPS in a crowded chase, on a 24 GB RX 7900 XTX with 64 GB system RAM, at 1440p and using the Series X profile. Their 40â€“46 GB number is private process memory. They also report exterior flicker, delayed textures and darker rooms with the performance preset's ray tracing off.
- The published tests report larger gains from avoiding whole-heap descriptor processing, preparing submissions on a dedicated thread, and letting dependent GPU work stay on the GPU. Thin command lists add an eight-worker recording path. Across all 32 matched standard-release windows, CPU reads of title memory waited 12.9 ms/frame median. Fill, copy and texture waits occurred 1.0, 0.55 and 0.0 times per frame respectively. The time is for all three causes, so it is an upper bound for a fill-only port. Start with constant-buffer fills whose descriptor slot has a single owner. The port needs the single host copy that owns the source, a GPU copy ordered before its consumers, and fallback for aliases and CPU readers. Texture readback is separately relevant to boat physics. The fork attributes about 9 GB of private memory to list allocators, 7 GB to address windows and 4 GB to uploads.
- Ran the isolated 1080p display / Series X arm using hardware, a copied save, the leftmost monitor and reduced in-flight and submission-join limits. It stopped in loading, before input, at 998 MiB free Windows commit with 15,395 MiB physical RAM available and 9,669 MiB process-private memory. The guard closed the owned process. No gameplay FPS was measured. This PC has 31.9 GiB installed RAM and the idle commit limit immediately afterward was 44.4 GiB. Evidence: .local/avatarmanz-hardware/20261125-172204/memory.csv and exit.txt. Sent the measured blocker through ntfy after a dry run.
- The fork's nine game-free card-step tests passed on this PC. The ordered read-after-write test matched the CPU reference and executed all 48 copies on the GPU with zero submission waits. A separate unwatched-memory test passed with 44 GPU copies and nine settles for title writes under earlier GPU writes, exercising fallback. Evidence: .local/avatarmanz-review/card-steps-tests.log and card-steps-unwatched.log. This verifies the fork's copy semantics on our hardware. The Meraki port and its live performance remain unverified.

### GPU constant-buffer fills (2026-11-25)

- Implemented the fill half of the AvatarManz queue-copy approach in the Series S renderer as `VICTORY_GPU_CONSTANT_FILLS=1`. A small table constant buffer keeps its original binding address for GPU copies and CPU fallback. The planner requires one card-resident host copy that covers the source, declines aliases and overlapping CPU writes, preserves other submission waits, and brings newer CPU bytes up to date through the existing write-watch path. Scratch is bounded at 64 MiB and recycled only after the owning list has finished.
- The diagnostics build passed. The targeted hardware test copied 480 bytes from an earlier queued write byte-for-byte, verified alias and pending CPU-write rejection, and emitted no D3D12 debug errors. The full GPU crate suite passed with 140 tests and 2 ignored. The separate hardware run explicitly enabled the new ignored regression test. Logs: gpu-constant-fills-build.log, gpu-constant-fills-tests.log, and gpu-constant-fills-suite.log in .local/upstream-rorodev6/logs.

### GPU constant fills measured; virtualization theory tested and rejected (2026-11-26, Claude lead)

- `VICTORY_GPU_CONSTANT_FILLS=1` on the release build (page-fetch + pack-fetch on, Series S + preset, prologue cutscene, hardware, focus verified): it fires ~3 times per frame for ~1.4 KB, so it changes nothing measurable; steady windows 7.6-9.2 FPS, 109-132 ms/frame, copies 44-58 ms, copy-back 33-78 ms. Logs `.local\upstream-rorodev6\logs\run41`.
- Hypothesis "VBS/HVCI (Memory integrity is running, hypervisor present because of VMware) slows the memory-protection and cross-memory calls the runtime relies on": **rejected** by `tools\syscall_bench.py` on this PC: VirtualProtect 1.7 us, RO+RW toggle 3.5 us, ReadProcessMemory 64 KB 4.6 us / 4 KB 2.1 us, GetWriteWatch 3.1 us - normal bare-metal values. Turning Memory integrity off is not expected to help; not recommended.
- Consistent with the earlier counters: the frame cost is the number of calls (~9,000 asks, ~750 ranges, ~100 MB) and the dependency chain between threads, not slow individual calls.


### Submit-thread profile with kernel calls resolved (2026-11-26, Claude lead)

- `tools\cpu_sample.py` now names system-call functions (ntdll/win32u exports) so kernel time is no longer lumped as "waiting". Live, Series S + page/pack fetch, prologue cutscene, 20 s at 100 Hz: the submitting thread (tid 27900) is **84% active**, and its time is mostly kernel memory calls: `ZwReadVirtualMemory` 37% of its active samples (copying dirty title pages into card copies), `NtQueryVirtualMemory` 17%, `NtGetWriteWatch` 5%, `NtProtectVirtualMemory` 3%, blake3 hashing 5%. The other busy threads (title recording threads, ~35% active) run `State::bind`, `shadow::lookup`, descriptor mirroring and the driver compiler `amdxc64` (`GetSettingsBlobsAll`).
- VirtualQuery call sites measured (temporary counters, run43): `uncommitted_end` ~6.7 million calls over the run (~8,000 per frame, ~0.5 us each, ~4-5 ms/frame), `is_view` ~65,000 calls, `writable` ~67,000 calls: ~4-5 ms per frame in these three, less than the sampler's ~12 ms for NtQueryVirtualMemory, so other sites also contribute. Cause of the 8,000/frame: every `guard()` call re-queries each uncommitted run it meets because ordinary title commits are not notified; caching the answers would risk skipping memory the title has just committed (stale zeros), so no change made.
- Kernel-call costs measured standalone are normal (1-5 us), so the submit thread's 84% is call volume: ~100 MB of reads in many ranges plus ~9,000 asks per frame. This is the ceiling for incremental fixes; cutting it needs fewer, larger operations (e.g. GPU-side change marks) rather than cheaper calls.
- Instrumentation removed again; the tree is as before.


### Ask-once now covers guarded view memory: CPU cost down, FPS unchanged (2026-11-26, Claude lead)

- Finding: `VICTORY_ASK_ONCE` is on by default (since run 81), so my earlier "ASK_ONCE=1" test was a no-op; it only covered write-watched memory. View memory (guarded blocks) fell through to a per-flush `writes()` -> `guard()` for each range (about 5,000 per frame).
- Change (`watch.rs`, `VICTORY_ASK_VIEWS=0` turns it off): `ask_kernel(.., views)` - for a range asked alone from `ask_once` whose first block the guards already hold, the guards are asked once for the submission (`guard()`), the written pages go into the journal under the ahead asking, and the flushes after it are answered from the journal. Merged runs (`ask_run`) never take this path, because a union can span memory between pieces that must not be guarded. Together with the earlier known-view skip of refused `GetWriteWatch`.
- Measured (live, Series S + preset + page/pack fetch, prologue cutscene, diagnostics build): guards **19 -> 2.2 ms/frame**, refresh asking **24 -> 5.2 ms**, refused `GetWriteWatch` **5,000 -> 320**, submit-side copies **52 -> 33 ms/frame**, preparing 100 -> ~89 ms in the matched 4,600-draw windows; frame 110-118 ms = **8.5-9.1 FPS**, same range as before (best windows 9.1 FPS). `victory-gpu` unit tests 140 passed / 2 ignored. No hang or device loss in the run.
- Reading: ~20 ms of submit-thread CPU removed, ~5-10 ms of it visible in `preparing`; the frame is now bounded by what is left: ~33 ms copies (reads of dirty pages through the kernel), 7-9 ms textures, ~49 ms other preparation work, and the copy-back thread's 63 ms chain.


### Heap-buffer pass: fast hash kept, rolling rescan rejected (2026-11-26, Claude lead)

- The per-frame "heap buffers" pass (`draw.rs::heap_buffers`, shaders that pick buffers from the view heap by index) costs 19-35 ms of submit-thread time every frame (one pass a frame). Per-frame phases (diagnostics log "heap pass per frame"): descriptors ~5 ms on normal frames and ~22 ms averaged over the ~8 frames around each full rescan (all ~83,000 slots are re-read every 16th pass, ~145 ms on that single frame: a periodic hitch and ~9 ms/frame on average), spans ~7 ms, buffer refresh ~1.4 ms, textures ~4 ms.
- Kept: a fast multiply-rotate hasher (`FastBuild`) for the pass's `ranges` and `spans` maps (SipHash was the cost of building them): spans phase 7 -> 4.5 ms/frame in the same scene; behaviour unchanged. `victory-gpu` tests 140 passed.
- Rejected after measuring: spreading the full rescan over 16 passes (a sixteenth of the slots a pass). The descriptors phase rose to 21-31 ms *every* frame (worse than the old ~13 ms average) even with the slice taken while walking the set without a full copy, so the per-slot cost of the slice is higher than the full pass's; removed. Left as an open item: `VICTORY_HEAP_*` interval of 16 passes could be raised (every 64 passes would cut the average by ~7 ms and the hitch frequency by 4x) at the risk of views of released resources staying stale longer; not done without a visual check.
- Frame rate with all kept changes (ask-once for views + known-view skip + fast hash), cutscene, diagnostics build: 8.6-8.9 FPS steady (110-116 ms/frame), within the earlier 8-9 FPS range.

- **Full-rescan interval 16 -> 64 passes (`VICTORY_HEAP_FULL_EVERY`, default now 64): kept.** Live cutscene (diagnostics build, preset + page/pack fetch): descriptors phase steady ~6 ms (was 5 ms normal + ~22 ms averaged around each full rescan), whole heap pass 15-19 ms (was 20-35), preparing 88-93 ms, frames 109-114 ms = **8.7-9.2 FPS**; picture checked on the prison-window shot (`.local\upstream-rorodev6\logs\run48`, screenshot taken at 8.9 FPS): fully lit, characters, inmates, tower and window all present, no stale or garbage views. Risk accepted: a view of a released resource can stay stale up to 64 frames; set `VICTORY_HEAP_FULL_EVERY=16` to restore the old behaviour. Tests: 140 passed.


### Missing walls / flicker: cause found, two attempted fixes (2026-11-26, Claude lead)

- User report (live): interiors have no walls, geometry flickers, hair/clothes dither. Reproduced with all of this session's CPU changes turned off (cell scene: posters, locker and a lamp floating over an empty horizon), so it is not caused by them.
- Cause (runtime log, every run): the title lights/builds its interiors through its own ray-tracing compute (probes, bounce, shadows); with the structure builder off the host drops ~300-890 draws/frame ("a stage of the ray-tracing structure builder, which is off") and ~600-730 draws/frame ("shades ray-tracing hits, left out"). Upstream's own README lists "Ray tracing: off (its structure builder is not emulated)", dark interiors and missing walls/posters.
- `VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=1` (runs all 53 builder pipelines): the **title crashed** (WER crash report, access violation inside the title image) about a minute after the world loaded (`logs\run60`). `docs/fork-ray-tracing.md` says the builder has never run end to end. Not usable here.
- `rtDynamicShadows=false` in a copy of the save settings (`state\data-rtoff`): the title still issues the same builder and ray-hit passes (888 and 733 draws/frame dropped, `logs\run61`), so the toggle does not remove them. 8.8 FPS at 1,720 draws in that scene.
- Next candidates: find which builder stage or traversal program makes the title crash with the builder on (access violation in title code after the builder writes), or give the dropped hit-shading passes a non-RT fallback so surfaces still draw. Both are translator/runtime work.


### Ray-tracing builder crash investigation (2026-11-26, Claude lead)

- Crash (builder on, `VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=1`, run60): first-chance/unhandled `0xc0000005` in the title's CPU code at `victory-image+0x4f38ba5`, reading `0xFFFFFFFFFFFFFFFF`. Disassembled (capstone, `.local\pytools\x86disasm.py`): it is a reference-count release (`mov rsi,[rcx+0x18]; lock dec dword [rcx+0x10]`) of an object whose pointer `[rcx+0x10]` holds -1; `rcx = 0xff800000ff800000` (two packed float -inf, the empty-AABB placeholder). Conclusion: a title object in memory was overwritten by builder/bounds data (stores landing in the wrong title memory), not a GPU fault. Later crashes in the same log are follow-ons of the corrupted heap.
- Why the builder runs so badly here: with it on the ~1,000 ray-hit compute programs (excluded from precompile before) are bound at the world's first frame and compile in-game at 5-9 s each (run62: 5-14 s/frame, 74-86 pipelines in flight). `victory precompile` with the builder switch on built them all (83,557 pipelines ready, 0 left out, 13 min).
- With everything precompiled and `VICTORY_BVH_BUILDER=1 VICTORY_BVH_QUARANTINE=1` (run63): 0 draws dropped, 0 indirect (the missing-wall passes now run), no crash in the first minute, but **0.2-0.3 FPS (3.4-4.4 s/frame)**: GPU 1.3-1.9 s/frame, preparing ~2 s (651 MB of copies/frame). The emulated ray tracing is ~25-40x too slow to use; not viable for speed even if stable.
- Whether the quarantine removes the crash is not established (run closed deliberately at 0.2 FPS, unusable). Next: (a) quarantine long run only if a faster build of the builder exists; (b) give the dropped hit-shading/builder passes a cheap non-RT fallback so walls draw at normal speed.

## Integer render-target blend fix (2026-11-25)
- Cause found for a refused mesh pipeline: a G-buffer pass blended into an R8_UINT target. D3D12 rejects this (E_INVALIDARG); the console ignores it.
- Fix: `GraphicsState::host_blend()` in `crates/victory-gpu/src/pipeline/host.rs` turns blending off for integer targets (`VICTORY_INT_BLEND=1` restores the old behavior).
- Result (run67, live, hardware, measured): 0 refused mesh pipelines, about 8.8 fps in the cell scene. The background still shows no walls, so this fix alone is not enough. The remaining dropped draws are the ray-tracing passes (about 300-2,600 per frame).
- Next: non-ray-tracing fallback for the dropped passes; then play through the intro on the ~9 fps build.

## RT resolve pass fixes the missing walls (2026-11-25, run68, live, hardware)
- `VICTORY_RT_RESOLVE=1` (runs the `CS_RaytracingResolve` compute pass instead of dropping it, builder still off) brings the walls, doorframe and lighting back in the prologue cell scene and the yard. Walls drawn, only mild edge speckle on the doorframe. Compare run67: dark empty background, artifacts.
- Cost (measured): 5.8-7.7 fps (130-170 ms/frame) vs 8.8 fps in run67; preparing 92-149 ms, copies 43-88 ms / 100-190 MB per frame, 3,000-6,000 draws in busy frames.
- Next: make it part of the default preset, play through the intro, then cut the copy volume.
- run68 reached gameplay ("Move around / Move the camera" prompt) with walls and lighting drawn, steady 115-138 ms/frame (about 8-9 fps, measured) at about 4,660 draws/frame. Patch `series-s-overlay-fix.patch` regenerated with the integer-blend fix.
- Open: speckle along bright doorframe edges; copy volume about 90-100 MB/frame; 15 fps not reached.
- run69 (VICTORY_KEEP_SNAPSHOTS=1 on the run68 preset): about 6 fps, 163 ms/frame, 4,502 draws, and the scene shows heavier artifacts (smeared, stair-stepped fence and doorframe) than run68. Not faster; leave it off.

## Shader validation follow-up (2026-11-25)

- Offline verification recovered 236 of 439 previously failing shader programs. Four compute pipelines now both validate and compile on the Radeon RX 7800 XT. The changes cover exact masked double-precision lane counts, native double arithmetic, record-count queries for descriptor-only buffers, and separating incompatible interpolation modes in DXIL signature rows.
- The focused sweep still finds 203 failures. The largest groups are mesh output declarations above the DXIL size cap, signature serialization mismatches, and descriptors selected by control flow. Two of the five compute programs that needed double arithmetic now reach a separate lane-mask limitation.
- Run68 remains the gameplay baseline at about 8-9 FPS. The revised shaders need a gameplay visual check and a matched frame-time sample before making a performance comparison.
- Signature rows now retain metadata order within each register. Sampler branches merge static and heap handles before annotation. Texture-array LOD queries omit the array index. The targeted sweep validated 360 of 439 previously failing shaders, with 79 remaining. Of those, 71 exceed the DXIL mesh-output size limit.
- Run70 on the earlier shader candidate reached interactive gameplay on the Radeon. Eleven matched prologue summaries measured 108.3-126.2 ms/frame (7.9-9.2 FPS), with roughly 4,500-4,700 draws/frame and 94-103 MB of title-memory copies per frame. Walls and lighting appeared and bright-edge speckling remained. The log attributes the dropped gameplay draws to the disabled ray-tracing builder. All 2,441 previously used pipelines precompiled with zero refusals.

### AvatarManz texture review

- Reviewed the current upstream main at `85d5641` in a separate worktree. It keeps the old compatible texture or a neutral descriptor until submissions that predate a new pooled texture have completed, then checks the slot's current record before switching. The pool uses 64 KiB by default and waits for texture references and GPU use to finish before reusing a piece.
- 44 focused pool, descriptor and texture tests passed. The full GPU suite passed 377, failed one timestamp calibration test and skipped 34. The timestamp failure also reproduced by itself. Tests are in `.local/avatarmanz-latest/texture-tests.log`. The texture tests do not establish in-game visual parity or the 15 FPS target.

### Unknown pipeline creation methods (slots 0x1c8, 0x218): format decoded in part (run80, run81)
`device.rs` now dumps each (pointer, size) blob of the description on the first 3 calls per slot (opt-in whole-blob files with `VICTORY_BLOB_DUMP=<dir>`, samples in `logs\run81-blob-dump\blobs`). Offline findings:
- The description is a root signature pointer followed by (pointer, size) blobs. For a graphics pipeline made by 0x1c8 the blobs are: a 0x17c-0x184 byte state block, a 0xbc byte signature (carries POSITION/COLOR/TEXCOORD strings, the same layout as the ISG1/OSG1 parts of a shipped container), a 0xb0-0xd0 byte resource table (20-byte entries with resource types 0x41/0x42 and 0xffffffff upper bounds, the shape `native.rs` documents for the "used" table), and 0x140-0x240 byte code blocks that are real GFX10 code (s_waitcnt, image_sample, s_branch, v_interp-style moves). The 0x218 slot builds the same pieces in a smaller shape (0x1c and 0x14 byte register blocks plus 0x118-0x240 byte code blocks).
- So these are the same compiler pieces `native.rs` reads out of a container, handed over loose. A way forward is to synthesize a container (signature parts, a native program record with code, root layout and used-resource tables, and the stage data) from the blobs and send it through the existing translator and host pipeline creation. The open work is decoding the state block (0x184 bytes, starts 4005d808 00000004) into the stage tables (inputs, outputs, SPI/DB registers) that `native.rs` expects at record+0x54.
- Not implemented yet; the slots still return E_NOTIMPL, so about 2.5-3k draws per frame remain dropped.

State block (0x184 bytes) of slot 0x1c8, read offline: it is not the native.rs record. It looks like a raw console pipeline-state register image (values such as 0x07ff0000, 0x00cc0010, 0xe5040504 repeated per render target, 0x01540154 repeated 8 times, 0x19fc0130). Decoding needs register-to-D3D12 state mapping (blend, depth, formats, input/output tables) with no reference yet. Not implemented. The next evidence step is to match a recorded draw's known state against its blob words.

Checked: the repo holds no GFX10 context-register names (CB_*, DB_*, PA_*), so the 0x1c8 state image cannot be decoded against an in-repo reference. A decode needs either public RDNA register documentation (e.g. the Mesa/amdgpu sid.h register offsets) or paired capture of a draw with known state. Status: still not implemented.

Register reference fetched: Linux gc_10_3_0_offset.h in .local\regdocs (mesa on gitlab blocks scripted downloads). Context register offsets: CB_TARGET_MASK 0x8e, CB_SHADER_MASK 0x8f, SPI_SHADER_COL_FORMAT 0x1c5, SX_MRT0_BLEND_OPT 0x1d8 (x8), CB_BLEND0_CONTROL 0x1e0 (x8), DB_DEPTH_CONTROL 0x200, CB_COLOR_CONTROL 0x202, DB_SHADER_CONTROL 0x203, PA_SU_SC_MODE_CNTL 0x205. In the 0x1c8 state block the two 8-wide runs (0xe5040504 at +0xe0, 0x01540154 at +0x100) have the shape of per-render-target blend-option and blend-control registers, but the block is not in plain offset order, so the layout is still undecoded. Next: derive the layout by diffing blobs of pipelines whose blend/depth state differs (many are in the dump).

State block diff (3 graphics pipelines of slot 0x1c8): +0xd0..+0xec is CB_BLEND0..7_CONTROL (blend on = 0xe5040504 with enable bit 30 and separate-alpha bit 29, blend off = 0x80000000 DISABLE_ROP3). +0x100..+0x11c is SX_MRT0..7_BLEND_OPT (0x01540154 with blend, 0x06000600 without). +0x0fc differs with blend as well (0x33333333, possibly the per-MRT target mask). Other differing words (+0x00c, +0x028, +0x038..+0x07c, +0x0a0, +0x0b8, +0x128, +0x174) are stage/format dependent and still unmapped. Blend state is now decodable; depth, raster and the shader pieces are the remaining work. Only 3 samples so far: dump more with VICTORY_BLOB_DUMP across a longer run to confirm.

run82 (loading, dump up to 400 calls per slot, 103 blobs, 9 graphics state blocks from slot 0x1c8): the blend mapping holds. A second blend value (0xe5010508 with option word 0x01510177) appears together in +0xd0..+0xec and +0x100..+0x11c, so those two runs vary together with the blend factors. Also varying: +0x0f4 (3-5), +0x0f8 (0/1), +0x0fc (0 / 0x33333333), +0x128, +0x174 (0/1) and the +0x038..+0x084 stage words. Samples in logs\run82-blob-dump-big\blobs. The loading run ends before gameplay draws, so later samples need the dump on through gameplay.

run84 (every 8th call dumped, 4,332 blobs, 504 slot-0x1c8 state blocks, loading plus first gameplay seconds; samples in logs\run84-blob-dump-every8\blobs). Findings: the blocks come in 4 lengths (0x17c-0x190). No word in the block takes depth-control or cull-mode shaped values (no DB_DEPTH_CONTROL / PA_SU_SC_MODE_CNTL candidates), so depth and raster state are probably set at draw time by the title's own state commands and the block holds blend, export formats and stage resource counts only. Words +0x00c, +0x028, +0x05c, +0x084 move together over 4-6 values (probably output/MRT counts); +0x0f8/+0x0fc/+0x0cc/+0x128 move together in two groups (blend on/off: 281 vs 213 of 504). Next: build the translator input (container from the code blob plus signature plus resource table) and take blend from this block, before the remaining words are decoded.

run84 code blobs: 1,007 of 1,073 large slot-0x1c8 blobs contain s_endpgm (0xbf810000), so they are complete GFX10 programs the existing gfx10 decoder can read. The rest (66) have none in the dump and are other tables. The code blobs are separate from the stage record (inputs, outputs, SPI registers, root layout) that native.rs reads, so the translator's input still needs those tables rebuilt from the state, signature and resource blobs.

Resource blob (slot 0x1c8, e.g. 0xd0 bytes) decoded in outline: header u32 2, u32 size, u32 0x76 tag, u32 flags; then 24-byte records led by a kind dword. Kind 0x41 = root/table entries (words: kind, root slot/flags, dword offset, count or size, 0x804-style flags, 0xffffffff bound), kind 0x42 = resource ranges (e.g. 0x000c0001, 0x000d0201 type/space words with a count and 0xffffffff bound), kind 0x43 = the 16-byte shader hash (record closes the blob, first dword matches the low half of the hash of the code blob's first word pattern, not verified). This is the same information native.rs reads as root layout + used-resource tables, in a different packing, so a converter to those tables is feasible. Field-by-field meanings still need confirmation against native.rs sample records of a container whose pipeline we already run.

Kind-0x41 records over 505 resource blobs (1,005 records): word1 is 0x300 (437) or 0x302 (505), 0x002 (53) or 0x401 (10); word2 is a multiple of 8 (0, 8, 0x10, 0x18), so likely a user-data dword offset of 8-dword (image) or 4-dword descriptors; word3 is a small register index (0-5, 0xb, 0x1f4 on 53 records, probably an unbounded-array base); word4 is 0 or 4 (4 paired with 0x808, 0 with 0x804 in the same record, so word5 = 0x804/0x808 carries a size or space flag); 0x10904 on the 53 0x1f4 records. Converting these to native.rs RootParam/UsedResource entries is possible, but the type codes (0x300/0x302/0x401/0x002) need one pipeline with a known root signature to pin down.

Correction: the kind-0x43 record is not a per-shader hash. Over 505 resource blobs only 2 distinct 16-byte values occur, so it is a hash shared by many pipelines (most likely the root signature's hash, which fits the description's first word being a root signature object). The per-shader identity is not in the resource blob. shaders.rpf (412 MB) holds the shipped containers; matching blobs to them needs the code hash, which the blobs do not carry either, so the match has to be made by code bytes.

Code-byte match attempt: 193 distinct code-blob keys from run84, scanned shaders.rpf (magic 9FPR) for zlib-packed XBCH native blobs with a plain byte search and found no XBCH streams, so shaders.rpf is stored compressed or wrapped and needs the runtime's archive reader (library.rs / shader-info), not a raw scan. Next: extract native programs through the repo's own reader (a small test or a victory subcommand) and match by code bytes.

Note: state\cache\shaders holds 92,284 translated shaders but they are host output, with no native code to match the blob code against. Matching by code bytes needs the native program extraction (victory-shader native::native_programs over shaders.rpf entries) done in a throwaway test binary. Not done.

shaders.rpf holds zero DXBC, XBCH or XDAT byte strings: its entries are compressed, so offline matching of blob code against shipped containers is not possible. Containers exist only in the title's memory after it decompresses them. The remaining way to pin the type codes is at run time: when a pipeline is created through slot 0x1c8/0x218, record the title's most recent root signature creation (create root signature call with its blob) next to it, since the description's first word is a root signature object whose bytes the runtime has already seen.

run85: the runtime now dumps the root signature each slot-0x1c8/0x218 description points at (root.rs serialized_of, dumped as rootsig-<slot>-call<n>.bin next to the blobs when VICTORY_BLOB_DUMP is set; 847 dumped in logs\run85-rootsig\blobs). They are standard DXBC containers with an RTS0 chunk (version 2 = root signature 1.1, e.g. 2 parameters, flags 0x801), so the host's own deserializer reads them and the pipeline's root layout is known exactly. Pairing them with the resource blobs of the same call shows 0x41 records are root parameter bindings (word1 0x300/0x302 = table, 0x002 = constants/descriptor with 0x1f4 as the 500-dword unbounded constant array seen in the signature) and 0x42 records are descriptor ranges (word2 = type/space word like 0xd0201, 0xc0001, 0x20001). So the blob root layout can be derived from the root signature itself (no new decode needed). Remaining unknowns are the state block and the stage records (inputs/outputs, SPI registers).

The 0xbc-size blob of a 0x1c8 call (e.g. call 1) is a vertex input layout: a header (magic 0x76e7bc07, 0x1000, count 1, record size 0x1c) then name-string table records (POSITION, COLOR, TEXCOORD, each with a 0x1c-byte record carrying a semantic index, a format code (0x0c, 0x09 seen) and offsets 0x00/0x10). That is the vertex-stream element list the host pipeline needs as D3D12_INPUT_ELEMENT_DESC; format and offset codes still have to be mapped to DXGI formats from a few known vertex declarations. Together with the root signature (known), blend (known, from the state block) and the code blob (decodable), the unknowns left are the VS/PS stage record (input/output semantics) and rasterizer/depth state.

Crash check of runs 79-85 (hardware, loading plus gameplay up to about 3 minutes each): no panic, access violation, device-removed or error lines in any err.txt (the one match per log is PowerShell wrapping the first stderr line). No open crash to fix in these runs.

Signature blob census (504 of the run85 slot-0x1c8 calls): the blob with header word1 = 0x1000 is the shader's input signature (ISG1-like), not a vertex fetch description: its semantic names are only COLOR, POSITION and TEXCOORD, in varying orders and counts (COLOR+POSITION 197, COLOR+COLOR+POSITION 163, POSITION 36, POSITION+COLOR 34, ...). These are screen-space/UI-style or particle shaders (position, up to three colours, texcoord), which also explains why they are many and small. Entry records are 0x1c bytes (semantic index, mask, type at +0x0c/+0x18); name strings follow. Mapping to the stage record rows still needs the mask and type words decoded.

Signature blob layout (single-POSITION samples): +0x00 u32 varies per pipeline (looks like a hash of the shader), +0x04 u32 0x1000, +0x10 u32 0x1c (0x41 in one variant), +0x24 u32 0x00030100/0x00030114 (the second form on samples with other state), +0x38 u32 0x29 (0x70, 0x4b, 0x6d on larger blobs, so it grows with the entry count and is probably the byte count of the entries plus names), +0x3c u32 0x0c, +0x4c u32 entry count (1 for POSITION; 2-3 for COLOR sets), name string at +0x54 and a u32 0x1000 right after it. So the first word is a per-shader hash that could pair these blobs with code blobs of the same call; the entry fields (mask/type) are the same shape for POSITION across samples, which suggests there are very few distinct values to decode (position=4 float lanes at a fixed row).

Verified: the existing gfx10 decoder reads every loose code blob. New ignored test crates\victory-shader\tests\blob_decode.rs (run with VICTORY_BLOB_DIR=<dump dir> cargo test -p victory-shader --test blob_decode -- --ignored --nocapture): 1,127 of 1,127 blobs with s_endpgm decode, 0 failures. So the code itself needs no new decoder work; what is left to build is the stage record around it (inputs/outputs, SPI/DB registers, user-data layout from the root signature), then translate and make the host pipeline.

Implementation shape for slots 0x1c8/0x218 (from emit/mod.rs): 	ranslate builds a NativeProgram (stage, wave64, vgprs, layout: Vec<RootParam>, used: Vec<UsedResource>, io: StageIo, code) from a container, then runs spill::build -> structure -> resource::track -> io::raise -> emit(&Container, ...). A loose pipeline can skip the container parsing by constructing NativeProgram directly (code = the loose blob, layout/used from the root signature + 0x41/0x42 records, io from the signature blob + state block), but emit still takes a Container (signature parts), so a minimal synthetic container (ISG1/OSG1/DXIL stub) is also needed. StageIo for PS/VS (input rows, v_interp attribute, parameter slots, export patches) is the part with no loose source identified yet: the state block (0x184) is the only candidate and its words +0x00c..+0x084 (counts, SPI-like) are still unmapped.

Design decision for the StageIo gap: derive it from the loose code instead of from the state block. PS inputs: every v_interp/v_interp_p1/p2 ttr and channel in the code gives a parameter row and mask; PS outputs: each exp mrt<n> gives the render-target mask (formats come from the blend/format words of the state block, patched exports as in NativeProgram::patched_code need the export patch code of the console, which the loose code already carries inline); VS outputs: exp pos0 and exp param<n> give position and parameter slots; VS inputs: the fetches through the root layout. The semantic names (COLOR, POSITION, TEXCOORD) come from the signature blob and give the HLSL-side names. This keeps the state block to blend and formats only.

Design check on the 1,127 loose code blobs (blob_decode test now also counts instructions): 9,304 v_interp instructions; export targets MRT0 x502, POS0 (12) x504, PARAM0..4 (32-36) x494/431/325/218/36, one MRT1 (8). That is one VS and one PS per pipeline (about 504 pairs): each VS writes position plus up to 5 parameters and each PS writes MRT0, so StageIo can be derived from the code itself as planned. Next: write the code that builds NativeProgram/StageIo from a blob and run it through spill::build -> structure -> resource::track -> io::raise.

Prototype (ignored test loose_programs_reach_the_structurizer in crates\victory-shader\tests\blob_decode.rs): each loose code blob wrapped in a NativeProgram with no layout/stage data and run through spill::build and structure::structure: 503 PS and 504 VS blobs succeed at both wave32 and wave64, 0 failures. VS vs PS is told by exp pos0 in the code. So the early translator stages accept the console code as is; what is left before emit is the root layout (RootParam/UsedResource from the 0x41/0x42 records, paired by call with a code blob: description order is state, signature, code, resource, code, resource), the wave size (flags unmapped), and StageIo from the code.

run85 blobs through resource::track (ignored test loose_programs_reach_the_resource_tracker; pairing codes with resources of one call by address order; layout guessed from the 0x41 records: root = word1>>8 (0 constants, 1 descriptor, 3 table, 4 immediate sampler), table offset = word2, base register = word3, user_data = word5 low byte, 1 SGPR per parameter; NOTE the 0x41/0x42 records are 8 dwords, not 6). Result over 1,007 programs: 53 PS track fully, 505 PS stop at 'an address with two computed terms' or 'address in an SGPR that is no root parameter' (SGPR / user-data mapping or descriptor-array form still wrong for these), and all 503 VS stop at 'an address in an SGPR that is no root parameter' at 0x38-0x48 (the vertex fetch / first user data). The 0x41 field meanings (sgprs count, user_data, slot) therefore need correcting; the tracker is a good oracle for it since it fails at the first untraceable address.

Layout variants against the tracker (env LOOSE_SGPRS / LOOSE_USER in the ignored test): sgprs 1 or 2 with user_data = word5 low byte or word4: 53 PS ok, 0 VS ok; user_data = word5>>8: 63 PS ok, 0 VS ok. Re-reading one VS blob record by record: (kind 0x401, off 0, reg 0xe, 0, w5 0x20004), (0x300, off 0, reg 0xd, 0x80, 0x804), (0x300, off 0x10, reg 0, 4, 0x808); a PS blob: (0x300, off 8, reg 7, 0x40, 0x804), (0x302, off 0x18, reg 0, 0, 0x804). So word5 low byte (4 or 8) is most likely the descriptor size in dwords (4 sampler/CBV, 8 SRV), word3 the register, word4 a byte offset/size, and word5>>8 is some slot/flag value (8, 9). The VS code starts with three VOP3 instructions that embed the shader hash then s_branch +3, and reads s_load_dwordx4 ... s[8:9] at dword 16, so the VS reads its first table through the pair s8:9; the tracker rejects that pair for every layout tried, so the VS user-data base (probably s8 meaning the first table) still has no parameter. Next: derive the user-data dword of each parameter from the order of the root signature's parameters (rootsig-*.bin) instead of the blob words.

Tracker results with user_data = word5>>8 and sgprs = word5>>16 (0 -> 2): 63 PS ok; 3 VS stop at 0x38 ('an address from a root parameter that is no address'), the other 500 VS at 0x48-0x58 ('address in an SGPR that is no root parameter'): with user_data 8 the VS reaches one more load than before (0x40 -> 0x48), so s8:9 is read as a table now. For VS user data, HwStage::user_sgpr(Vs, i) = i, so the VS code reading s[8:9] confirms user_data 8 for that table (word5>>8), and ranges of one table share it (the two 0x300 records with offsets 0 and 0x10). The remaining VS failure is a later load through other SGPRs (likely the 0x401 record, root constants/descriptor at a user-data dword the layout does not give). The PS failure 'address with two computed terms' (505 of them at 0x3c-0x60) is a dynamic or array access, probably the 0x302 / 0x1f4 unbounded-array form (resource heap, SM 6.6) that needs RootType::ResourceHeap, not Table.

VS prologue read by hand (call 1, code dword index): 9 s_branch +3, 10 s_mov_b32 s9, 0 (high half of the table address zeroed, so the table pointer is the 32-bit s8 alone, as RootType::Table expects), 12 s_load_dwordx2 s[16:17], s[8:9], 0 (a 64-bit pointer stored at table entry 0), 14 s_load_dwordx4 s[24:27], s[8:9], 0x40 (a descriptor at table offset 0x40, the 0x80/0x40 word4 of the 0x300 records, so word4 = byte offset in the table), 16-19 s_mov building a buffer descriptor word (0x10400) for a buffer addressed by s[16:17]. The tracker then fails on the use of s[16:17], an address loaded from the table (the 0x401 record, reg 0xe, root constants/CBV pointer in a table slot). Root type variants (descriptor / resource heap / sampler heap) for the 0x401 and 0x302 records did not change the VS or PS counts.

VS tracked: 263 of 503 loose vertex programs now pass resource::track (was 0). Findings: (1) the rootsig dumps are authoritative for root constants: nearly every pipeline's root signature is [table, 2 root constants at b500 (RTS0 param type 1: 500, 0, 2)], and the VS treats those two dwords (s0:s1, user data 0-1) as a 64-bit buffer address, so they are a RootType::Descriptor at user data 0, 2 SGPRs, not constants; (2) the console cuts that address's high dword to 8 bits (s_bfe_u32 s11, s11, 0x80000) before s_load_dwordx4 s[8:11], s[10:11], null loads the vertex buffer descriptor, so resource.rs high_half now accepts that bfe (one change in crates\victory-shader\src\resource.rs, 214 lib tests pass, 2 ignored); (3) the table is at s8 (user data 8, one 32-bit address, high half zeroed by s_mov_b32 s9, 0), ranges at table byte offsets 0x20, 0x60 etc. Remaining VS failures: 'descriptor changed after loading' (~170: the descriptor registers are reused by later code) and ~7 others. PS failures (440) are still the 'two computed terms' form at 0x3c-0x60.

PS tracked: 497 of 503 loose pixel programs pass resource::track (was 63). The PS reads user dwords 8 (resource table, high half zeroed), 9 and 10 (two sampler indices, shifted left 4 and added as a dynamic term), and 11 (the sampler heap base address: s_mov_b32 s8, s11; s_mov_b32 s9, 0; s_load_dwordx4 s[40:43], s[8:9], index). Adding RootType::SamplerHeap at user data 11 (1 SGPR) to every PS layout resolves the 'two computed terms' failures: the loose pipelines use SM 6.6 style sampler heap indexing. Together with the VS result (263 of 503): 760 of 1,006 programs trace their resources with a layout assembled from the root signature and the 0x41 records. Remaining: 6 PS ('descriptor neither loaded nor built'), about 240 VS ('descriptor changed after loading', and a few earlier ones).

VS tracked: 496 of 503 (was 263), PS 497 of 503, so 993 of 1,006 loose programs trace their resources. The VS failures were the console's scalar patch of a loaded buffer descriptor: the record count times a stride taken from the descriptor (s_bfe_u32 on dword 1, s_max, s_mul_i32 on dword 2) and flag bits cleared and set (s_and_b32 / s_or_b32 with constants on dword 3). resource.rs classify now looks through those edits (new Tracer::unpatched, documented shortcut: the descriptor is taken as loaded, so the bound and stride the patch computes are the host buffer's own). 214 lib tests still pass. Left: 6 PS ('neither loaded nor built') and 7 VS (earlier address failures at 0x38-0x58, the 0x401 root parameter mapped as a sampler and the layouts without root constants).

io::raise on the loose programs: with StageIo derived from the code (VS: outputs = the exp param<n> targets as Generic rows n, no fetched inputs since the VS fetches its vertices with buffer_load from the root descriptor; PS: inputs = the _interp attributes with channel masks, SPI_PS_INPUT_ADDR 0x2 for persp-center barycentrics in v0/v1, no export patches), all 993 programs that tracked also pass io::raise (test stage_io_of in blob_decode.rs). Remaining before emit: a Container for emit (signature parts), and the host-side pipeline creation.

New module crates\victory-shader\src\emit\loose.rs (listed in sources(), dxbc::build no longer test-only): translates a loose program: stage from the code (exp pos), StageIo from v_interp / exp, ISG1/OSG1 synthesized from them, root layout from the resource record and root signature (layout()), then spill -> track -> raise -> emit. Two lower.rs changes: compressed colour exports are taken as f16 pairs (documented shortcut; render target format not read), and a vertex program's s2 (base vertex) reads zero because the host's SV_VertexID already holds the base. First emit results (ignored test loose_programs_translate): 54 PS translate to DXIL; remaining PS: 434 'an opaque lane mask' (probably needs wave64 or lane-mask handling), 9 'one input interpolated two ways', 6 tracker; VS: 263 'a structured buffer of unknown stride' (the descriptor patch shortcut hides the stride the console sets), 233 'a typed-format load by index' (tbuffer_load_format with idxen, the vertex fetch), 7 tracker. 214 lib tests pass.

loose.rs: 497 of 503 pixel programs now translate to DXIL (was 54). Changes: wave32 first then wave64 (the pixel programs are wave64: 301 at once); the pixel root layout gets the two root constants as Constants at user data 9 (the two sampler indices) with tables as one dword; a pixel program's prim-mask SGPR (read only to set M0) reads zero (lower.rs); signatures use one scalar TEXCOORD element per channel (index = attr*4 + channel, row = attr + 1) so a row read both flat and interpolated keeps one mode per element. Remaining: 6 PS tracker cases; vertex: 263 'a structured buffer of unknown stride' (descriptor-patch shortcut) and 233 'a typed-format load by index' (tbuffer_load_format idxen: the vertex fetch) plus 7 tracker cases. 214 lib tests pass.

VS emit: the vertex fetch now passes the stride stage: lower.rs gives a typed-format load by index its element start (index * stride + offset), used_kind finds the used entry of a root descriptor, and loose.rs supplies one (stride = end of the furthest idxen load, documented shortcut: packed vertex buffer, from the load sizes and BUF_FMT widths in the instruction text). That moves 496 VS to the next error, 'a descriptor outside the heaps': the buffer descriptor is loaded with s_load from the address in the two root constants (s0:s1, high dword cut to 8 bits). The two root constants are therefore the GPU address of a descriptor inside the title's CBV/SRV/UAV heap (heap base + index*descriptor size), not a buffer address. Next: translate that address as a heap-relative descriptor access (the 32-bit s0 as a RootType::ResourceHeap / Table style address with the high half known to be 0), so the existing heap path resolves it at draw time. 214 lib tests pass.

MILESTONE (offline): 993 of 1,006 loose programs from slots 0x1c8/0x218 (497 PS, 496 VS) translate to DXIL and pass the real DXC validator (ignored test loose_programs_translate now validates). The VS root constants are a RootType::Table of two dwords with the second dword (cut to 8 bits by s_bfe_u32) taken as zero (documented shortcut in resource.rs high_half; a heap lies below 4 GB), so the descriptor the vertex buffer comes from resolves through the existing heap path. 214 lib tests pass. Left: 6 PS and 7 VS tracker failures; then the host side (HostPipeline creation from two loose shaders plus the state block's blend and formats), wiring unknown_pipeline in device.rs to call loose::translate, and a live test. Nothing of this runs in the game yet, so the 2.5-3k dropped draws per frame are unchanged.

run86 (hardware, VICTORY_LOOSE=1, first live use of the loose pipelines): new crates\victory-gpu\src\pipeline\loose.rs makes the pipeline of a slot-0x1c8 call from its pieces (state block, signature, then program + resource record for the VS and the PS), translated through victory_shader::emit::loose and handed out through pipeline::hand_out; device.rs unknown_pipeline calls it first (off unless VICTORY_LOOSE=1; the state block's blend/format words are not read yet, the host takes state from the command list). Result: the game ran from the menu into the prison-window scene with no crash or device error, walls, glass, the inmate shirt lettering and the yard NPCs present, 4,640-4,800 draws per frame and about 264-272 dropped (the RT builder's), 5.7-8.1 fps while loading and 5.0 fps at the screenshot (a hardware run on a busy desktop; not a performance measurement). In this scene the dropped count is the same as before, so the loose pipelines do not change it; their effect shows in scenes with the 2.5-3k drops (the cutscene scenes). 458 loose-program translate failures were logged: the same ~13 tracker failures repeated per call (failures are not cached). Not done: the 0x218 slot, state block, a cutscene comparison, failure caching.

loose pipelines: failures are cached by program key (pipeline\loose.rs FAILED). victory-gpu lib tests: 133 passed, 2 ignored; victory-shader lib: 214 passed, 2 ignored. series-s-overlay-fix.patch regenerated (includes the new loose files) and passes a reverse apply check.

CORRECTION (run87, VICTORY_LOOSE=1, cutscene scene reached): every frame report in the run names the same drop reason for its 'dropped N/frame without a host pipeline': 'a stage of the ray-tracing structure builder, which is off' (all 10 reports; 2,816/frame at 3,647 drawn in the cutscene, matching the baseline's 2,622-3,100 in run79). So the 2.5-3k dropped per frame are the builder's compute dispatches, NOT draws lost to the unimplemented 0x1c8/0x218 methods; the earlier note calling them the largest geometry gap was wrong. The loose pipelines (about 1,000 UI/particle-style shaders: POSITION, COLOR, TEXCOORD) change neither the dropped count nor the drawn count (3,647 vs 3,658) in either scene, so they are not what makes walls, glass or clothing go missing, and they give no frame-rate change. Keep VICTORY_LOOSE off by default. Next: return to the real costs (copy volume, copy-back, preparation) and the intermittent missing geometry (depth-plane twin path).

Profile of the submitting thread (run 88/89, in-process sampler with dbghelp symbols; wpr needs elevation and is refused): 77% busy, of it let_go_idle (resource.rs, walks all ~19k textures at every one of ~30 submissions a frame) 37-55%, plus ReadProcessMemory/NtQueryVirtualMemory in copy_mapped/watch earlier in the run. The audio decoder thread (opus) is the second busiest, partly waiting. let_go_idle is now throttled to once per 250 ms (VICTORY_SWEEP_EVERY_MS, 0 = every submission). A/B on the diag build, same save, 200 s after Enter: throttle 250 ms median of the last 20 reports 8.7 fps (min 5, max 9.4); every submission median 8.7 fps (min 2.4, max 9.4). NO FPS change, so that thread's CPU is not what paces the frame. The no-diag build (run 89) also showed 8.8 fps, so the diag counters are not the limit either. One run (run90, no-diag, throttle on) ended abruptly after about 3 minutes with no panic text in the log (process gone, log cut mid-line); the two later diag runs with and without throttle stayed alive; cause unknown. Visual quality varies run to run (run91 showed a speckled, partly missing head; runs 86 and 89 showed clean walls, glass and lettering), so a screenshot is not evidence for or against a change.

Sweep: the 250 ms time throttle did not remove let_go_idle from the submitting thread's profile (still 36% in run 94), so it is replaced by a slice per call (resource.rs: 1,024 textures from a cursor in the sorted table, VICTORY_SWEEP_SLICE=0 for the whole table). Profile of run 95 (no-diag build): let_go_idle is gone; the submitting thread (about 78% busy) now spends its time in lock waits on the watch mapping (12%), NtQueryVirtualMemory (11%), ReadProcessMemory in copy_mapped/shadow update (about 20%) and blake3 (4%). FPS did not follow: diag build, 200 s after Enter, median of the last 20 reports 8.8 fps (min 4.2, max 9.4), prep 86-94 ms and copies 31-35 ms as before; one no-diag overlay reading 10.0 fps / 100 ms (single sample). So the frame is paced by something other than that thread's CPU. User preference: the frame from run 89 (no-diag build before any sweep change, loose pipelines off, 8.8 fps) looked best.

Ceiling test (run97, diag build, VICTORY_COPY_MODE=none: title memory copies skipped, picture is wrong by design): about 12 fps (82-86 ms/frame) with preparing 48-53 ms and copies 4.5-5.6 ms (was 8.8 fps, 88 ms prep, 33 ms copies). So making the 33 ms of copies free gains about 25 ms per frame and caps this path near 12 fps; 15 fps (66.7 ms) additionally needs about 16 ms more out of the rest of preparing (heap passes 14 ms, textures 7 ms, lock waits, memory queries) or the copy-back chain (about 60 ms on its own thread, 33 MB per frame).

## Session summary at pause (2026-11-26) - findings saved on request

State: hardware runs reach gameplay (prison-window scene) at about 8.4-9.4 fps (108-128 ms/frame); 15 fps is NOT reached. Goal paused by the user.

Tried in the last stretch, with results:
- run98 (diag build): large host-bound copies (>= 2 MiB) read on up to 4 threads (host.rs read_wide, VICTORY_COPY_THREADS, default 4). No gain: copies still 31-35 ms/frame, median of last 20 reports 8.4 fps (min 5, max 9.4). So the 33 ms is not dominated by long contiguous reads; it is many refreshes. read_wide is left in the tree; set VICTORY_COPY_THREADS=1 to turn it off, or remove it.

No change in drawn/dropped counts or fps; slot 0x218 not wired; state block not read.

Code state (all under .local\upstream-rorodev6\repo, uncommitted): resource.rs high_half bfe case, unpatched() descriptor look-through, sweep slice; lower.rs compressed colour exports as f16 pairs, prim-mask SGPR zero, vertex s2 zero, typed-format load by index; dxbc::build public; emit/loose.rs; pipeline/loose.rs; device.rs hook and blob dump (VICTORY_BLOB_DUMP); host.rs read_wide; root.rs serialized_of; tests\blob_decode.rs (ignored tests, need VICTORY_BLOB_DIR). Next candidates when resumed: (1) the per-frame refresh count rather than byte rate (about 585 refreshes/frame; big buffers refreshed 2-5x per frame), (2) the copy-back chain (about 60 ms on its own thread, 33 MB/frame), (3) the remaining preparing time (heap passes 14 ms, textures 7 ms, lock waits on the watch mapping 12%, memory queries 11%), (4) what the intermittent missing walls/glass/clothing depend on (depth-plane twin path, untested).

A recorded list retains the twin and views across submissions rather than referencing a shared ring slot another recording may overwrite. The upscaler still uses its original ring. Previously it wrote directly after a mapping check that the title could invalidate. The new regression test protects part of the destination before fallback, verifies the reachable page is cleared and the protected page retains its bytes, and passes. The final diagnostic build passed and the GPU suite passed 134 tests with two ignored. The earlier unexplained live crash has not been attributed to this edge case. Some expensive records show implausibly large raw arguments. This establishes that the budget is active, but does not establish which geometry those records affect. Visual and frame-time evidence: .local/upstream-rorodev6/logs/run109-stable-depth-copy-fallback.
- Saved the diagnostic executable in target-d/diag/victory.exe and the full working-tree overlay in series-s-overlay-fix.patch under .local/upstream-rorodev6. The overlay includes existing uncommitted work and passes clean-index forward and working-tree reverse application checks. All 134 GPU library tests and 214 shader library tests passed, with two ignored in each. The three compute shaders from the prior failure list also passed standalone DXIL validation. No further experiments were started after run109.

## DexNom graphics transfer and FPS priority

- Built the current DexNom RayTime source at 64bd58f5 in an isolated C:\Meraki worktree. The older local review was pinned to 2e01ab4. Used the verified local BCPack format table, the locked dependencies, and the existing local toolchain.
- Copied DexNom's pixel quad swizzle and DPP changes and explicit-gradient cubemap mip correction from 921d8d3 into the Meraki Series S shader translator. Kept the related pixel-quad regression test. This port is limited to the shader changes and does not take DexNom's ray-memory-directory ABI. The build succeeded and 215 shader tests passed (2 ignored). Fresh overlay passed both clean-index application and reverse application against the working source. The run109 executable and overlay were preserved locally for a control and rollback.
- Evidence and both checkpoints are under .local/dexnom-review. The RayTime 4K Quality hardware test uses its documented Series X RT profile, an isolated copy of the Meraki save, and the local signed FidelityFX provider.
- The documented RayTime Quality profile booted the legal build in Series X mode and accepted focused menu input. The test stopped while loading at 158 seconds with 1,155 MiB of Windows commit free and 1,350 MiB of RAM available. The log never exceeded 16 draws per frame during this launch, so there is no gameplay FPS or graphics result for the Quality profile on this host. The run and memory evidence are under .local/dexnom-review/logs/20261126-094901-quality.
- Kept both the DexNom source and executable and the Meraki shader changes at the user's request. Testing DexNom in Series S with its static-BVH mode and sparse texture uploads next. The Meraki run109 baseline and its overlay are still available.
- The original RayTime Series S/static-BVH arm stayed alive for 300 seconds with 11 menu draws per frame after focused Enter. The window showed only RayTime's diagnostics HUD. No gameplay FPS was measured, and the user confirmed the black screen.
- Ported Meraki's separate Series S display plane composition, title-upscaler fallback and renderable HDR target substitution into .local/dexnom-review/raytime-series-s. The original RayTime worktree and executable are preserved. The port uses RayTime's native list and barrier APIs and keeps the overlay resource alive until the presentation list is retired.
- The first Series S display run ended during the unrelated PC reboot. After restarting, the patched build rendered the account picker, which had been black on the original DexNom build. Windows denied the first attempt to focus the window, so the input helper exited before sending Enter. The runner now retries focus and sends input only to the confirmed game process. The display patch passed clean-index application and working-tree reversal checks.
- The focus-retry run advanced through the account picker and into world rendering on the Series S display build. The title called the new fallback upscaler with a 1280×720 source and a 1920×1080 target. The first world frames took several seconds while pipelines compiled. At the end of the 300-second observation the title was still responding, but the last five reports ranged from 0.6 to 2.3 FPS across changing scenes. The runner stopped its process at the observation deadline. The old helper recorded no late PNG while the window was unavailable to the capture. The run did not verify gameplay image quality. Updated the capture helper for smaller windows and added explicit capture logs. Evidence is in .local/dexnom-review/logs/20261126-105804-series-s-display.
- DexNom Series S with the display patch and ray tracing disabled reached the interactive prison room and completed the observation window. The user approved the textures. Captures show face, hair, clothing, patterned glass and room geometry. In the last eight reports with at least 4,200 draws and zero pending pipelines, the observed median was 4.95 FPS (203.5 ms/frame). The report windows span camera movement. Median preparation was 126.0 ms, including 68.3 ms of copies, and median copy-back was 98.85 ms. These stages overlap. Baseline evidence is in .local/dexnom-review/logs/20261126-110407-series-s-display-no-rt.
- Kept the no-ray visual preset as the control for FPS work. The next arm adds only DexNom's in-flight GPU readback capture. No graphics settings changed between these arms.
- The in-flight readback run reached the prison window with the accepted textures and completed the observation window. Its last eight reports with at least 4,200 draws and no pipelines being made measured 7.05 FPS median (142.05 ms/frame) and 42.75 ms median copy-back. The baseline median was 4.95 FPS and 98.85 ms copy-back. Camera angles and draw counts differed between the runs, so the FPS difference is observational. The final readback samples show about 8�11 MiB published per frame. Evidence is in .local/dexnom-review/logs/20261126-110950-series-s-display-no-rt-readbacks.
- Added an opt-in CPU experiment in the DexNom Series S source that skips Windows write-watch calls when the first block of a range is already a protected mapped view. Other ranges follow the original system-call path, including ranges the runtime last saw as uncommitted. The existing guard and journal paths continue to track writes. Build, focused tests and a live comparison are pending.

- Offline 20 raw FPS work is on `perf/foxhound-20fps-offline` from the released runtime. Replaced repeated linear write-watch coverage checks with a sorted index that preserves the original single-range containment rule. The refresh path now skips per-flush lookahead preparation when it already has a submission-wide answer, uses one lock for page and partial-range coverage, and reads the experimental chunk setting once. A sparse descriptor scan went from 0.88 to 0.82 ms in the synthetic sample, so it was left out. No game process was launched. The 50 ms frame budget still requires gameplay verification.
- Offline verification passed 51 write-watch and 5 refresh tests. On the same synthetic 12,000 queries, indexing reduced search time from 1.08 ms to 0.10 ms across 256 ranges and from 3.93 ms to 0.13 ms across 1,024 ranges. These timings isolate the lookup. They do not measure total frame time.
- Diagnostics release build and non-diagnostics release check passed. The candidate executable is under the performance worktree target directory. The local launcher runtime was restored from the released build after compiling through the shared Cargo cache.
